Then they crouch and lean back (27-31) and LEAP (32-40, 52 px up) turning over in the air bristles-first (a half turn, faster and faster), and the bristles hit the floor under them on frame 41, upside down, jammed 12 px in as their feet are (the design's "slam the brush into the floor": the body IS the brush, so it is a dive, not a swing). THE FIRST SLAM (41) sends out the BLUE WAVE along the floor both ways (a rolling crest with a curling lip, foam and a lighter swell, in code): 420 px at 14 a frame, 50 tall, 4% (8% shown with FS_DMG_MULT), a small push (knock 4, up 3), and slow for 120 frames; a blue splat spreads where they landed. They stay planted shaking (42-46), heave up 16 px (47-52) and slam again (53-55). THE SECOND SLAM (55) is the RED WAVE, TODAY'S BLAST: the old row's own tick (the same fsLane object, FSAR_PB_LANE, with its numbers untouched: 600 px at 20 a frame, 70 tall, 12%, knock 18 and up 12, burn 160) called 41 frames later; only its drawing is new (a red crest with flame tongues, in place of orange discs). They then flip back up (63-74, 30 px high) and land on their feet, standing as the waves run out; the row ends on frame 125 (the old row's 84 + 41). No knock-back (the waves leave from their feet). THE CATCH lands on row frame 41, FS_WIND frames before the red wave, as the old catch landed FS_WIND frames before the old blast (the row lays its own: the framework's catch at the start finds no one, and the CPU still asks the real box and its rule, so it fires exactly as before), so nobody is held through the whole entrance and a foe is held no longer before the red wave than before the old blast: the old lane's box (616 either side, 70 tall). The blue wave has its own hit set, so the red one still hits whoever the blue one did; a foe the catch holds takes the blue wave's 4% and the slow in the hold, its stun is cleared at once (a held foe's stun runs down anyway; so the red hit is not a link of a chain, as on the old row, and the let-go is the old one), and the row lets it go a beat after the red hit with the red hit's launch (the old `lockEnd:'one'`, done by the row: the blue hit would have ended a real 'one'). The red wave's launch is worked out from the foe's % as it stands then, with the blue wave's 4% in, as any later hit in a match is. In the air they hang at the height they fired at (placed, not thrown: the step's gravity would sink them) until the red slam, then fall as the old row's did after its wind. Their swing timeline is cut at the red slam (their body is the row's own there). A guest draws it all from fsT, fsWind, fsAx/fsAy, their facing and place, and a held foe's wire flag fsLk: no new wire field. Helpers and the row are in the `FSA lane r` block (fsaR_pb*, FSAR_PB*; the row is made there because it needs the block's constants: the old row's place holds a pointer); their body is one line in FIGHTER_ANIM.Paintbrush.body (the picture) and one in deform. Changed from the design and why: the design's three waves (blue, yellow, red) became the owner's two (blue, red; no yellow wave); the design's one slam of about 34 frames became two slams 14 frames apart (the blue one at 41, the red one at 55: the old blast), with a crouch and a leap before the first, 28 frames from the last face to the red wave; the face ramp is the entrance and not the waves; the catch is laid late (frame 41) instead of at the start; the blue wave adds 4% (8% shown) to what a caught foe takes (4 + 12 row-%, was 12) and the slow, which the redesign needs; the red wave is drawn red (was orange) because rage-3 is the red of their Mood-Coloured Brush Stroke. Checked in jsdom: the frames as played (both facings, ground and 100 px up: catch on 41, a foe 120 / 215 / 400 ahead hit by the blue wave on 46 / 53 / 66 and by the red on 59 / 63 / 73, which are the old row's 18 / 22 / 32 + 41, let go on 62 / 66 / 76, 124 row frames; in the air they stay at their height through frame 54); THE BLAST ON THE OLD BUILD (smash-bal) AND ON THIS ONE in 17 scenes (1, 2 and 3 foes, either facing, a foe behind, at their feet, past both waves' reach, 0-120%, in the air with foes at their height pinned, and one in the air with the foe on the ground): the red hit (damage 12, knock 18 and -12, 24 shown, burn 160), the hit set (who takes it, or nothing: 24 of 24 foes), the let-go vector, the let-go stun (23) and burn, and the held time before the red wave (21 frames for a foe 120 ahead, as before) are identical, 41 frames later; the old build was run with the foe at +4% where the blue wave hits it (its launch is worked out from that), and the whole flight after the let-go is the same path for the length of it (50 to 110 frames) when the blue slow is switched off in the harness; with the slow on the flights match until the stun ends (23 frames) and then the slowed foe is slowed, which is the blue wave's own effect. (The old build's foes end higher because ii-dlc's FS FLOOR tops them up: this branch does not have it yet; it notes foes by the Final Smash's hits, the blue ones too.) The holder KO'd at 13 moments (faces, crouch, leap, planted, heave, red slam, flip, standing), a held foe KO'd at 5, two CPUs attacking them through the whole row at three distances, a CPU beside them, nobody in reach, a second Final Smash right after: clean ends, one stock lost at most, no stuck pose, prop, lock or double stock; the row's draw, their body's deform and key run clean for every frame in 4 scenes (no bad number, no text drawn); the CPU's cue (ai, the catch box it asks for, wind 41, dur 125) is the old row's; the in-process online pair (both facings, a foe close, a foe behind, a foe at 480) agrees on both screens: the same wire fields, the same drawing calls to within 0.4 px, the same picture; three CPU free-for-alls (Goiky Field, Yoyleland, the Sky Tower) clean with them firing 6, 9 and 6 times; boot and syntax clean, CRLF intact. - **FSA lane r, 2026-11-02, batch 7: Dough's Final Smash (Shameless Knockoff) is redesigned by the owner ("BORING"; "he puts the purgatory mansion 'up for rent', then he fills it with 'wordplay', and launches them out"; canon: in s2 Bow asks Dough if he is putting this place up for rent, in s4 his key is falsely in a room with snares, mares, hares, dares and nails) and gets an entrance and a knock-back:** LETTERS ARE ALLOWED HERE (the owner, 2026-11-02: "letters are allowed occasionally"; the no-words rule only stops move-name popups): the only text the game draws is the sign's FOR RENT and each kind's word for 24 frames as its hit lands (SNARES, MARES, HARES, DARES, NAILS), grown in over 12; checked string by string. ENTRANCE (the row's wind, 14 -> 40 frames): he rises slowly like dough, taller and wider (0-13), loses his nerve and sags back with a sweat drop (14-21), rises again higher (22-35) and slams down (36-39), flour puffing off his sides; a pale flour-coloured copy of his traced outline trails him by 8 frames the whole way (the design's "always a beat behind himself"; drawn from his hurt outline put through his body of 8 frames earlier; it comes back for the bounce). THE MANSION is the Purgatory Mansion card's gradient art drawn in code (its dark #282626 gabled house with a tower and three lit windows, the pale teal sky and dull green ground): it rises out of the ground beside him over 0-11 (160 px from his middle on the side of the nearest foe, 208 wide, 128 tall to the room's ceiling, 250 with its roofs; on what he stands on, clear of solid slabs and the stage edge, else on the other side), the FOR RENT sign (a white board with a house and a key on it, in code) drops onto its tower and swings (12-23). THE CATCH lands on row frame 26 (FS_WIND frames before the first kind, as the old catch landed FS_WIND frames before the old blast: the row lays its own, the framework's at the start finds no one, and the CPU still asks the old circle and rule): every foe in the old 220 px circle is held, the front wall clears (26-35) and they are drawn into the room over 10 frames (a little hop each, spread along its floor, nearest first; one whose place would be inside a solid slab stays where it is; nobody is moved on Moon Caves), his key hangs in the middle of it. THE FIVE KINDS, one a beat of 12 frames (40, 52, 64, 76, 88), each a hit on every held foe (2.5% = 5% shown, knock 4, up 2: 12.5% in all) and its own picture, which stays in the room: SNARES (rope loops snap shut round them), MARES (horse heads burst out of the walls, toss and stay peeking), HARES (five rabbits dart back and forth along the floor), DARES (a glove slaps across their faces, drops, bounces and lies there), NAILS (nails bristle out of floor, walls and ceiling); the key rattles at each and the held foes jolt (drawn only, from the wire flag fsLk). THE DOOR BURSTS on 104 after a crouch (96-103): the old first ring to the number (220 px round where he stood, 18%, knock 19, up 14) on every foe in it and directly on every foe the room holds (the room is not where the ring is centred), the door, planks and the key fly out the far side; the foes it held are let go three frames on (107) with that launch (the let-go is one blow, the biggest); the old second ring (320 px, 12%, knock 15) on 124 for whoever the first missed; the row ends on 166 (the old 62 frames after the blast). KNOCK-BACK: he springs up about 30 px (drawn only) and lands 30 px behind where he stood (placed along the floor over 16 frames, never thrown; cut to the longest of 30, 24, 18 ... for which every spot on the way and a body's width either side is over a surface and clear of a solid slab: 0 on Moon Caves), then a smaller 12 px bounce (drawn only) as the late second ring goes. A guest draws it all from fsT, fsWind, fsAx/fsAy, fsBx/fsBy (the mansion's middle and floor), his facing and place and a held foe's fsLk: no new wire field. Helpers and the row are in the `FSA lane r` block (fsaR_dg*, FSAR_DG*); the row's own place holds the row; his body is one line in FIGHTER_ANIM.Dough.deform and a held foe's jolt one line in drawSpriteBody. Changed from the design and why: the old design's slam-and-two-rings blast and its "Bow's chairs" redesign are replaced by the owner's (the chair helper is not on this branch and is not needed: the room uses the framework's own catch and lock, as Loser's chamber does); the entrance is the design's (rise, sag, rise, the late copy) but its second rise ends in a slam beside the mansion and not in the blast, and the blast comes 64 frames later; the knock-back is the design's (30 px back, 30 px up, a smaller bounce at the second ring) but off the door's burst; the held foe takes 12.5% more than before (30.5 in all, was 18: the owner's "damage split near today's 18% + 12%"), the ring itself and the second ring are the old numbers; every held foe is thrown out of the one door, so a foe who stood on the other side of him from the mansion is thrown the other way (the old ring threw each away from him); the foes the catch holds are held 81 frames (was 17), the five kinds' time, by design. Checked in jsdom: the frames as played (both facings, ground and 100 px up: catch 26, kinds 40/52/64/76/88 on both held foes, door 104, let-go 107, a third foe 280 away takes only the second ring on 124, knock-back 30 px); THE BLAST ON THE OLD BUILD (smash-bal, read only) AND ON THIS ONE in 15 scenes (22 foes: 1, 2 and 3 foes, either facing, a foe behind, at his feet, just outside and past both rings, 0/40/80/120%, in the air with foes at his height pinned and one on the ground): the ring hit (damage 18, knock 19, up 14, 36 shown, stun 30) and the second ring (12, 15, up 14, 24 shown, stun 24) are identical on all 22 foes, and who takes which is the same set, shifted 90 frames (104 vs 14); the let-go (frame 107 vs 17, the vector, the stun 29) is identical for the 18 held foes against the old build with the foe started 12.5% higher (the row's own damage of the five kinds, which counts toward a launch as it does for any later hit in a match: at the same start % the old blast launches 1.23x shorter, 14.3 against 17.6 on a 0% foe), mirrored for the one foe who stood on the far side of him; their flights are the same displacement for the first 40 frames except the four that fly out of the stage within 8-36 frames (the high-% foes and the mirrored one: they start from the room, not from where they stood). The holder KO'd at 16 moments (each rise, the sag, the slam, each kind, the crouch, the door, the bounces, standing), a held foe KO'd at 5, two CPUs attacking him through the whole row at three distances, a CPU beside him, nobody in reach (he still springs back 30 px), a second Final Smash right after: 59 checks, clean ends, one stock lost at most, no stuck pose, prop, lock or double stock; the row's draw and his body's deform run clean for every frame in 4 scenes (no bad number; the six strings and no other text); the CPU's cue (ai, the catch circle it asks for, reach 220) is the old row's (wind 40, dur 166); 100 random scenes on Goiky Field, Yoyleland and the Sky Towers (on the ground or up to 160 px in the air, at ledges half the time, either facing): he always ends alive over a surface with his stock, never past a blast line at any frame, the knock-back never over 30 px (cut short at 16 of them; at the ends of the surface he stands on since 2026-11-03: before, only the floor's ends were, and "never past a ledge" did not hold for a platform's); the in-process online pair (both facings, a foe close, a foe behind, a foe at 480) agrees on both screens: the same wire fields, the same drawing calls to within 0.5 px, the same words (the check found that a guest's copy of him has no hurt outline, so the late copy was missing on the guest: it is now looked up by name, as hit art does); three CPU free-for-alls (Goiky Field, Yoyleland, the Sky Towers) clean with him firing 6, 9 and 6 times and holding foes; boot and syntax clean, CRLF intact; the diff is four hunks (the helper block, his row, one line in his body, one line for a held foe). FIX PASS 2026-11-03 (the checker's should-fix): THE KNOCK-BACK WAS NOT CLAMPED AT THE LEDGE OF THE SURFACE HE STANDS ON (fsaR_dgSlide asked landable, true over ANY surface below, so near a platform's end he was slid off it and fell to the floor or a lower platform: Stone Pillars y 461 to 606, Sky Towers y 1330 to 1550). Measured on six stages (Stone Pillars, Grand Plains, Sky Towers, Yoyleland, Forest, Incin), standing on a thin platform 4-28 px from an end with the mansion on the far side: BEFORE 92 of 120 trials ended hung past the end; NOW 0 of 120 (grounded on the platform; the knock-back is 0-12 px near an end and the full 30 px away from it; 30 at most). fsaR_dgSlide shares fsaR_sameTop with Teddy Bear's slide (each spot, and 16 px either side, over the surface he stands on within 10 px) when he starts on a surface, as fsaR_dgSpot's fits() already did for the mansion; an airborne start is as it was. Main-floor knock-backs are unchanged (12 trials on Goiky's floor: rows identical before and after). Blast unchanged: in all 15 blast scenes the new build is identical to the build before this pass (every blow, let-go, flight path and final state), and against live main the rings are the old ones (the same 22 foes, 0 different); the let-go launch differences the compare script lists are the held foe's 12.5% from the five kinds, by design. Checks this pass: the interruption/draw script (all pass, words FOR RENT / SNARES / MARES / HARES / DARES / NAILS only), the in-process online pair (all cases pass), three CPU free-for-alls with Balloony and Teddy Bear clean (he fired 3-5 times each), boot, syntax, CRLF. NOT CHANGED (not in this pass's items): Yellow Face's and Loser's knock-backs (fsaR_yfSlide, fsaR_loseSlide) use the same landable-only test and very likely have the same platform-end flaw.- **2026-10-30, assist batch A2 (build plan step 12; branch `g-ev-a2`):** 9-Ball, Battery, Clapboard, Conch Shell, Rubber Spatula, Scissors and Snare Drum join the bag (ASSIST BATCH A2, `ASSIST_ROSTER.push` and `AS_ACT.stay/wire/cut/horn/swat/snip/drum`), each act as event-fighters-phase1.md section 4 writes it with the review's toned-down numbers (damage x1.5 as every trophy): 9-Ball hops in between the summoner and the nearest foe and stays put for the whole stay, a bumper (5%, light knockback, once a foe a second, never moves); Battery walks out toward the foe, clips on (36 f) and a wire to the summoner grows in (12 f) and is live 3 s (5%, a 6-frame flinch, 45 f before the same foe again), snapping past 380 px; Clapboard's clap (32 f lift) freezes grounded foes within 240 px for 40 f, the first hit ending it at half the launch (`f._cutT`, read in applyHit; its own clapperboard icon), twice; Conch Shell puffs up (40 f) and blows a slow ring (4 px/f for 480 px; 4%), three; Rubber Spatula flinches (30 f), hops to the spot and swats down 110 px wide (8%: a grounded foe 30 f stunned with a small pop, an airborne one popped up and sideways, never a spike), three; Scissors spread (30 f), lunge 100 px and snap (9%, bleed), or cut a summoner who is held, rooted (staples, cuffs, roots) or swallowed within 200 px free, three; Snare Drum plants within 220 px (the design's "within 260"), 4 + 6 beats each sending floor ripples both ways (1%, a 6-frame flinch), then the crash 260 px wide (10%); jumping dodges, once. Everything an act leaves grows in over 12 frames, nothing is drawn ahead of a hit, no name tag over these seven (the other assists' tags go in batch A1, question 22). Online: the pose carries what they put out (`P.a2`), the CUT! clock rides NET_FIGHTER_FLAGS (`_cutT`); no new relay message. Art: the seven owner-approved wiki renders, cut to 200 px here (CREDITS.md, sprite-manifest.json); 9-Ball never mirrored (`still`). Pronouns: 9-Ball, Battery and Scissors stay they/them (question 101; the wiki answered 402). The bag: 24 rows, 23 since Eraser left at the launch; it stays at 17 or more as kits 2-6 add their names (18 after Cloudy). **For the owner:** batch A1 is not built yet, so Selfie Stick, Toilet and MePad are still in the bag and the older assists still wear name tags; no fighter can be caged yet, so Scissors has no cage to cut; seen, not changed: a trophy's body stands on groundY()'s line, which on the Teams arena is 32 px above the floor fighters stand on (every assist hovers there), so Snare Drum's ripples ride the surface under her instead. Checks (jsdom and the in-process pair, no browser, no relay): syntax, boot; each act's numbers, phases and leaving; Teams arena; Boss Rush (every act but Clapboard hits the boss's side; a boss is never frozen); CPU FFA, Teams and Boss Rush with all seven summoned, no errors; the pair with each trophy on each side, the guest's phases, what they put out and the %/statuses the host's. - **2026-11-02, FSA lane a, batch 1 (Goo, Lifering, Bonesaw, Spikey, Candle, Cammy, Cheesy, Fan): every Final Smash gets an in-character ENTRANCE before its blast (the owner: "every Final Smash gets an in-character ENTRANCE ANIMATION before its blast comes out exactly as it does now"; Pencil's hop into the van is the model):** fsaA_wrap (marked block "FSA lane a" ahead of Goo's row) puts E.W frames in front of each row -- Goo 32, Lifering 28, Bonesaw 36, Spikey 40, Candle 34, Cammy 24 (+6 of its own delay: the flash is on frame 30), Cheesy 36, Fan 34 -- and runs the row's own frames E.W - FS_WIND later, so every blast number is the old one frame for frame (48 scenes old vs new, foes' every field and every shot identical from the blast on). The CATCH still lands FS_WIND + 1 frames before the blast (a foe is held no longer than before; at fire the row hands the framework an area that holds nobody and lays the real one in tick, so no framework line changed); in the air the holder hangs where it was fired through the entrance. Drawn from wire fields only: row.draw (the props), FIGHTER_ANIM.deform (the body), a forced picture of the pose set (fsaA_pose: Goo engulf-1..4, Lifering whistle/dive, Candle chime, Cammy squint/flash); the generic startup's lean is off through it (the swing is re-armed as 'spit' and each member's FIGHTER_ANIM.atkDrive is 0 then). No words, no dice. SPIKEY's release is the one new motion: a scripted push up about 30 px and back about 40 (away from his facing, clamped by fsaA_room: the other way or none where the surface under him ends, a solid block is in the way or the stage's side line is near; none on the Moon) and he lands bald (_baldUntil +150, on the wire; his Can't Hold It In special waits for the spikes as its own rule says). Design changes: the poses are forced by key rather than a 'kind:fsgather' row (that clock is the swing's 18 ticks, not the entrance's); Cammy's wind is 24 and the 6-frame delay of her marker follows it. Frames, props and checks: reports/moves/fsa-a-1-sheet.png. **2026-11-03, second round (the checkers' findings on Spikey, Bonesaw and Fan; the helper block gained fsaA_floorOf, fsaA_rest and fsaA_hotHalf, fsaA_low takes a slack and fsaA_wrap takes E.late):** SPIKEY's release can no longer carry him off an edge by itself. The push is spread over the hop's own airtime (twice the lift-off speed over this stage's gravity: 20 frames on a normal floor, 24 on Yoyleland), so he travels exactly the room fsaA_room gave him (it stops 8 px short of the last good spot) instead of 3.6% more, and he is stopped dead (vx 0) the moment he lands instead of sliding on. His hop now uses the stage's gravity too (the lift-off speed was worked out for 0.62, and on Yoyleland's low gravity he went up 38 px, now 25 against 24 on Goiky). Measured on the same scenes before and after (both facings, 34 to 48 px inside each end of the Yoyleland floor and platforms, Goiky's platform, the ends of Grand Plains' floor, and the checker's own Yoyleland x=902 and Grand Plains x=3110 scenes): nobody left what he stood on, before or after; the smallest room left to an edge was 6.6 px at a floor end, 6.2 px at Goiky's platform end and 2.6 px at the Grand Plains platform end before, and is 8.0 px, 10.0 px and 6.0 px after (vanTopsAt gives a platform end 2 px of play). The three falls the checkers found did not reproduce on the committed build, whose batch 3 fix already stopped him 8 px short of the last good spot; what was left was the overshoot, the slide and that 2 px of play. The bald look after the release is RENDER-ONLY now (_fsBaldUntil, on the wire; _baldUntil is left to his special), so Can't Hold It In is not refused for 2.5 s after every Final Smash (measured: refused with 95 frames of cooldown before, taken as on main after). His red half glowing hotter is the red crescent of his own picture now, traced from it (FSA_SPIKEY_RED, 32 points of the 183 x 200 render, 3 px in from the rim) and laid over again through drawSpriteBody by his FIGHTER_ANIM over hook (fsaA_hotHalf: a copy of the picture that is orange-red only inside the crescent), so it sits on the painted split and follows his swell and tremble; before it was a world-space half-disc that began 27 px left of the split, cut a straight line through his face and poked above the ball over the top spike. Not drawn on a skin or a costume (about half the CPU Spikeys wear a skin). The frame layer's held 'spit' frame is off through the entrance for Spikey, Bonesaw, Cheesy and Fan (FRAME_SRC pose = fsaA_framePose, as batches 5 and 6 did): Spikey's body was 1.072 x 1.024 on top of the designed 1.2 (about 1.29) with the cheeks bulging, and is 1 x 1 now, so the designed numbers are exact (the face is the frame's, as before). BONESAW's rust flecks and FAN's paper flecks come down onto the floor line (fsaA_floorOf: the surface at or below the feet), lie there still at the angle they landed and go on fading, and are never drawn below it: the lowest drawn edge with alpha above 0.1 was 40.6 px (Bonesaw) and 42.7 px (Fan) under the line before (72.7 and 56.2 at the most), and is at least 0.9 px above it after, on the floor, on a platform and in the air. The blast against live main in the same 18 scenes: identical frame for frame.
- **2026-11-02, FSA lane a, batch 2 (Soap, Tissues, Yin-Yang, Starfruit, Blueberry, Cherries, Woody, Toothpaste): the owner's approved entrance designs, built on batch 1's fsaA_wrap (the helper block gained E.kind, fsaA_xf, fsaA_tab and fsaA_slide):** Soap 36 frames (three pumps at 3, 11, 19 each 8% shorter, a pink dollop arcing onto a shoulder each time, a lather climbing her to the eyes, the shiver and the cloth coming out at 24-36; the foam stays on her through the scrub), Tissues 38 + stream (three sniffs at 2, 12, 22 with dot flecks sucked in, one tissue rising on the second, the sneeze picture from 32, the tissue blown off at 33; he skids back 60 px over the stream), Yin-Yang 34 (Yang's picture from 4, a disc hoisted 4-16 and spun faster each beat, hurled down in front of them 28-34, bouncing into the two rollers), Starfruit 40 (the spotlight dropping on him over 12 frames, the lean back and breath 8-28, three notes out of the mic at 18, 25, 32, the belt leaning in from 34), Blueberry 36 (the sigh and shrug, three claps at 8, 18, 28 each with a white ring, the arm lifting 29-36 and dropping as the berry starts), Cherries 34 + skid (the olive-oil bottle growing in 2-14, tipped 14-24, the pour and a puddle no wider than the pair; they skid back 40 px from the blast frame), Woody 36 (freeze, tripled tremble, the shared gather's raised arms, three sweat drops, a ring of eight splinters jolted off one every 3 frames, a squash and stretch, the ring streaming into the lane), Toothpaste 34 (two finger-pads squeezing up her in three pulses, the cap popping at 22 and a swirl of paste; she is pushed back 30 px evenly while the 14 blobs go). Every blast number is the old one frame for frame (48 scenes old vs new, with the knock-backs off; the same 48 against live main): the catch lands FS_WIND + 1 frames before the blast, no framework line changed. The knock-backs are scripted slides (fsaA_slide: the room worked out at the start by fsaA_room, so never past the surface they stand on; none on the Moon; no launch, nothing the foes can hit): measured 58 / 29 / 36 px on the floor (60 / 30 / 40 designed, the step's friction allowed for), 6-8 px at a ledge. A shot leaves from a holder who has moved, so with the slides on a far shot can land a few frames later or fall short by the slide; every shot's damage and launch is unchanged. Nothing new on the wire (every prop is drawn from fsT, fsWind, fsAx/fsAy, facing and position). Checks: both facings, ground and air; KOs at five moments of each; the in-process online pair draws the same on both screens (0.4 px); three CPU free-for-alls clean; sheet in reports/moves/fsa-a-2-sheet.png. **2026-11-03, second round (the checkers' findings on Yin-Yang, Tissues and Toothpaste):** YIN-YANG's 'swap' sparkle (frames 4-8) is gone: the design asks for the Yang picture, a glare and a lean, and it popped in and out over 4 frames, not 12 (drawing calls at frames 4-7: 41 before, 28 after, the disc alone). TISSUES and TOOTHPASTE: with the slides on, a caught foe was held longer than on main (Tissues 72 frames against 67, Toothpaste 83 against 81, same scene). The shots leave from the sliding mouth, as designed, so the last hit lands later and the rule that lets go a beat after the last hit waits for it; pulling the rows' numeric lockEnd earlier does nothing (tried: the let-go is bound by the last hit, not by lockEnd), so the catch is laid later instead (fsaA_wrap's E.late: 5 frames for Tissues, 3 for Toothpaste, the shots and everything from the blast frame on untouched). Foe held for Tissues 67 / 78 / 64 frames (main 67 / 78 / 64) and Toothpaste 80 / 92 / 78 (main 81 / 92 / 78) in three scenes each; the catch now lands 10 and 12 frames before the blast instead of 15. With the slides off (TISSUES_FS.slide and TOOTHPASTE_FS.slide set to 0) every foe, shot and hit of all 12 comparison scenes equals main frame for frame, the lock flag included; with them on, the shots still leave from the sliding mouth and a far shot can land later or fall short by the slide, as the row says (Toothpaste, a foe 300 px away: 64% on main, none now).
- **2026-11-02, FSA lane a, batch 3 (David, Firey Jr., Fern, Ruler, Sidewalky, Roboty, Tree, Cake): the owner's approved entrance designs, built on fsaA_wrap (the helper block gained fsaA_swing, fsaA_cancel, fsaA_shake, fsaA_fl, fsaA_cloud, fsaA_leaf and fsaA_gear):** David 38 frames (a grey breath cloud out of him 1-13, sagging to the floor 6-24 and thinning from 30, while he slumps forward; Every blast number is the old one frame for frame: 48 scenes (six a fighter: both facings, ground and air, foes in and out of reach) run on the build just before this batch and on this one, every field of every fighter and every shot identical from the blast on (the holder's own position, speed and grace included, with Firey Jr.'s hop off), and against live main up to where each Final Smash ends (live main's FS FLOOR tops a surviving foe up after it, which this branch does not have yet). The CATCH still lands 15 steps before the blast, as before; no framework line changed. With Firey Jr.'s hop on, the same hits land in the six scenes. Design changes: the painted limb rig has arms for David (one), Firey Jr. (one) and Fern (two) only, and none for Roboty, Sidewalky, Tree or Cake, so the gather's raised arms show on those three and are the body's own motion on the rest; David's stomp is the shared stomp's lifted leg, with 85% of its lean back and squat taken off (fsaA_cancel) so he stays slumped, and Fern has 60% of the gather's crouch taken off so he stays composed; Sidewalky's three pictures are crush, brace and breaking (the red enraged one is left for his own special); the shakes are networked impacts with no dust (the guest feels them too). Knock-back (Firey Jr.): a scripted slide with a hop (fsaA_slide, fsaA_room, fsaA_head): 28.0 px a volley measured with the hop (25 if he stays on the floor), the hop 17.7 px high (12.4 on low gravity), shorter and then none near a ledge: fsaA_room tests the whole slide every 4 px and 12 px past its end and stops it 8 px short of the last good spot, so the slide, or float error at a span's end, never puts him past the edge (the first build tested every 8 px and not the last spot: standing 1-4 px inside the ledge on Goiky, x 126-129 facing right or 895-898 facing left, the first volley ended past it; the same helper serves Spikey's release in batch 1); 1184 starts across Goiky, Yoyleland, the Moon, Stone Pillars and the Incinerator (every 3 px along the 44 px at each span's ends, every 40 px along them, both facings, on the floor and 60 px up): none KO'd, none off the surface; no hop high in the air, under a solid block or on the Moon, and no launch. Pre-existing, left as it was: Firey Jr., Fern and Tree's own deforms take Math.pow of a negative swing pulse through the blast's long swing (a NaN scale the canvas ignores). Checker fixes: fsaA_wrap arms the entrance's swing 3 frames longer (every batch), so a guest is sent its kind to the last frame (it lost it two frames before the blast and drew the shared gather's lean for those two: the in-process pair now matches the host on every entrance frame of all eight); the blast is still the old one frame for frame (the 48 scenes again, hop off); Fern's sun is paler so his face shows; Sidewalky's hurled slab fades over its last frames; Ruler is dead upright from the last click. Left alone, not this batch: a CPU holder can queue a normal smash in the tail of its own Final Smash (the old build did too, its Final Smash was just over first). Checks: both facings, ground and air; KOs at five moments of each and a hit through the entrance; the in-process online pair draws the same on both screens (0.4 px) and the guest is sent the same limb kinds; three CPU free-for-alls clean; sheet in reports/moves/fsa-a-3-sheet.png.

Checks (jsdom and the in-process pair, no browser, no relay): syntax, boot; each act's numbers, phases and leaving; Teams arena; Boss Rush (every act but Clapboard hits the boss's side; a boss is never frozen); CPU FFA, Teams and Boss Rush with all seven summoned, no errors; the pair with each trophy on each side, the guest's phases, what they put out and the %/statuses the host's. - **2026-11-02, FSA lane b: an entrance for Silver Spoon, Tapey, Tea Kettle, Bot, Eraser, Knife, Lightbulb and Taco (II) (the owner, "every Final Smash gets an in-character ENTRANCE ANIMATION before its blast"; "All OK, build them"):** each row is its old row with an entrance built in front of it (`fsaB_row`, the marked block "FSA lane b" in `index.html`): the wind grows to the entrance's length (Silver Spoon 40 frames, Tapey 34, Tea Kettle 38, Bot 30, Eraser 34, Knife 40, Lightbulb 36, Taco 36), the old row runs on the shifted clock so its blast, hits, numbers, lock and end are the old ones, and the catch lands 15 frames before the blast as it always did (nobody is held through the entrance). SILVER SPOON blacks out into a shuddering silhouette, gold climbs him from the foot of the handle to the bowl, a gleam flashes on the bowl and he floats a few px (his render, `fsaB_body`). TAPEY: REWIND (whirring shake, tape loops spooled out and in), PLAY (button clicks and glows, grille swells), then EJECT: the wave rocks her back 50 px with the tape streaming off her. TEA KETTLE: rattles, flushes, three steam puffs each bigger and quicker, tips her spout at the foe and a plume of steam wraps round them like arms by the first squeeze; the boil-over shoves her back 60 px. BOT: a hop with a spin, then one stilt-leg (Bridge the Gap's drawing) grows out to the foe in 12 frames, pistons with each kick and whips on the last. ERASER: slumps, one slow lazy wipe with a pale smear growing over 12 frames and crumbs, a shrug, and the smear snaps away as the slate is wiped. KNIFE: swings a sack off his back, digs in and pulls a blade glint, a caltrop and a spark up in turn, hoists and upends it as the nine things pour. LIGHTBULB: a threaded socket grows over her head, she hops up and over into it (her base is at her feet), is screwed in with her glow stuttering, and drops back as the flash goes off. TACO (II): dead still, her Retractable Arms bring a lemon in each hand to her mouth and her cheeks puff in one snap. Knock-backs (Tapey, Tea Kettle) are scripted slides from the frame after the blast, kept on the surface and at least 28 px inside any ledge or solid block by `fsaB_room`: it looks every 4 px (and at the slide's exact end) out to 30 px PAST the slide's end, and cuts the slide to the last good spot less 30, so a ledge anywhere in that reach shortens it and a holder already within 30 px of a ledge is not moved at all (none on the Moon; the checker's first version looked only to the slide's end and could drop her off a ledge 49-50 px, Tapey, or 57-60 px, Tea Kettle, from it: fixed). Framework untouched but for two hooks: a Final Smash row can turn the gather off (`stupK` is wrapped in the block) and `drawSpriteBody` calls `fsaB_body` after the face layer. No words, no new wire fields. Checks (own scripts, jsdom): the blast on the old build (`smash-bal`) and this one in the same six scenes per fighter (both facings, in the air, foes on the ground and in the air, near and far) is identical frame for frame from the catch on (foes' places, launches, damage, stun, statuses, holds, KOs, shots erased); every entrance plays through in both facings, on the ground and in the air; holder KO'd, foes KO'd, a foe's own Final Smash, a hit on the holder at 8 moments each end cleanly; an edge sweep of the knock-back (the floor ends of Goiky Field, Yoyle, Grandplains, Sky Tower and Canyon and both ends of a platform, the holder every pixel from 4 to 100 px from the end, recoil toward the drop) never leaves the surface, loses no stock and ends 28 px or more inside; the in-process online pair draws the same op for op; the CPU fires on the same frames; three CPU free-for-alls of batch members run clean (Final Smashes 2-6 each, no NaN, nothing stuck). Polish after the check: Tapey's tape loops spool out over 12 frames, her button glow and grille grow over 12 and the EJECT ribbon streams out over 12; Taco's arms are drawn at her Retractable Arms' width. Contact sheet: `reports/moves/fsa-b-1-sheet.png`.
- **2026-11-02, FSA lane b, batch 2: an entrance for Marshmallow, Apple, Baseball, Pickle, Nickel (II), Paper, Microphone and Salt (the owner, "every Final Smash gets an in-character ENTRANCE ANIMATION before its blast"; "All OK, build them"):** built the same way as batch 1 (each row is its old row behind an entrance by `fsaB_row`, in the marked block "FSA lane b" in `index.html`; the wind grows to the entrance's length, the old row runs on the shifted clock, the catch lands 15 frames before the blast): Marshmallow 42 frames, Apple 34, Baseball 40, Pickle 36, Nickel (II) 30, Paper 36, Microphone 36, Salt 32. `fsaB_row` gained an optional `pin` (the old row's tick and draw see the holder where she stood when the blast went: Marshmallow's rings stay centred on that spot while she is flung). No words are drawn anywhere; nothing is random. MARSHMALLOW: a spinning saw blade (toothed, flat on the floor) grows in under her over 12 frames (0-11) while she is up off the floor, floating down (5-21) with her feet paddling (her body's wobble and little arcs of air under her), stops dead a hair above the teeth (22-25, a squash and jolt marks), fills with breath (26-41, her body swells, air streaks close on her mouth) and screams; the scream flings her up 70 px and back 40 over 10 frames (the render's hover fades as she goes) and she floats down after it (her fall speed held at 1.5); the knock-back is cut to stay 28 px inside any ledge (`fsaB_room`), 160 px inside the top blast line (`fsaB_rise`: she ends no higher than 100 px above the top of the world, and the rise stops under a solid block overhead) and does nothing on the Moon. APPLE: looks left (3-9) then right (10-16), her render mirrored each way (the throw's direction is untouched), sags and hangs her head (17-26: the leaf is part of her render, so the droop is the whole body's), stamps twice (24, 29) with little hops and dust, and yanks a pencil out from behind her with a thin black arm (it grows in over 12 frames, 21-32; the yank 29-33) so she ends facing the foe with it held ready. BASEBALL: two embarrassed 5 px hops (2-8, 11-17, dust and a small shake at each landing), a deep crouch (19-25) and one 100 px leap (26-39) that comes down on the frame the shockwave goes; his place never changes. PICKLE: a small gold bell swings in above him over 12 frames and dings twice (6, 13, with arcs), he bobs and weaves on his toes (9-21), red gloves grow on over 12 frames, a loose one-two (22-26, 27-31) with a white flash at each glove, then a deep crouch (30-35); his render turns to the spot the old row fixed (his facing in the sim does not change: the blast's throw for a foe exactly on the spot is his facing, which a first build that turned him changed and the parity check caught). NICKEL (II): leans back smug (0-8) while two empty speech bubbles with a zig in them (his jab's bubble), a pale one at his left for Light Nickel and a dark one at his right for Dark Nickel, grow in over 12 frames and swell side by side (12-26); the shrug (24-30); the pale bubble bursts on the first ring and the dark one on the second, 8 frames later. PAPER: a flat white ruled sheet grows in his hands over 12 frames, three folds 9 frames apart (11, 20, 29) take it to a tiny paper piano (the glyph's own shape in paper), he glances up nervously (30) and tosses it out of sight overhead (32 on: it climbs faster and faster, shrinking, and is gone by about 48); the real pianos come down as before. MICROPHONE: two speaker cabinets grow in on either side of her over 12 frames, their cones throb three times (13, 20, 27), each bigger, with arcs of sound toward her, shaking her so she rings like feedback; she draws a breath (her body stretches tall, 29-35) and shouts; she skids back 40 px (37-46) as it leaves (the lane stays where she stood: it is worked out from the spot she fired on) and the cabinets drop away. SALT: a glass shaker with a silver lid grows in over her head over 12 frames in both her thin black arms, shaken three times (12, 18, 24) with grains pattering round her; when Pepper is up her black shaker jiggles 7 frames behind each (the beat her echoes use; a guest's summons carry no owner, so Pepper is the nearest Pepper summon within 220 px on either screen); then she tips it toward the foe, salt running from the lid, and the arc comes out as before. Checked (jsdom, throwaway scripts): the blast is IDENTICAL to the old build (ii-dlc) in six scenes each (right, left, close, in the air, foes in the air, a low foe), 1404-1476 frames per fighter from the lock on: foes' places, launches, damage, stun and effects, hit sets, KOs and projectile counts, with the lock-to-blast span 15 frames as before; the entrance runs start to finish in both facings on the ground and in the air with no draw error, no words and no non-finite number; 22 interruption scenarios per fighter (the holder KO'd or thrown off at six frames from the first to 30 after the blast, a locked or free foe KO'd, a foe's own Final Smash, a hit aimed at the holder, glowing CPU foes) end cleanly with no stuck pose, prop, lock or double stock; Marshmallow's and Microphone's knock-backs over 1248 edge cases each (Goiky Field, Yoyle, Grandplains, Sky Tower, Canyon) and 204 platform-end cases never leave the surface, lose no stock and end 28 px or more inside; the fling never carries her above 100 px over the top of the world (the top blast line is 260 px over it); the in-process online pair draws the same op for op; the CPU fires on the same frames as before; three CPU free-for-alls of batch members run clean (Final Smashes 2-6 each, no NaN, nothing stuck); a 2v2 never hits or holds a teammate and plays the same as before. Contact sheet: `reports/moves/fsa-b-2-sheet.png`. 2026-11-03, checker fix (Paper): the old row's gather (the glow disc and the two pianos circling his head) stood there whole on the frame of the blast, because its own 14-frame wind never plays under the entrance; it now grows in over frames 24-35 from his middle (the disc from nothing to 112 px across, the pianos to 20 px wide), and frame 36 is the picture it always was; the blast is untouched (7 scenes against live main: 0 mismatches).

SAW: a fence-post plank grows in on frames 0-11, 34 px in front of her; she turns her teeth (they are down her left edge) to it by frame 5 and saws it in strokes at 10-13, 14-17 and 18-21 (she rocks into it, sawdust, a small shake on each, the cut a third deeper each time), the top half topples away over 20-29, and she revs up (two turns, upright again at the blast) while the old tell's blade circle opens over 20-33. TENNIS BALL: a small drawn trebuchet (A-frame on a wheeled base, long arm and sling, a counterweight box on a rope, a pedal plank) rolls up in front of him and grows in over frames 0-11; he hops (12-17), lands on the pedal on 18 (deep squash, a small shake), the counterweight drops on 19-25 (a thump and a shake on 25) and the arm cocks back over 21-30 with the ball in its sling; it then snaps its arm forward on each of his ten throws (frames 40, 44 ... 76: half way on the frame the ball goes, forward the next) and is put away over 12 frames after the last. SNOWBALL: mad pose; stomps on frames 5 and 12 (a foot lifted for 4 frames, a shake, a flat white ring out along the floor and eight snow chips off his top each), the dig crouch from 15 while the heap in front of him packs and grows over 12 frames, snapping up into the charge pose on 27 and holding it 10 frames past the blast as the heap goes; sixteen loose flakes sift down round him from the start. BOOK: flares wide open over frames 0-5, a cream page grows in on her front and a quill grows in over 12 frames and scribbles three loops on it over 6-18 (no letters), the page lifts off with two loose ones over 19-30, the old tell's five purple books rise round her over 22-35, and she snaps shut on 31-33 with a thump and a shake on 33; her own swing takes over at the blast. RUBY: a shaft of white light drops onto her from above over frames 0-11 (a pool of it on her footing), she twirls two turns in it (3-26), seven four-point glints pop off her facets one after another (frames 4, 7 ... 22, each for 9 frames), the light flares white over 21-27 and fades over the 8 frames after the blast. Checks (own scripts, jsdom): every entrance played start to finish on the ground and in the air, both facings (every frame drawn through a recording canvas: no thrown error, no word, no non-finite number); the old build (this branch before the batch) and the new run the same scenes (right, left, close, holder in the air, far, foes in the air, near an edge) and every field of every foe and shot from the first frame of the old row, shifted by the lengthened wind, is identical (damage, launch, hit set, stun, poison, weak, slow, burn, lock, KO: 0 differences over 56 runs; the only fields that differ are time stamps that moved with the wind), the foe is caught 15 frames before the blast as before; the CPU fires on the same frames as before (72 positions); 22 interruption cases each (the holder KO'd at eight moments or thrown off the stage at three, a held foe or a free foe KO'd, a foe's own Final Smash, a hit on the holder, CPUs glowing) end cleanly with no stuck pose, prop, lock or double stock; the in-process online pair draws the same op for op (and the forced pictures are the same keys on both screens); three CPU free-for-alls of batch members run clean (Final Smashes 1-6 each, no NaN, nothing stuck); a 2v2 never hits or holds a teammate. Checker fixes: the `still` rule (no gather lean or glow through an entrance) now finds the row by `fsaB_of`, so the posed pictures (Box's flaps, Snowball's mad, dig and charge) run clean too (the lookup used to miss under the posed name "Box (flaps-1)" and the framework's startup lean and glow were laid over them); the last bits that did not grow in over 12 frames now do (Box's peanuts spiral in over 18 frames and grow from nothing over the first 12, and are packed away as the flaps slam; Trophy's camera grows from nothing; Test Tube's green inner light ramps over 12 frames from frame 22, full as she goes off). Things to know: a fighter wearing a skin keeps the skin's plain render (drawFighter skips the body hook under a skin: the rule for every pose-set fighter), so a skinned Box or Snowball plays only the props, shakes and squashes of its entrance, not the flaps or the mad, dig and charge pictures, and CPUs wear a skin about half the time; Test Tube's old startup burst (a small shake and ten glass chips) fires at entrance frame 20, as the third crack starts; Snowball's heap and rings and Tennis Ball's trebuchet stand on the line of the holder's feet, so a Final Smash fired in the air shows them hanging there; the lock's `until` backstop is the entrance's length later than before (the lock itself is released on the same frames). BELL: three swings from the loop on top (10, 22 and 35 degrees, far ends at 8, 21 and 34) in her "before ring" picture and her "ding" picture at each far end, a brass clapper grown in over 12 frames clanging the lip each time (a pair of ring-arcs and a puff each), snapping upright on the third as the ring goes (her ding picture goes on through it). BRACELETY: two hops (3-14, 14-25) with her painted arms up in the framework's gather (held at a fixed light strength: `gather`), the ICY sign overhead on a drawn arm to each corner (grown in over 12 frames; a plain blank board if its art has not loaded), glints at each landing, the sign flung spinning up and away on 27, her eyes opening wide over 12 frames (from 22) and her held stillness. GATY: squeezes edge-on about her gold hinges over 14 frames, hangs open 6 (the hinges glint, paint flakes drift down off her), swings back and shuts on 27 with a thunk and a puff of dust. GRASSY: Tree (the Grasstree picture) grows up out of the floor behind him (0-12; drawn from drawFighter, behind the body), sways three times (12-30) with leaves shaking loose while he strains, sinks back into the floor (30-40) and the blades burst up from there. LIY: three clicks, a silhouette and a white spark at the toggle on the first two OFFs (3-6, 10-13), the match's own dim dipping to near-black on each (a screen darkening after the match's dim: `screen`), the third OFF held 8 frames (17-25) with her lit and the dark closing round her; then the lights snap back. LOLLIPOP: her laughing picture 10 frames (head back), her twirl pictures 9, her 15 screech pictures one frame each, pink candy glints flying off the last frames. MARKER: the cap flicked off (2: a small drawn green piece spinning up and away) with his Uncapped picture (the skin's, as a new one-picture pose row POSE_SETS.Marker), tipped forward nib-ahead (3-10) with three quick shakes (12, 17, 22), a bead of purple ink swelling at the nib (13-25) and three drops spattering the floor; he stays tipped through the volleys, each pushing him back 10 px along the floor (four skids after the volleys at 30, 36, 42 and 48; none in the air), and rights himself after (54-64). Knock-back (Marker): 40 px at most, kept on the surface and 28+ px inside every ledge by `fsaB_room`; the old row fires every volley from where he stood across when the first went (`pin`, sideways only: a holder in the air falls as before), so the blast is the old one. Checked: the blast is identical (same foes, damage, launch vectors, hit set, stun, effects and lock, frame for frame from the blast on, six scenes each: both facings, close, high, air) to smash-bal over the whole run; the entrances play start to finish on both facings on the ground and on a platform with no draw error, no word and no non-finite number; every interruption ends clean; the in-process online pair draws the same on both screens; CPU free-for-alls and teams runs of batch members run clean. Things to know: a fighter wearing a skin keeps the skin's plain render, so no pose picture shows (Bell, Lollipop, Marker keep the motion and the props); a costume does the same; the sheet's Bracelety shows her painted arms down because the sheet does not rig limbs; Grassy's Tree is not drawn by the entrance while his own Grasstree special is up (its tree is already behind him); Liy's dim is a screen overlay, so it also covers the HUD for those frames, as the match's own dim does; Marker's volleys leave his middle, not his nib, as before. The blast is identical to live main (the one difference in the foes' numbers is the hit-sequence counter `_chainSeq`, one higher because the entrance arms the gather once more; it is only ever compared with the holder's own count, and it was so before this fix too). 2026-11-03, checker fix (Bell): her clapper (a rod and a brass ball) hangs below her lip, and the ring-arcs and puffs of each clang reach under it, while she stands with her lip on the floor line, so all of it was drawn in the grass (measured per frame against the line of the floor, f.y + f.r + 6, on the build before the fix: the rod 12-28 px, the ball 15-31 px and the ring-arcs 22-65 px below it); with the holder on the floor the clapper's hang point is now kept 3 px and its ball 7 px above that line (`keepRod`, `keepBall`: the ball rides her lip, still striking it at the same three beats, and each clang's ring-arcs and puff are centred on the ball where it now is), and the whole entrance draw is cut at the floor line (a clip), so no part of it is drawn below the line (0 px on all 53 frames, both facings); a holder in the air has no floor under her and is drawn as before. Render only: the sim, the blast, the catch and the timings are untouched (the blast is the same as live main in the seven parity scenes, with no differing foe field).

Second Hand 6%, Twelve Sharp 5%, Six O'Clock Sweep 4.2%, Pendulum 4% + 4%, Clockwise 7.5% and Off the Wall 9.5% (spike; both jump-cancel on a hit), Counterclockwise 9.5%, Ten to Two 5%; Bends All Logic (reach 28) with Tick Away 2.3% (the design's 1.5 times the 2026-10-28 pummel scale 2.5/1.6) and four throws (7, 8, 5, 5); Clock Hands 6% to 16% by Natural Time (cooldown 48, works in the air), Spring Forward 5%/8% at the tips, Fall Back (half of the last 3 s back, 10% at most, its own 8 s, heal only: 51), Strike Twelve 23% (knockdown), Daylight Savings Swingset (FINX `swing`: 3 + 3 + 8, rooted, a hit on him stops it; 52) and Infinitely Powerful (his face huge in the sky; the 300 px catch greyed and held, 12 x 1% then 26% in play); side tilt and side special kept as data rows. (3 vs 1: you vs Clock, Loser and Pin, all three on the team in the wiki's All For One page), Fast Forward (Goiky Field, fast forward, the A-twos-ment Park backdrop); his assist row in `ASSIST_LEAVES_AT_LAUNCH` (55). Contact sheet: `smash-bal/reports/moves/event-clock-sheet.png`. AFTER THE CHECKER (2026-11-01): HELD, NOT TO BE PUSHED until the owner answers O28's open question, how a kit joins the lap. Today a kit joins by its event row alone, so this build ALONE makes next week Clock's: on 2026-11-01 this week stays Eraser's, then the weeks go Clock (11-02), Eraser, Clock, Eraser, Clock, Eraser (on ii-dlc every week is Eraser's), and the title's "Next week" turns from Eraser to Clock. A Fall Back that cannot start (under a third of Natural Time, or inside its 8 s) now does nothing at all (`clockFallReady`, asked in `fireSpecialInner` before any cooldown is set): it used to set the shared down-special cooldown and lock Clock Hands and Spring Forward for 0.85 s. Clock Hands now fires at once after such a press; a real Fall Back is unchanged (120 of Natural Time, its own 8 s, 8 of 16 taken back); the CPU already pressed it only when ready. DECIDED WITHOUT AN OWNER ANSWER: Wind Back after a lost stock -- when the newest note 10 s old is from the stock before, the fighter goes to 0%, how this stock began (a literal reading would leave them as they are). FOR THE OWNER: Clock Hands' least in real play is about 7%, not 6%, because the gauge refills through the 10-frame snap-up and the 48-frame cooldown (6.3% from an empty gauge, 11.3% at half, 16% on the hour). Left for the lead's eye: a thin tan halo on the upper-left rim of `clock-ring.png` (and two specks above it) and a faint teal line on the left rim of `clock-win.png`; they hardly show at game size.

Art: her TPOT render only (`pie.png`, already the trophy's picture); every stretch, flap, crumb and berry is drawn in code (`FIGHTER_ANIM.Pie`, `pieDrawBerry`), no new picture, no download; no FR_FACE or FRAME_SRC row (her face stays the render's), `LIMB_RIG.Pie` empty, hurtbox traced from the render without her painted stick limbs (66 px body). Event data: `EVENT_INFO.Pie`, `EVENT_MATCHES.Pie` -- Cake at Stake (Goiky Field with the new `cakeatstake` backdrop: the Announcer's place's own `annDecor`, no boss; usual items, Smash Balls), Death P.A.C.T. (the 3 vs 1: you vs Pie, Pen and Remote on the Teams arena, Stone Pillars' sky; no Smash Balls) and 500 Steps (Yoyle Fortress, throwables only; you vs Pie, Remote and Bomby) -- and `EVENT_BACKDROPS.cakeatstake`. Online: no new relay message -- `_flutT`, `_flutT0`, `_crumbT` join NET_FIGHTER_FLAGS, the berry's `berry` and `bst2` ride its projectile row, the rest is the swing, the wind-ups, the FINX row and the fs* fields; Arm Flutter and her crumbs are busy frames for the guest's prediction (`netPredOut`). ASSIST: Pie is also an assist trophy (act `bolt`). The hand-over rule of the six earlier kits (`ASSIST_LEAVES_AT_LAUNCH`: the trophy leaves the bag once events have launched) would have taken it the moment she was added, weeks before she can be earned (the launch is long past), so her name is in the new `ASSIST_LEAVES_AT_OWN_WEEK` instead: the trophy stays in the bag until her own week has begun (`eventWeekBegun`: the first lap week that is hers, 2026-12-07 on this build), and stays for the boss endings and the crowd as before. DIFFERENCES FROM THE DESIGN: (1) the Final Smash's damage is the redesigned Final Smashes' size, about 100% on a foe held at 0% (the owner's "DUDE, WHAT DID I SAY ABOUT HIGH DAMAGE"): the design's 4/4/10 became 11/11/28 in the row (22/22/56 doubled by KO-LEVEL, measured 100.0% on a held foe), the last pop launching; it holds the three nearest foes (`catchMax` 3) so its length is fixed (120 frames), the design said every foe within 300 px; (2) the fork's bleed is 70 frames, not the framework's 150: her 7% plus a full bleed (6.75%) would out-hit her own 15% explosion; (3) the jab is the default jab with reach 24 (the design's "medium reach" is the slap's word on the card); (4) the up throw's berry is a chain only (it sets off her berries within 120 px of the foe; the throw itself is 5.6%); (5) the CPU's "stay under the fork only below 100%" is a read in `aiPieOut` (she walks out from under it otherwise). Arm Flutter rises about 146 px at recovery 1 (the other recoveries measured the same way: 121-151 px), so `rec` stays 1. CHECKS (jsdom only; no browser, no relay): syntax; boot (`ffa bad positions 0 teams bad positions 0 errors none`); CRLF intact; every move on a still dummy (jab 6, up tilt 5.6, down tilt 4.6, side tilt 6.4, Tin Spin 3.6 x 2, Stretch Punch 7.6, Wrong-Way Kick 9.2, Filling Pop 6, Upside Down 9.8 and its spike, the grab's 6-frame start and 36-frame whiff, pummel 1.5 and the four throws 7.4, 8.2, 5.6, 5.2); the berry (8-frame pluck, pops on a foe 7%, lands and sticks, grows in, pops by itself after 56 frames, a foe touching it before it is whole does nothing, two out and a third sets off the oldest, Set Them Off with and without berries, never hurts her team); Arm Flutter (3 flaps, steers 109 px in 24 frames, once per airtime, clips a foe beside her 3%); Taffy Snap 20%; the fork on a foe (8.2% with the bleed so far), on her own head (foe 15% and launched, she took 6%, 20 frames of crumbs) and on nobody (sticks in the floor); Chain Reaction 100.0% on a foe held at 0%, and the three nearest caught in a four-way room; her three event matches through the real screens CPU against CPU to the end (settings handed back exactly, tokens paid: +10 and the first-win +10 on the duel, +5 on the two lost); her unlock at 100 tokens (`isUnlocked`, playable, counted in `rosterCount`, never in a random lineup or the Daily while locked, greyed in the Event fighters group like the others); three Expert CPU free-for-alls with her in the room, 3600 frames each, no errors or bad positions; the in-process online pair with her on the guest (the berry in flight and stuck, the flutter, the fork and Chain Reaction the same on both screens, the guest's own walk, jump and steered flutter 0.0 px from the host's). HIT_JUMP_K: the air-loop search (a CPU Pen too) finds no loop for her at 1, so she keeps the default. BALANCE (the house yardstick: 1v1 against five mid-pack Hard CPUs -- Nickel, Tree, Bell, Pin, Pillow -- Goiky, 2 stocks, items off, 7200 frames, three seeds each; three seed bases): as designed she won 10 of 15, 11 of 15 and 15 of 15, 36 of 45 (80%), KOs 81 to falls 46, damage dealt 11342 to taken 7761 (1.46). That is above the middle (about 7 of 15), not mid-pack: the berries are strong in the CPU's hands. NOT TUNED (the brief said report; the design's numbers are kept, and no bot was touched). Owner calls: whether to nerf her (the cheapest knobs: the berry's 7% or its 50-frame cooldown, her weight 78, the fork's self-hit) and the differences above. Left out: Foldy, Stapy and Beach Ball (their own jobs), Moon Caves (another session's).

TOTAL 15 + 10 + 75 = 100% on the held foe, exactly (the framework's doubling, his damage stat and an Attack Up's x1.4 are divided out of every hit: `fsb3_row`), the orange band starts near 88%. KO-LEVEL, not a sure kill (Goiky Field, a CPU foe that steers back, the lowest start % that is KO'd in the middle, on the open side and at the edge): Puffball (46) 0/0/0%, Pen (100) 5/0/0%, Tree (138) 30/20/0%. A SECOND FOE near is only shoved back by the burst (4%, never held); a teammate is never caught, hit or shoved; a boss or an add is never touched (the catch only takes fighters; the row calls no damageSummons). NOBODY TO CATCH (or the foe stepped out or is dodging): the flash, the shove and the glow, 25 frames, no damage. THE CPU fires it only when its catch should take someone (a wrapper on `fsBotInput`, marked `fsb3Exact`: the default's "glow nearly out and a foe merely near" no longer fires it, and a CPU that declines keeps the keys it had, so with the glow nearly out it walks on to the foe instead of standing frozen; every other fighter's CPU is as it was). Its keys lag a few frames behind its choice, so about 1 press in 12 still finds nobody (6 of 79 in four 5400-frame free-for-alls before the fix): a CPU's press that does gives the glow back (less the 34 frames it took) and no buff (a wrapper on `fsTryFire` keeps the glow the press was made with); a human's miss is the flash, the shove and the buff, as designed. A foe KO'd mid-scene (a hit's own cap, at any of the three), the foe lost or the user KO'd ends it there with nothing locked and nothing stuck: he is back where he stood within 8 frames. WIRE: no new fields (fsAx/fsAy his place, fsBx/fsBy the statue, fsCx/fsCy the point under its feet, the foe's lock flag); the outline comes from `haHurtOf`, since a guest's fighters have no `.hurt` (the first draft drew a guest an ellipse). A move-name or other word is never drawn. Checked (own jsdom scripts): the sequence start to finish on the ground and in the air, facing right and left, with him facing away, the foe straight over him and above him (80 checks); the miss, the dodge, the shove and the CPU's trigger (18); the user KO'd at frames 14, 30, 60, 73 and 90, the foe KO'd at each of the three hits, a second foe, a teammate and a boss (31); every stage (nine kinds incl. the tall ones and the Moon): the middle, both walls and a foe in the air off the ledge: the statue's head clear of every platform, he stays inside the world and is back where he stood (144); the in-process online pair (a host and a client, no relay): every client frame matches a host frame and its drawing is the same call for call (6); three CPU free-for-alls of 5400 frames each: 28 of his Final Smashes, no error, no stuck lock; syntax, boot (`ffa bad positions 0 teams bad positions 0 errors none`), CRLF intact. CHECKER'S FIX ROUND: the spotlight's dark fill was a 1640 px square whose edges showed on any wide view (a grey box); it is now 8000 px (the gradient is flat past 780 px) and darker (0.34 -> 0.5; the light cone 0.34 -> 0.42); the punch's coins, shards and plinth chunks cut off when the row ended (the draw hook stops at the row's 100th frame): every piece now fades out by the last drawn frame (the row's 99th); a cut scene (the foe gone before the punch) used to draw the punch's debris from the clock's jump: the cut now clears the statue's places (fsBx/fsBy/fsCx/fsCy to null, their start values), so nothing of the scene is drawn; he drops back still gold: `end()` sets `_starT` (the shimmer his Glowing Gold special gives) beside the buff, so the gold carries on after the coat fades; the flame round his head is seven thin rising tongues with a pale core (it read as a palm leaf); the CPU's frozen stand and the miss, above. - **FSB lane 2 (2026-11-02), Poppy's Final Smash is MEEPLE CONNECTION (the owner's own design: "3 metags bounce the fighter between them 2 times per metag, 8 percent each time, before launching them diagonally up"; branch `g-fsb-2`):** it replaces The jig is up! (twelve kernels twice, id `popcorn`) and renames the row: the owner titled the design, so the id is `meepleconnection` now (the old id was on no table but the row). THE ROOM'S MIDDLE is the foe's own spot, but at least 215 px from Poppy (so the near MeTag never stands on them), clamped 170 from the world's sides; its floor line is the floor they stand on (in the air, just under them, never under the ground, never so high the top slab leaves the screen); the foe is pinned inside the world's sides and top and above that line. MISS: with nobody caught (nobody in reach, a dodge's invulnerability, a step out in the 10 frames) the same entrance plays and then today's two kernel volleys (12 kernels each, frames 30 and 44, 9% a kernel; the old row itself, `FSB2_POP_FALLBACK`, run late by the difference between the two startups). DAMAGE is the owner's own 6 x 8%: 48% on the held foe, below the orange line (88% for a mid-weight), so the orange floor of branch `g-fs-floor` (not on this branch yet) is what takes a surviving foe the rest of the way. PROVED (jsdom, no browser): the sequence in both facings, on the ground and in the air (120 row frames, every blow 8%, 48% in all); the draw lists balanced with no text and no bad numbers; the user KO'd at nine moments, ground and air, and the foe KO'd by a blow (1st, 3rd) or by the launch or by an outside KO mid-lock all end clean with nothing thrown at whoever comes back; a second foe nearby, a teammate (Teams) and a boss untouched; the foe at either wall, high in the air and over a gap on Goiky Field, Yoyleland, Pillars, Grand Plains and Sky Tower pinned inside the world; the in-process online pair draws the same on both screens (structure identical, 0.5 px at most); three CPU free-for-alls with Poppy ran clean (41 uses, 40 holding a foe). ### O31 · Event fighters: kit 4 (Black Hole) -- what it left  [BUILT on `g-ev-blackhole`, NOT PUSHED: two decisions for you first]

Kit 4 (branch `g-ev-blackhole`, build step 11 of event-fighters-phase1.md) is Black Hole (he/him), from section 3.4 and the approved
questions 43 to 49. He is the fourth event row in ROSTER (`event:true`, kit `accrete`). His code is the section EVENT FIGHTERS: BLACK HOLE
(`BLACK_HOLE`, the `bh*` functions): Tug (jab), the tilts, the five aerials, the grab and four throws, Accretion / Slingshot, Wormhole,
Singularity, Event Horizon (SMASH_SPEC `horizon`), Spaghettify (FINX `spag`) and Mass of Twenty Suns (`FINAL_SMASHES['Black Hole']`). The Death
P.A.C.T. rule (`bhNoDown`): none of his hits sends a foe downward. His hurtbox is his black middle only (HURTBOX's circle, question 47).
His side special (Gravity Slide) stays off, kept as a data row. His assist row left the bag (`ASSIST_LEAVES_AT_LAUNCH`). His three event
matches are data (`EVENT_MATCHES['Black Hole']`), with two new twists, the pull and low gravity anywhere (`EVENT_TWISTS_BUILT`: `pull`,
`lowgrav`). Art: no downloads; his one render (already in the game as his assist picture), scaled, spun, squashed and stretched; the clod
and the pull streaks are drawn in code (CREDITS.md, `scripts/sprite-manifest.json`). Online: everything he draws rides the snapshot.
Contact sheet: `smash-bal/reports/moves/event-blackhole-sheet.png`.

- **Picks the design left open (the builder's own, not the owner's):**
  - *Death P.A.C.T. Again* (the 3 vs 1): the Teams arena with Yoyle Canyon's sky, items on (his Accretion pulls in loose items), no Smash Balls.
  - *Gravity* (the duel): Goiky Field, items off, no Smash Balls.
  - *Back to the Top*: Sky Towers in low gravity, items on, with Smash Balls.
- **Fixed after the check (2026-11-01):**
  - Spaghettify on the floor: the foe slid along the floor straight through his core. Now near a floor the low half of the orbit is turned
    over his head (`spU`), so they swing over him and back. In the air the full circle stays.
  - Singularity also spent the shared special cooldown, so it locked his Wormhole for 51 frames. Now it has only its own cooldown (150), as
    its comment said. The Wormhole fires the frame the Singularity ends.
  - The pull twist: `PULL.max` 1.2 -> 1.0. The old number left out HEAVIES MOVE SLOWER. The slowest walk is Tree's, 4.85 px a frame, and a
    quarter of it is 1.21, so 1.2 had almost no room.
  - Event Horizon's pull 10 -> 7 (the nerf rule of 2026-10-26). The design's 220 px and 21% are unchanged. A foe running away now gets clear
    from about 150 px; before, it was reeled in from about 180 px.
  - `scripts/trace-hurtboxes.mjs` skips him, so a re-trace cannot replace his middle-only circle with his whole render.
- **Balance: still well above the middle of the pack (YOUR DECISION; accepted at the strong end on 2026-11-02).** The tournament: 15 1v1 matches against 15 mid-pack Hard CPUs, run
  on three seed sets.
  - Before the fixes: 10, 12 and 15 wins out of 15, so 37 of 45.
  - After them: 14, 11 and 13, so 38 of 45.
  - Event Horizon still scores the most KOs (median KO about 120%).
  - Nerfs measured but not kept, each on two seed sets (30 matches):
    - pull 7 with 18%: 12 wins on one set;
    - reach 150, pull 7 and 18%: 23 of 30 (Spaghettify then took most KOs);
    - weaker launches on Event Horizon and Spaghettify: 27 of 30;
    - his hurtbox at the normal 24 (question 47's answer reversed, as a test only): 21 of 30.
  - No single Event Horizon change brings him to the middle: he is strong all round. The normal-size hurtbox gave the lowest number, but
    15 matches a set are noisy (one set went 10 -> 14 after a nerf). Choose the nerf.
- **OWNER DECISION BEFORE THIS IS PUSHED: how a kit joins the lap (O28's open question).** `eventLapOrder` runs the lap only through the
  kits that are built. So his row alone RE-DEALS EVERY WEEK SINCE THE LAUNCH: Eraser 10-26, Black Hole 11-02, Eraser 11-09, Black Hole 11-16,
  and so on. Today every week is Eraser's. Next week's card would change at once, against "a new lap is announced at least two weeks ahead".
  Spongy and 8-Ball come before him in the lap, so each of them landing would re-deal the weeks again. Two ways out:
  - O28's rule: each kit joins on a Monday of its own, at least two weeks after its build goes live;
  - or merge the kits strictly in lap order (Spongy, 8-Ball, Black Hole), with your OK.
  - **DECIDED 2026-11-02: "Straight into the lap, now"** (see O28): all six kits join the lap with their rows, and the week in progress re-deals.
- **Before merging:** the assist bag. Live main has 16 assists (Eraser left), and his leaving makes 15. The design says never below 17. Assist
  batch A2 (`g-ev-a2`, +7) is built but not merged. Merge it first, or together with this kit.
  **DONE 2026-11-02 (`g-ev-integrate`): A2 is merged in the same build, and with the five kits' launch departures the bag holds 18 (floor 17).**
- **Moon Caves:** nothing of his was changed for it, and it was not touched. A read-only check on the moon found no errors.

Checks (2026-11-01, the scratchpad's `events2/blackhole-fix/`): syntax; boot (FFA and Teams clean); every move fires and lands its numbers
on a still Pen, including the fixes above; his CPU at Hard uses every part of the kit (6 matches); his three event matches run CPU vs CPU to
the end and pay; the in-process online pair shows the special, finisher (with the turned-over orbit) and Final Smash the same on both
screens; the pull and low gravity predicted on the guest without snapping back.
- **2026-11-02, every difficulty's "late" recovery start is its own reaction (the owner, "do all four", of the CPU follow-ups):** the Expert change shortened the flat 26-frame blank of the recovery slip to one reaction; the other levels still froze for 26 frames (Hard slips 2.5 times as often as Expert, Normal 5 times, Easy 8.75 times). Now the blank at every level is that level's own `CPU_HUMAN.react`: Easy 30, Normal 20, Hard 10, Expert 10 (Easy is 4 frames longer than before, Hard 16 shorter). The slip itself, its rate and the drift and early slips are unchanged.
- **2026-11-02, skin names say the look (the owner: "skins could be better named"):** 118 of the 320 skins renamed; only the `name` text changed, every id, picture file, price and row position is the same, so saved wardrobes (the game stores the id), owned skins and the online codes are untouched, and nothing in the game keys on a name. A name counted as weak if (a) its leading word is a state, material, season or theme word shared by 3 or more skins (Blindfolded 16, Yoyle 9, Frozen 7, Season 5, Rockstar 5, Twinkle 5, Sweater 4, Snowy 4, Tangerine 4, Zombie 4, Cracked, Burnt, Snotty, Ghost, Announcer, Safety Vest, Hard Hat, Purple, Witch), (b) it read "Season N <Fighter>", (c) it was a vague adjective or a lore or era word that does not show the look (Damaged, Happy, Evil, Upgraded, CGI, BFDIA, Early BFB, Camera #1, Inner Flame, Thinking Hat), or (d) it was clumsy (Half-Frozen Toothpaste, Glowing Gold Spoon, Tree the Green). Costume-role words (Chef, Detective, Vampire) stay; the 9 prize skins and the 104 other Halloween costumes were left alone, except Chef Taco (II), which loses the clumsy (II). The 16 blindfold skins now lead with their blindfold's colour (Orange 5, Lime 5, Blue 4, Gold 2), the only names still sharing leading words, because the pictures differ only in team colour. All 118 new names are unique, 27 characters or fewer, and keep the fighter's name. Kept in step: the skin table in `assets/sprites/CREDITS.md`, the row keys and `name` of `scripts/sprite-manifest-skins.json` (each row's `slug` and `file` keep the old picture name: the pictures did not move, so to re-fetch one, give `fetch-sprites.mjs` its old name), and the skin names in comments of `index.html` and in `docs/animation-move-design.md`; the wiki file names (`File:Blindfolded Taco (S4E3).png` and the like) and older ledger rows keep the old spellings. The full table with the reason for each is in `smash-bal/reports/skin-renames.md`.
