- **2026-11-02, FSC lane 2, batch 1 (Rocky, Soap, Basketball): three Final Smashes rebuilt as short staged scenes (the owner: "the final smashes have to look cool! look at steve's final smash", "just dont make them TOO complicated; you can vary the damage and knockback as well"; branch `g-fsc-2`):** Each is a catch-then-cinematic lock on the nearest foe in a box ahead of the holder, at most five beats, the foe visible all through, the last blow the launch, no words, every prop grows in over 12 frames, own art; a catch that takes nobody is spent at once with a small puff. The holder hangs where she is (in the air, or on a platform) and a foe is placed relative to her, so every scene plays the same on the floor, on a platform or in the air; the foe is never placed outside 40 px of the world's sides. ROCKY, INFINITE MATTER OVERLOAD (the owner: "use the 'rocky generator' thing from bfdi 13"): the Infinite Matter Generator whirs up in his open mouth and the foe is drawn in to 118 px; acid barf 14% (frame 20), red balls and blocks 18% (36), cake batter 20% (52), the stream thickening between (a piece every 3 frames, then 2, then 1) while the generator heats green to red and steams; at frame 80 it overloads and is destroyed, 48%, the launch (forward and up). 100% in all, held 83 frames. Canon (BFDI wiki, Rocky): the generator makes vomit, red balls, blocks and cake batter, and is lost in "Return of the Hang Glider" (the wiki numbers that episode BFDI 25, the owner wrote 13; the wiki does not say how it was destroyed, so the blast is our staging). The pitch's volcano (Mount Rocky) is not built: the owner's direction replaced it. The id is now `matteroverload` (the old `matter` carried a launch cut in FS_CATCH_KB measured for the old stream). SOAP, SCRUB FOR HOURS (the pitch, simplified): the stage dims to a mansion hall (wallpaper, skirting board, corner cobwebs, dust bunnies, a window) as she pumps her head three times; spray 8% (frame 14), wipe 8% and wipe 8% (28, 40), suck 10% (54: a canister rolls in, the foe is yanked onto the nozzle), two cobwebs torn through 8% each (82, 98) while she walks the hall up to 250 px with them on the nozzle, then the dial clicks inward to outward (114, icons) and the blow, 40% (122), sends them out through the window (glass bursts, dust bunnies trail), forward and a little up. 90% in all, held 126 frames. The walk stays on what she stands on (each frame there must be a surface under her, and 40 px of margin from its edge; in the air she stays where she is). Canon (II wiki, Let Er' R.I.P.): she cleans cobwebs and dust bunnies with a vacuum, a foe sticks to it, she switches it to blow and they fly out of the house. Changed from the pitch: 2 webs not 3 and 7 hits not 8, the suck without a long yank, the veil drawn about the view with no new geometry; 100% became 90% for a light fighter. Id kept. BASKETBALL, BEST FRIEND SLAM (first FAST BREAK: renamed, see the fixes row; the owner: "Summons robot flower. you choose the rest."): she calls her best friend; a court plank grows in under the scene and Robot Flower streams in at a runner's speed (her legs a blur) and skids in beside the foe; slam 22% (frame 30) and a higher slam 28% (52) into the plank (it cracks), a swing round overhead and the throw 12% (64), and the foe comes down on Basketball, whose Bounciness sends them back, 38% (86), up and back; Robot Flower is off again at speed. 100% in all, held 90 frames; her petals' buttons light on each blow. Canon (BFDI wiki): Robot Flower is Basketball's best friend, a robotic Flower replica with five metal petals that work as buttons, a lens for a centre and bolts; she runs to the moon in 2 seconds, lifts something twice her size, and in Balancing P.A.C.T. slams Basketball so hard she deflates; Basketball's Bounciness bounces back what falls on her. The pitch's hoop and dribble are not built (the owner's direction replaced them). The name is the pitch's (FAST BREAK) and the id `fastbreak` (the old `nothingbutnet` carries a x4 launch boost in FS_KO_FIX measured for four bouncing balls). CARD text is in each row's comment. KO from the foe's 0% on Goiky Field (a CPU foe steering back, both facings, three spots): a light foe (Bubble) and a mid one (Pen) from 0%; the heaviest (Tree, 138) from 10 to 15%. No framework change: the shared pieces are a marked block (FSC lane 2, `fsc2_`). Checks (2026-11-02, the scratchpad's `fsc-2/`): syntax; boot (FFA and Teams clean); every beat's frame and damage per hit and the total printed, both facings, ground and air; ledge, wall and open-air scenes (ends of the floor facing in and out, a platform's ends, open air, a foe behind her or 500 px off: spent at once, glow gone); the user KO'd and the foe KO'd at five moments each, the foe KO'd by the damage cap on the first blow, a second foe, a teammate and a boss in the box: all end clean with no stuck lock; the CPU fires only when its catch will land (foe 250 px ahead, at the box's edge, or after turning to face one behind it) -- apart from the framework's own rule that a CPU with under 150 frames of glow left fires with a foe within 420 px, which then misses and is spent; the in-process online pair draws the same on both screens (structure identical, 0.5 px at most, the held foe within 0.6 px); three CPU free-for-alls with all three in them, 6000 frames each, clean; no text ops in any picture; a contact sheet at `smash-bal/reports/moves/fsc-21-sheet.png`.

- **2026-11-02, FSC lane 3: Coiny's, Fries's and Bomby's Final Smashes rebuilt as staged set pieces (the owner picked all three pitches; "just dont make them TOO complicated; you can vary the damage and knockback as well"; branch `g-fsc-3`, batch 31):** Each is Bow's chairs' model: the nearest foe within 420 px is caught and held, a scene of drawn props grows in round them over 12 frames, timed blows land on the held foe, the last the launch (KO-level through `fsKbMult`; the framework's orange floor tops a survivor up). No framework line was touched: the three rows, one marked helper block (`// ---- FSC lane 3: begin/end`, names `fsc3_`, consts `FSC3`, `FSC3_COINY`, `FSC3_FRIES`, `FSC3_BOMBY`) and the card text (the comment above each row). THE CATCH of all three is a circle of 420 px round the holder, the very reach of the CPU's own fallback cue (`fsFoeNear(f, 420)`), nearest foe only (`catchMax` 1): a CPU press is never into nothing (the pitch's 360 px 'ahead' for Coiny and 200 px for Bomby became this one circle, because the framework's CPU cue fires on any foe within 420 whatever a row's catch is). A boss, an add or a teammate is never caught or hit (the rows touch no summon: their rings are fighters only). Row numbers on the wire are the existing `fsAx .. fsCy`; a guest draws from `fsT`, `fsLk` and those. COINY (id `monitor` kept, the pitch does not rename it; the monitor lob is gone): canon read on the BFDI wiki ('I'm the Main Character', BFDIA 22: Coiny feeds Leafy a Yoyleberry, grabs her and jumps down the hole; he and Needle run down the chasm sides hitting and shoving each other; the core of the Earth is a marble with bouncy properties; a burst of lava; the Earth closes up; the Coiny page: immune to lava, melting point near 1100 C). He brings the foe to his side (66 px, or 48, on the same floor: with nowhere safe they stay where they were caught), a drawn Yoyleberry goes in and tints them silver (a tint, no armour), a crack runs from his feet and the ground peels into a chasm: rock walls with scrolling strata and glowing veins, the dark closing round the pair. SIX BLOWS, 100%: left wall 12, right wall 12, lava dunk 16 (foe lit orange), core marble 14 and 16 (they bounce 52 then 96 px up and come down on it), then the Earth shuts (the walls slam together in the dark, no hit: the pitch's 12% is folded into the lava and the core) and the ERUPTION 30, a lava geyser that throws them straight up (kb up -27, 2.5 across), ring 130 px / 14% for anyone else at the spot. Row frames after the 10-frame catch: berry and crack 0-13, blows on 26, 40, 54, 70, 84, 112, 136 in all; held 116 frames. FRIES (id `getdigging`, the pitch's name; the old `fryfan` is gone): canon (the Fries page, Powers: Persuasion, a shovel landing in a target's hand makes them dig; 'Get Digging'; 'Respect to the Wicked': the ground collapses into the Noctiluca yoylean Cavern and his body glows azure; 'Start the Shift': Yoylite makes him blow up; the Fry Dart as the spark is the design's own link). A spinning shovel lands blade-down in the foe's grip; a foe in the air is driven down onto the floor under them (none within 380 px: a floating slab of earth is conjured under their feet, the held foe falls nowhere). SEVEN BLOWS, 74% (round 2): three scoops 5, 5, 5 (10 frames apart: a clod over the shoulder, the pit widening, a mound rising over their legs), the pit crumbles 8 (a glowing azure cavern cutaway under the floor line with fungus), two Fry Darts 9 and 9 dropped from above into the hollow (a blue-white pop each, the glow swells like a lit fuse), the blow 33 (a column of light and dirt, the shovel spinning away, kb up -30, 3 across), ring 120 px / 22% at the crater. Frames: shovel 0-12, blows on 24, 34, 44, 54, 68, 82, 98, 124 in all; held 102. BOMBY (id `meaty`, the pitch's name; the old `detonation` is gone): canon ('Bomby's explosions': in 'Meaty' he exploded six times after being thrown by Needle and once by Yellow Face onto the Eiffel Tower; the Meaty page: the French-themed hand-powered recovery centre, Needle throws him at the tower, Spongy says repeat at super speed, he explodes purple paint on the tower). The foe is brought to stand 150 px off (100, 60) on the same floor (nowhere safe: spent); a lattice Eiffel Tower rises behind them past the top of the view and a French hand-cranked booth (it hides Bomby; bell, crank, no letters) stands beside them. SEVEN BLASTS, 100%: six drawn throws of a copy of him, 6, 7, 8, 9, 10, 11 (= 51) on 22, 46, 64, 78, 88, 96 (24, 18, 14, 10, 8 frames apart: the last ones a strobe, the foe jittering), the tower turning purple, a soot husk dropping each time; a 12-frame silence (booth empty, one calm Bomby at the top); THE DROP 49 on 132: the real Bomby falls the whole tower with a long burning fuse and lands on the foe in a purple-white mushroom with shock rings (kb 19 sideways, -12), ring 200 px / 26%. The price kept: 5% a blast (7 x 5 = the old 35, never past the KO point), stunned 90 frames, `exposed`. 162 frames in all; held 136. The CPU's own 'will it land?' asks `catch` with no Final Smash running: for Bomby it sees nothing unless a foe could be brought to the tower. WHAT THE LAST BLOW KOs AT, Goiky Field, the foe a CPU from the let-go (starts 0, 10, 20, 30, 40, 60, 80%): all three from 0% for a mid-weight foe (Pen, w 100), from 10% for the heaviest (Tree, w 138); the light one (Puffball, w 46) from 0% but she floated back and lived at 20% (Coiny, Fries) and 80% (Coiny): a CPU's recovery, not the launch (the first tuning, up -33 / -34 / kb 28, KO'd Tree from 0 and was cut to Bow's reference). Total on the held foe printed per hit by the checks: 11 11 14 12 14 28 / 5 5 5 8 9 9 33 / 6 7 8 9 10 11 49 = 90 / 74 / 100% (round 2: Coiny a strong fighter 11 11 14 12 14 28, Fries a weak one 5 5 5 8 9 9 33, Bomby 6 7 8 9 10 11 49 = 100 because he pays 35% himself; measured exact per hit), at the row frames above, in both facings, the holder on the ground and in the air, the foe on the ground, in the air, on top of the holder and at the reach edge, the holder on a platform and at its edge, at the lip of Goiky's cliffs and over Yoyleland's gap (the held foe always has a floor under it, Fries's slab apart; the holder never lost a stock). INTERRUPTS end clean (the row checks the foe, `koSince`, before every frame; nothing is thrown at whoever comes back): the first blow KOs (the damage cap), the foe KO'd between blows, the holder KO'd early and late (Bomby at the top of the tower), a second and a third foe (never held, untouched till the last blow; the ring hits the near one once and not the far one), a teammate (never held or hit), a boss next to the scene (no hp lost), a missed catch (the intro plays, then a small puff; no stun, no cost) and a foe made invulnerable as the catch lands (missed): no lock outlives the Final Smash, no stuck intro. THE CPU: fires at once on a foe inside the catch; not from out of reach (it walks up, then fires and catches); not at a foe invulnerable for longer than its reaction delay (the CPU sees its foes as they were a moment ago: a foe who turns invulnerable this instant is still fired at and missed, as with every row); and fires the nearly-out fallback into a catch that takes them. Three CPU free-for-alls (4, 5 and 6 fighters, Goiky Field and Yoyleland, 3000 steps each, a Final Smash granted every 380 steps): 24 casts by the batch members, all caught, none spent, no error, no NaN, no stuck lock. ONLINE (the in-process pair, host Coiny/Fries/Bomby against a client Pen): the client shows the same id, its fsT within 1 frame, the same lock on the foe, the same row numbers (a 0 is absent on the wire), and draws all 181 / 169 / 162 frames through the validating canvas with no fault. Contact sheet `reports/moves/fsc3-r4-sheet.png` (Coiny's descent in twelve moments, then the bystander panels at 160-190 px and 150-250 px, then Black Hole and Spongy through the closing walls).
- FIN lane c2 (2026-11-02, branch `g-fin-c2`, NOT PUSHED), batch c21: Clover, Jack, Magnet, MeTag and Poppy get their own finishers (the owner: "finish the finishers."; design `smash-bal/reports/moves/finisher-ideas-v2.md`, Inanimate Insanity table). Each had the shared swing; each now has a bespoke finisher in the FINISHERS V2 framework (a FINX row, `finxStep`, drawn by `finxDraw`, on the snapshot as `fnx`, hit art, the 12-frame startup, grow-in, one at a time, foes only, boss = a plain hit). The code is one section before batch 8's header ("FINISHERS V2, LANE c2, BATCH c21") plus a marked helper block (`// ---- FIN lane c2: begin/end`, names `finC2_` / `FXC2`); no shared line was edited: the kits' rows are set with `ATKSPECIALS.x = ...`, the CPU's reads by wrapping `finEventWant` (`finC2_want`), the list with `FINX_AI_KITS.add`, and the five kit texts in `MOVE_TEXT` are the only existing lines changed. **Clover, Lucky Leaf Pinwheel:** a 12-frame spin, then she drifts up to 220 px along her floor (7 px a frame, stopping short of a wall or a floor's end, and with floor under the foe she carries) as a disc 44 px round her; whoever it touches is caught and carried in front of her, 3% on the touch and every 8 frames, the 4th flings (9 sideways, 10 up): 4 hits of 3% per foe; each foe flung lends her Attack Up for 3 s (given after the fling, so the four hits stay 3%; canon: Clover's luck is taken from the players round her, Snapshot Showdown and Tragedy at 60 Feet); then dizzy 14 frames; hold back in the spin to go the other way. **Jack, Grudge Haymaker:** a 12-frame wind-up (fists glowing red), then a fan 70 px past his body (about 94 px from his centre: "short reach" by the game's reachWord scale, the word on his card since 2026-11-02), live 5 frames: 12% and a hard launch (11, 8); on whoever last hit him (`lastHitBy`, a living foe) 18% and 1.6 times the launch; a miss leaves him open 16 frames. **Magnet, Stuck Together Like Fridge Magnets:** a 12-frame crackle, then a pulse on the nearest foe in clear sight (320 px at the press, 400 by the time it lands), 4%; a horseshoe mark grows on them 12 frames; then for 90 frames they are dragged 8 px a frame into the nearest other foe of hers (360 px, clear sight) and stuck to them: 6% each where they meet, both stunned 24 frames, both rooted to the end of the 90; with no other foe, rooted 90; a wall, a slab, the stage's side or a gap in the floor stops the drag (it only lets go); a boss takes a plain 4. **MeTag, Barrier Clap:** a 12-frame glow of the logo (canon: it glows while they track someone), then two see-through panes rise 60 px either side of the nearest foe (more for a big one), 104 px tall (a jump clears it), growing 12 frames, standing 24 (step out), closing over 7: 11%, stunned 24 frames, squeezed up a little; needs floor either side and no wall; a boss takes a plain 11. **Poppy, Jury-Rigged Wind-Up Walker:** a 12-frame winding while a walker of scrap builds itself 34 px ahead (12 frames, harmless), clanking on at 2.6 px a frame up to 300 px, 40 px tall (jump it); the first foe it meets is clamped in front of it and zapped at 8, 24 and 40 frames (3% each), then it pops at 56 (4% and a launch, 9 and 10); any foe's swing or shot wrecks it (the owner's and a friend's pass) and lets them go; stopped by a wall or a floor's end it winds down 24 frames; hold back in the winding to send it the other way; a boss takes the zaps as plain hits when no grace of the owner's own blocks them, and the pop pays the rest of the 13% (13 less what the zaps landed): an intact walker deals 13, a wrecked one keeps what landed (2026-11-02, fixes below). **Changed from the doc, and why:** the 0.6 s wind-up (Jack) and 0.5 s shimmer (MeTag) are the 12-frame startup rule plus what the move puts in the world (MeTag's panes shimmer 36 frames, 12 rising and 24 standing, 0.6 s, because 0.5 s left a person's reaction almost no time to step out of a 93 px strip); Clover's fling is the 4th hit as in Leafy's and Lollipop's cards, not a fifth beat; Magnet's doc row gives no numbers, so 4%, 6% a clash, 24 frames stun and 90 frames root are mine, a finisher worth about a launch and a free second; Poppy's speed and clamp timing are mine (the doc gives the 3% x 3 and the 4%). Question 1 of the doc (cooldown of the long ones) is applied as everywhere: MeTag's panes, Magnet's pair and Poppy's walker hold the finisher while they last and the 74 frames count from when they are gone; question 2 does not touch these five. **Checks** (own jsdom scripts, scratchpad `fin-c2/`): syntax; boot (FFA and Teams clean); every finisher from the real keys (V and C key events on a person's fighter) with its frames, damage, launch and reach printed; teammates never hit, held or marked; a hit in the startup stops each cleanly; a hit later (Clover's carry dropped; the other four go on without them); the user KO'd mid-move by the damage cap loses exactly one stock with nothing held, locked or drawn after; a foe at the damage cap is KO'd once by the move and let go; walls and floor ends (Clover and Poppy stop short, the foe never inside a wall or over a gap, Magnet's drag stops at a wall, a slab or a pit); in Boss Rush the boss is never held and takes plain hits (Jack 12, Magnet 4, MeTag 11, Clover one plain 8 (2026-11-02; it was 3 a beat and the boss's own grace let 2 of the 4 through), Poppy's walker is wrecked by the boss's own attacks as anyone's hit would); misses end cleanly (Magnet fizzles, MeTag whiffs, the walker winds down); the CPU at Hard in 5 matches each: Clover 10 presses, Jack 12, Magnet 22, MeTag 7, Poppy 5; three CPU free-for-alls with batch members (5, 6 and 7 fighters) clean: no errors, no bad positions, no double stock loss, every stock lost one KO; the in-process online pair (never the live relay) shows each the same on both screens (same phases, same percents) from the host's keys, and from a client's keys for Poppy and Magnet; kit text and move card carry each finisher's name. The contact sheet is `smash-bal/reports/moves/fin-c21-sheet.png`. **Fixes after review (2026-11-02, same branch; each proved on the same scene before and after, scripts in the scratchpad's `fin-c2/fix/`):** (1) **Jack's card** said "medium reach" for a fan that reaches 94 px from his centre (70 past his body, radius 24): it says "short reach" now (reachWord: short up to 120 px, medium up to 240). (2) **Poppy against a boss:** the three 3% zaps, 16 frames apart, fell inside the boss's own per-attacker hit grace (17 to 30 frames): of the 13% the card promises, 6% landed (zaps 1 and 3) and the 4% pop never did. The first fix made the zaps on a boss only the show and the whole 13% ONE plain hit at the pop, but then a walker wrecked before the pop (a boss's own swing does it, 3 or more times a run) dealt nothing, where the old code dealt 3 to 6%. **Second round (2026-11-02):** the zaps land as plain 3% hits again, each when no grace of the owner's own is open on the boss (the zaps are 16 frames apart and a grace is 17 to 30, so one is lost), and at the pop the boss takes what is missing of the 13% (13 less what the zaps landed) as one plain hit, tried every frame for up to 30 frames after it if a grace is open (a jab of the owner's a moment before): an intact walker still deals 13 in all (3, 3 and 7), a walker wrecked first keeps the zaps that landed (0, 3 or 6), and never more than 13. (3) **Clover against a boss:** each of the four 3% beats was a separate hit through the boss's grace and the disc drifts past a boss in about 37 frames, so 2 of the 4 landed (6%); `FXC2.pin.boss` (8) was defined and never read. Now the disc's touch is ONE plain 8%, once, while she drifts (a frame later if a grace is open); a fighter still takes 4 hits of 3%. (4) **Magnet:** the other foe was rooted for the full 90 frames from the moment the drag started and never let go, so a drag that ended without a clash (a block rising in the dragged foe's way, a pit or ledge ahead, the dragged foe let go of or KO'd) or a row that died mid-drag (a KO of hers or of the dragged foe) left them rooted up to 85 frames with no hit. Now their own root from before the drag is remembered and put back at every such end (only the root this row gave: a longer one some other effect gave them since is left alone, and a root of theirs from before runs on, neither cut nor stretched); a clash is unchanged (both rooted to the end of the 1.5 s, identical frame by frame before and after).
- FIN lane c2 (2026-11-02, branch `g-fin-c2`, NOT PUSHED), batch c22: Silver Spoon, Tapey, Tea Kettle, Teddy Bear and Bot get their own finishers (the owner: "finish the finishers."; design `smash-bal/reports/moves/finisher-ideas-v2.md`, Inanimate Insanity table; the last five of that table). Each had the shared swing; each now has a bespoke finisher in the FINISHERS V2 framework (a FINX row, `finxStep`, drawn by `finxDraw`, on the snapshot as `fnx`, hit art, the 12-frame startup, grow-in, one at a time, foes only, boss = a plain hit). The code is one section after batch c21's (`FINISHERS V2, LANE c2, BATCH c22`) with its own marked helper block (`// ---- FIN lane c2: begin/end (batch c22)`, names `finC2_` / `FXC2` / `FINC2_`); the kits' rows are set with `ATKSPECIALS.x = ...`, the CPU's reads by wrapping `finEventWant` (`finC2_want2`), the list with `FINX_AI_KITS.add`, and the five kit texts in `MOVE_TEXT` are the only existing lines changed. Four small wraps sit inside the helper block, none touching a shared line: `fxRelease` (what to undo when a finisher's object ends for any reason: the bought foe back to their own side, the paint off), `applyHit` (Teddy Bear's 25%, read only for a paintball of hers on a foe she painted), `asTrackPct` (the Clock's once-a-frame memory, now also the position memory) and `finEventWant`. **Silver Spoon, Everyone Has a Price:** a 12-frame wind-up with a fat bag of gold held up (a hit on him stops it), then it is lobbed in the carton's arc at the nearest foe ahead within 360 px (nobody: 200 px ahead; hold back in the wind-up to lob it behind); it flies as a body and lands on the first foe, boss, floor, wall or stage side it meets: the foe takes 8% (a small flinch) and is BOUGHT for 120 frames by the engine's own Outbreak mechanism (they join his side, mirror his keys, in a free-for-all their own blows rebound on them), then go back to their own side (`_origTeam`); a foe already turned, a hit that KO'd them or a counter turned away is not bought, and he cannot buy while turned himself; a KO of either side ends it at once (the bought foe is put back the same frame); a boss takes the 8 as a plain hit. Canon: his exit interview ("I charge a small fee of one carat gold per annoyance"), Yin-Yang in A Jury of Your Fears ("Don't fall for his fools' gold"). **Tapey, Rewind Them Two Seconds:** a 12-frame wind-up (her two reels spinning up), then a length of tape reaches out 12 frames to the nearest foe within 300 px in clear sight who stands on the stage (they can leave its 380 px or its sight, or knock her out of it), catches them, and winds them back along their own recorded way over 20 frames to the oldest safe place in the last 2 s (floor or a platform under it, no wall or block round it, inside the stage: a place over a pit or inside a new block is skipped, so never off the stage; a foe already off the stage is not a target at all, it would be a rescue): 8% and stunned 24 frames where they land. The engine piece the doc asked for is the position memory (`finC2_histRec`: every fighter's place for the last 132 frames while a Tapey is in the match, a KO or jail empties theirs, none on Moon Caves where the finisher does nothing); nothing to wind back to (just respawned) and the 8 lands where they stand; a boss takes the 8 as a plain hit and is not rewound; a hit on her snaps the tape and lets them go where they are. Canon: Tapey's fourth button "cycles backwards through her files" (her page, Appearance). **Tea Kettle, Pour a Fresh Cup:** a 12-frame tip of her spout (heat and steam gather at it), then a scalding stream for 60 frames: ten points two frames and a bit apart on an arc (8.2 px a frame, gravity 0.18, so level it droops to the floor in about 125 px and reaches about 200), stopped by the first floor or wall, extending over 12 frames; held up or down it is aimed up (-0.62 rad) or down (+0.7) as the pour starts and steers 0.05 rad a frame all through (a CPU aims at its foe); six splashes (6 frames in, then every 10): each foe the stream touches takes 2% (12% if they stand in it the whole second), a small nudge back (not a stun: they can walk out) and burns 110 frames; a teammate it touches is healed 1.5% a splash; a boss takes six plain 2s; she is held still through it and a hit on her stops it. Canon: she "poured tea for The Thinkers" (The Overthinkers) and her Oversteam is steam out of the spout. **Teddy Bear, Paint Grenade, Then Paintballs:** a 12-frame hold of a paint grenade (a hit on her stops it), then a lob at the nearest foe ahead within 340 px (nobody: 200 ahead; hold back to lob behind) that goes off where it first meets a foe, boss, floor, wall or stage side: a splat spreading out from there to 160 px over 12 frames, 7% to each foe in clear sight of it once, and every foe hit is PAINTED for 240 frames: her Paint Gun (and the paintballs of her moveset) does 25% more to them (5 becomes 6.25, read in the `applyHit` wrap only for her paintballs on her paint); a boss takes the 7 as a plain hit; the finisher waits for the paint to wear off; KO of any side or her own KO takes the paint off. Canon: "MePhone4 then lets out that the paint is explosive" (War De Guacamole). **Bot, Today's Challenge: Dodgeball!:** a 12-frame wind-up in which a big red ball (22 px round) swells in at their feet and a leg is drawn back, then they boot it along the floor at 7 px a frame; it bounces up off the floor (7 px a frame, gravity 0.4) and ricochets off every wall, block, ceiling and the sides of the stage, and goes flat on the third; every foe it touches takes 8% and is popped up (3 sideways, 11 up), once each (a ball can pass through several); it falls out of the world at a gap; a boss takes a plain 8, once; they need a floor and room for the ball. Canon: the Invitational's first challenge is "A Friendly Game of Dodgeball" (Breaking the Ice; Paintbrush kicks a ball and pops Balloon), Bot kicks "using their legs as weapons" and co-hosts with Test Tube. **Changed from the doc, and why:** Tapey's 0.5 s hold is the 12-frame startup rule plus 12 frames of tape and 20 of reel, and the 0.4 s stun and the stage-only target are mine (the doc gives 8% and "never offstage"); Silver Spoon's doc row gives no damage to a fighter and 8% to a boss, so the bag is 8% to everyone, and "like Outbreak's infected" is the engine's Outbreak exactly (including a free-for-all's rebound); Teddy Bear's "its shadow shows" is not drawn (the house rule: no marks where a hit will land; the grenade is seen in flight), and the doc's 160 px is the splat's radius; Tea Kettle's "aimed up or down" is steerable through the pour, and the heal (1.5% a splash) and the splash count are mine (the doc gives "up to 12%"); Bot's announcement is the ball and the leg, no words, and "three times" counts the floor, walls, ceilings and the stage sides, each ball hitting a foe once. Question 1 of the doc (cooldown of the long ones) is applied as everywhere: the purchase, the paint, the tape, the ball and the grenade hold the finisher while they last and the 74 frames count from when they are gone; question 2 does not touch these five. **Checks** (own jsdom scripts, scratchpad `fin-c2/`): syntax; boot (FFA and Teams clean); every finisher from the real keys (V and C key events on a person's fighter, Up and Down held after the press for the pour) with frames, damage, launch and reach printed; teammates never hit, held, bought, painted or rewound (the pour heals one); a hit in the startup stops each cleanly; a hit later (Tapey's reel snapped, the pour stopped; the bag, grenade and ball go on without her); the user KO'd mid-move by leaving the stage loses exactly one stock with nothing held, locked, bought or painted after (a bought foe is back on their own side in the same frame); a foe at the damage cap is KO'd once by each; floors and walls (Tapey's destination skips a place over a pit or inside a new block and ignores a foe off the stage; the stream stops at a wall and never reaches the foe behind it; the grenade stops at a wall and its splat does not reach through; the ball ricochets off a wall and falls into a pit; the bag bursts on a wall); in Boss Rush the boss is never bought, painted, rewound or held and takes plain hits; misses end cleanly (nobody in reach not spent, the foe leaving the tape fizzles, no floor or no room not spent); hold back in the wind-up turns the bag, grenade, ball and pour round; the CPU at Hard in 5 matches each: Silver Spoon 5 presses, Tapey 22, Tea Kettle 11, Teddy Bear 8, Bot 6; three CPU free-for-alls with batch members (5, 6 and 7 fighters) and two CPU teams matches clean: no errors, no bad positions, no double stock loss, every stock lost one KO, no team left switched; the in-process online pair (never the live relay) shows each the same on both screens (same phases, same percents) from the host's keys, and from a client's keys for Tapey, Silver Spoon and Bot; kit text and move card carry each finisher's name. The contact sheet is `smash-bal/reports/moves/fin-c22-sheet.png`. **Fixes after review (2026-11-02, same branch; same-scene before and after in `fin-c2/fix/`):** (5) **Tapey:** only the reel's end point was checked for safety, so a solid block that rose on the foe's recorded way, or where they were to land, was passed through (6 of the reel's 20 hold points were inside the block, and only the engine's own push-out kept the foe out of it; a hit on Tapey then freed them wherever the hold had put them). Now every point of the reel and the landing goes through the same wall test `finC2_safe` uses (`finC2_clear`, fxWallSpan): the tape catches on the block, the foe waits at the last clear place and lands there (the 8% and the stun still land), so a stall ends somewhere they can stand (see (9): never inside a block, and on a floor); a hit on her mid-reel frees them where the reel has them, on the recorded way, as before; with no block it is unchanged. (6) **Tea Kettle:** Up is the jump key, so Up in the frame of V + C (or a frame or two ahead of it) jumped her in the jump block before the finisher was read, and the pour never started (the finisher was not spent: it did nothing). Now that jump, if it is at most 2 frames old and from the floor (about 24 px), is taken back when the pour starts: she is put back on her floor with the jump unspent, and Up is the stream's aim as the card says; a jump 3 frames old or more stays a jump (no pour in the air, as before), V + C alone and Up alone are unchanged. **Checks of the six fixes** (own jsdom scripts, real V + C key events): syntax; boot (FFA and Teams clean); each fix on the same scene before and after (Poppy 6 of 13 became 13 on the boss, Clover 6 became 8, Magnet's other foe 77 to 85 frames rooted became 0 at every no-clash end, Tapey 2 to 6 hold points in a block became 0, Tea Kettle with Up in the press 0 pours became a pour with no jump); the six finishers' damage through applyHit against a fighter identical before and after (Clover 3, 3, 3, 3; Jack 12 and 18; Magnet 4, 6, 6; Poppy 3, 3, 3, 4; Tapey 8; Tea Kettle six 2s); a lockstep hash (FNV every 30 frames) of two same-seed CPU matches holding none of the six, MeTag, Silver Spoon, Teddy Bear and Bot, and Firey, Pen, Knife, Pin, Bubble and Blueberry, 3600 frames each, identical before and after (`b083830b`, `2b8cf3c`); the in-process online pair (never the live relay) shows both batches the same phases and percents on both screens, and a client's Tea Kettle with Up in the press frame pours on both; three CPU free-for-alls with the six (6, 7 and 5 fighters) clean: no errors, no bad positions, no double stock loss, nothing held or running at the end. **Second round of fixes (2026-11-02, same branch; same scene before and after, a copy of the pre-fix file as `before`, scripts in `fin-c2/fix2/`):** (7) **Tea Kettle at a ledge:** the taken-back jump of (6) put her back on the floor height but not where she was, and never asked whether there was floor under her: with Right held and Up two frames before V + C and the floor ending 2 px past her centre she was snapped to the floor height, the pour began, the next physics step found no floor and she fell about 30 frames inside the pour (locked out of any air jump, a stock lost over a pit; also 4 px with Up two frames ahead, and the step that follows the take-back carried her on another 2.7 px). Now the take-back happens only if the floor she left is still under her (`surfaceBelow` at her x within 4 px of her feet), and she is stopped where she is (vx 0: the pour holds her still anyway); with no floor under her the jump stands and the finisher is not spent, as before (6). Proved with the floor ending 2, 4 and 8 px past her centre, both facings, Up in the press frame and 1 and 2 frames ahead: before 3 of 9 per facing fell for 30 frames in the pour (312 px down); after none falls, and the one that cannot be on the floor (2 px, Up 2 frames ahead) is a plain jump with nothing spent; the open-floor scene (V + C, Up in the frame, Up 1, 2 and 4 frames ahead, Up alone, Up after) is identical before and after. (8) **Poppy against a boss (hardening):** a walker wrecked before the pop dealt 0 after the first fix; now each zap that no grace blocks lands as a plain 3% and the pop pays 13 less what landed (see Poppy's fix (2) above). Boss scene (a pinned boss with its own attacks held off; the boss's swing wrecks the walker through the engine's `finxSwingHit`), before / after: intact 13 / 13 (one hit of 13 at the pop / 3, 3 and 7); wrecked before the first zap (frames 12 to 18) 0 / 0; wrecked after the first zap (frames 19 to 50) 0 / 3; wrecked after the third (frames 51 to 66) 0 / 6; with the owner's own 3% landing on the boss at 20 different frames (each opens a grace) the walker's share is 13 every time, before and after (a zap a grace turns away is paid by the pop, for example 3 and 10), never more than 13. (9) **Tapey (hardening):** the reel's wait point `o.lg` was only wall-tested, not floor-tested: if the recorded way was a jump arc over a pit and a block rose on the arc, they were freed in the air at the last clear point and fell into the pit (a stock lost). Now a stall point must have a floor right under the foe's feet (`finC2_floored`, within 6 px), else the foe's own start spot (where the tape caught them); so "somewhere they can stand" means never inside a block and on a floor (a foe caught in the air is put back where the tape caught them; the destination of an unstalled reel is as before, the oldest safe place, with floor somewhere under it, and a hit on her mid-reel still frees them where the reel has them, which on a jump arc can be in the air). Pit-and-block scene: before 18 frames in the pit, a stock lost; after the foe lands on the floor at its start spot with the 8% and no stock lost; the plain reel and a reel stopped by a block on a floor walk are identical before and after. **Checks of the second round** (own jsdom scripts, real V + C key events): syntax; boot (FFA and Teams clean); the six finishers' damage through applyHit against a fighter identical to the pre-fix file (Clover 3, 3, 3, 3; Jack 12 and 18; Magnet 4, 6, 6; Poppy 3, 3, 3, 4; Tapey 8; Tea Kettle six 2s); a lockstep hash (FNV every 30 frames) of the two same-seed CPU matches that hold none of the three, 3600 frames each, unchanged (`b083830b`, `2b8cf3c`); one fall one stock (a fall in the scenes cost exactly one stock; the three CPU free-for-alls below have no double loss and every stock lost one KO); the in-process online pair (never the live relay) shows both screens the same phases and percents: a client's Tea Kettle at a ledge pours on the floor at 4 px and jumps with no pour at 2 px, a host's Tapey reels back over the pit with and without the block (the foe arrives with the 8% and no stock lost), a host's Poppy takes 13 off a boss intact and 3 when the boss's swing wrecks the walker after the first zap, the boss's hp the same on both; three CPU free-for-alls with the six (6, 7 and 5 fighters) clean: no errors, no bad positions, no double stock loss, nothing held or running at the end.
- **2026-11-02, a Final Smash that does not KO leaves the foe in the orange (the owner: "final smashes, if they dont kill you, should put you in the orange at MINIMUM"; branch `g-fs-floor`):** a foe a Final Smash HITS and who is still alive when its effect on them is over now ends on at least `FS_FLOOR_FRAC` (0.35, one constant) of their KO threshold -- the start of the orange in `pctColor` (Pen 87.5%, Tree 100.8%, Puffball 68.6%). Built once in the framework (`// ---- FS FLOOR: begin/end`, after `fsHitBy`), no row edited: `applyHit` calls `fsfNote` at the line the damage lands (a row's own frames, its start and end, and the shots it threw -- a shot keeps counting as the Final Smash's after it ended: Bow's last snowballs, Tennis Ball's balls), each Final Smash keeps its foes on its state (`st._fl`, with their KO stamp `_koN`), and `fsfStep` (called from `fsStep`) settles it once it is over for any reason -- its end, or its holder's KO (decided: the foes it had hit are still topped up, its effect on them had landed) -- and none of its shots is still on the way (`FS_FLOOR_WAIT`, 90 frames at most; a shot that lands later tops its foe up as it lands). Each recorded foe still on their feet is raised by `fsfTop`: only a flat % add (damage taken and dealt are kept too), no launch, no stun, no word, and an orange ring and sparks in `particles` (so online guests see them; the dice are the spot and the frame, never `Math.random`). A foe whose flight still carries them out past a blast line is waited for (`FS_FLOOR_FLIGHT`, 100 frames at most): KO'd, or topped up. Never touched: a teammate (friendly fire turns the hit away before it is recorded), the holder ("the user" of the Final Smash), a Boss Rush boss or add (not fighters; `BOSSRUSH.active` also stops it), a foe already above the line, a foe KO'd (`koSince`), a foe nothing of it hit. Two Final Smashes on one foe top up alone each. MEASURED (all 103 Final Smashes and the default, a still foe 60 px in front on Goiky Field, items off, the foe at 0%; the build before the floor, and the floor at 0 and at 0.35): Pen: 55 are KO'd (nothing to do), 48 leave him alive and 47 of those are raised (mean +45.2, most +66.7: Roboty), Dora already above; Tree: 18 KO'd, 85 alive, 84 raised (mean +56.0, most +80.0), Saw above; Balloon: 69 KO'd, 34 alive, 32 raised (Dora and Golf Ball above); Puffball: 78 KO'd, 25 alive, 23 raised (Bow and Golf Ball above). The old build and the floor at 0 agree on all 416 shots to the last decimal; every survivor ends at or above the line (no exception; every Final Smash lands a hit on Pen); nothing already above, KO'd or unhit moves (a foe 480 px out of reach: 62 Final Smashes hit nobody, the foe untouched). Also checked: a foe starting at 30, 60, 70, 95 and 150%; 2v2 Teams with a teammate in reach (103: never a teammate or the holder); Boss Rush (103: the boss and the fighter identical with the floor at 0 and 0.35, nothing recorded); the holder KO'd at the first hit (208 of 208 foes still topped up, nothing left waiting); the last stock (208: the last-KO slow-mo and sting identical in all three builds); the online in-process pair (host and guest show the same %, orange on both); two identical seeds give identical runs; 9 CPU free-for-alls and 5 CPU Teams matches with Final Smashes handed out (279 fired, 100 top-ups, none on a teammate, none under the line, no error). Not counted (known): shots of the fallback `FS_DEFAULT`'s own finisher (no fighter uses it: all 103 have a row). Syntax and boot clean, CRLF intact. CHECKED AND FIXED (the independent check, 2026-11-02; merged with live main e37de6b, so 108 Final Smash rows now, with Spongy, 8-Ball, Black Hole, Clock and Cloudy): (1) MUST-FIX, the Plays Camera: `FSF.pend` is a match-level global and was not in `pcSave`/`pcLoad`/`pcBase`, and `fsReset` cleared it in place, so with several matches stepping in turn through `pcLoad` a waiting entry was settled by another match's `fsfStep` (`fighters[t.idx] !== t` there), the foe was marked topped and never raised (21 of 56 pairs of matches gave a different foe % than the same match alone). Now `pcSave` carries `_fsf:FSF.pend`, `pcLoad` sets `FSF.pend = s._fsf`, `pcBase` starts it at `_fsf:[]`, and `fsReset` makes `FSF.pend = []` (never cleared in place: saved states hold the arrays). (2) One's ghost fighter (`_oneGhost`, a boss-phase fighter with a 70 HP bar, team -1) was not excluded (the guard only checked Boss Rush): `fsfNote` now returns early for `t._oneGhost` (and for `COBSFIGHT.active`, for safety). PROVEN: the pair test (8 pairs of Final Smashes, every ordered pair of two different matches interleaved through `pcSave`/`pcLoad` for 700 frames against the same match alone) gives 0 differences in 56 pairs, where it gave 21; the ghost takes the Final Smash's hits (its bar 70 -> 49.2) and is not topped up (0 top-ups, 20.8%) while the same hit on an ordinary foe is (1 top-up, 87.5%), and Steve Cobs's fight flag does the same (0 top-ups); a real Plays Camera session (`startTournament` spectate with the row On, `pcStart`, 1500 `pcFrame`s, 8 matches and their scouts, Final Smashes handed out inside it although the World Cup has none: 59 fired, 24 top-ups) ends with 0 warnings and every scout still with its match, where the same session without the fix loses scouts ("went apart" 4 times); the measurement over all rows re-run on the merged build (109 shots a foe: 108 Final Smashes and the default): the build before the floor and the floor at 0 agree on all 436 shots to the last decimal, and every survivor ends at or above the line -- Pen: 56 KO'd, 53 alive, 52 raised (mean +45.5, most +66.7; Dora above); Tree: 18 KO'd, 91 alive, 90 raised (mean +55.9, most +80.0; Saw above); Puffball: 81 KO'd, 28 alive, 26 raised (mean +35.9, most +47.8; Bow and Golf Ball above); Balloon: 72 KO'd, 37 alive, 35 raised (Dora and Golf Ball above); nothing already above, KO'd or unhit moves; the five new rows all land a hit and are raised like the rest; 3 more CPU free-for-alls with Final Smashes handed out (36 fired, 17 top-ups, none under the line, none on a teammate, no error); syntax, boot and CRLF clean. KNOWN, BY DESIGN: a status that ticks after the settle adds a few % above the floor (correct: 'at minimum'); the top-up is damage, so it changes what later launches do (a foe raised and then hit by a second Final Smash can die where he would have survived: by design); `fsKoFlight` looks only the hit-stun plus 4 frames ahead, so about a quarter of the raised foes who were at 0% are KO'd 10-60 frames later (they flash orange just before dying: by design, the owner should know); a foe KO'd by one hit, respawned and re-hit by a late piece of the same Final Smash is not topped up (rare); 'never the user' was read as never the HOLDER of the Final Smash (a human player hit by a CPU's Final Smash is topped up like any foe).
- **2026-11-02, FSC lane 1, batch 1 (Liy, Taco (II), Nickel): three Final Smash redesigns, each a staged set piece on the foe the catch holds (the owner, round three: "REDESIGN as pitched"; then "just dont make them TOO complicated; you can vary the damage and knockback as well"; branch `g-fsc-1`):** each is a catch, then a timed sequence of drawn props, each hit a direct blow on the held foe at its own landed size (KO-LEVEL's doubling, the holder's damage stat and Nickel's sarcasm divided out: `fsc1_hit`), the last one the launch the let-go throws. 100% on the held foe in all three; the foe held about 1.2 to 1.8 s; no words drawn. **Liy, Lights Out** (id kept): the catch is the box 520 across and 200 up or down ahead of her (nearest foe). She blitzes onto them (startup, 12 frames); the lights go out (the view dims to near black but for the two of them) and the cut hides her turning onto her wooden back and the switch's click; at row frame 26 they are high above the spot (up to 450 px, less on a short stage) falling together: boat 1 at frame 40 (24%), swung across and boat 2 at 54 (30%), the floor boat at 68 (46%, the launch: it bursts in planks, the lights blaze back on); 72 frames held. **Taco (II), The Arena of Death** (id and name the pitch's; the old Lemon Spit fan is gone, and its stale `lemonspit` entry in `FS_CATCH_KB` is left alone, nothing carries that id): the catch is a circle of 380 round her (nearest foe); she does not move. Plinths rise (frames 0 to 12), the foe is slid 280 px across to the one on her facing side (shorter where the floor ends, else the other side); three rounds of lob, gulp and spit-back, rocks 1, 2, 3 (frames 32, 52, 72: 10%, 14%, 18%); the foe is flung over the gap in a high arc (frames 72 to 86) and hangs over her (87 to 102) while a giant lemon swells in her cheeks; the lemon at 103 (58%, juice and rind, a launch up and away over the far edge); 107 frames held. **Nickel, Static Storm** (id and name the pitch's; the old Spare Change is gone): the catch is a circle of 500 round him (nearest foe); a storm front (dimmed edges, a cloud ceiling, rain), his balloons lift him 150 px and he drifts over the foe (a foe caught in the air is brought down to the floor first); ray one at 30 (14%, with an x-ray of the foe's bones), ray two at 46 (18%, along the floor too), the obelisk shoots up beside them (frames 48 to 60) and ray three at 64 bursts it (28%; a foe other than the held one within 90 px of it takes a ring of about 8%); the balloons pop and he drops onto the rubble at 82 (40%, the launch); 86 frames held. The flashes are soft blooms round the bolt, 16 frames apart. CHANGED FROM THE PITCHES, for the owner's "not TOO complicated": Liy has two boats and not three (24, 30, 46, as "Seasonal Shift" has two boats and then the slam), Taco (II) has three rounds and not four (10, 14, 18, 58 where the pitch had 8, 10, 12, 14, 56), and the launches are kb 9 / 10 / 14 (Liy / Taco / Nickel) so that the heaviest foe survives 0% as Bow's chairs do. KO (Goiky Field, from the foe's 0%, a CPU foe that does not steer): a light (Puffball) and a mid (Pen) foe are KO'd by all three; the heaviest (Tree) from 5% (Liy) and 15% (Taco (II)) on that day's 5% grid and at 0% ends on 100.8%; Nickel's heaviest was KO'd from 0% as built and was nerfed on 2026-11-02 to survive 0% and be KO'd from 10% (see the end of this row, where it is measured to 1%). Each row also carries `needFoe`, `exact` and `botOk`: Bow's framework change (`g-bow-fs`, 9b50a5e) reads them once it is in (a press with nobody in the catch is spent with no intro, a CPU presses only when the catch will land, leading a moving foe) and on this base they do nothing; without it a CPU on a nearly spent glow can still press with a foe near and none in the catch, and the row is then spent in a puff. A catch that took nobody is spent the same way. A KO of the foe (the cap, or any) or of the holder ends it where it is: the foe let go, the holder set down on firm ground (`fsc1_settle`); bodies moved by position are put down only on the floor where it has no gap or on a platform's top (`fsc1_firm`, `fsc1_stand`); bosses, adds and teammates are never caught. New shared helpers sit in one marked block, `// ---- FSC lane 1: begin / end` (names `fsc1_*`), before Nickel's row; each fighter's change is inside its own `FINAL_SMASHES` block. No wire fields added: the points a row keeps are `fsBx / fsBy / fsCx / fsCy`. Checks (2026-11-02, the scratchpad `fsc-1`, jsdom): every beat's frame and landed % printed and exact in 5 placements each (both facings, holder or foe in the air, a platform) and the held foe's total exactly 100; ledge cases (foe or holder on either ledge, foe over the gap) end on the floor; a miss, a foe stepping out of the catch, a second foe, a teammate and a boss; the user or the foe KO'd early and late and by the cap (no stuck lock, nothing thrown at whoever comes back); the CPU's trigger on this base and with Bow's framework change applied in memory; the in-process online pair draws the same frames on both screens (differences at most 1 px); no banner and no text drawn; three CPU free-for-alls with all three in them run clean; syntax, boot (FFA and Teams, no errors) and CRLF clean. The helpers `fsc1_switch` and `fsc1_cheeks` moved inside the FSC lane 1 block. **Round-2 re-check (same day, after the Lightning / Clover / Donut / Tree fixes):** nothing in these three rows or their helpers changed; re-run in a match on both facings: Liy beats 40 / 54 / 68 at 24 / 30 / 46%, Taco (II) 32 / 52 / 72 / 103 at 10 / 14 / 18 / 58%, Nickel 30 / 46 / 64 / 82 at 14 / 18 / 28 / 40%, each 100% on the held foe (100.8% with the orange floor on a survivor), held 72 / 107 / 86 frames, ends clean in the air and at both ledges, the Taco arena standing on firm ground when cast in the air, Nickel's foe dragged onto the rubble, Liy's dim 0.6 and big switch, the fizzle and the CPU's trigger as before; the in-process online pair draws the same frames on both screens for all three (no state or lock mismatches, no text). KO on Goiky Field from the foe's 0% (a CPU foe that does not steer), re-measured to 1%: a light (Puffball) and a mid (Pen) foe are KO'd by all three from 0%; the heaviest (Tree) survives 0% on 100.8% and is KO'd from 2% (Liy) and 14% (Taco (II)); **Nickel's heaviest foe: KO threshold, and the nerf (nerfed 2026-11-02 at the owner's word, "nerf nickel."; branch `g-fsc-1`):** history: the pitch and the first measurement had the heaviest foe (Tree) KO'd from about 10%; it changed the same day with the rubble drag to KO'd from 0% (the same at the commit before round 2; a smaller launch did not bring it back, even kb 10 KO'd it; the checker, round 2, measured it independently in five placements: with kb 14, 10, 8, 7 and 6 it was KO'd from 0% every time and only kb 5 survived 0%, KO'd from 3%), and round 2 accepted it as it was. THE NERF changes two things in his row and nothing else: the damage per hit (14 / 18 / 28 / 40, 100% in all), the frames, the launch's size (kb 14, ky -10), the catch, the fizzle and the CPU's trigger, the look, and every other fighter are as they were. 1) The stomp throws the foe toward the middle of the stage (`st.ld`: the side of `WW*0.5` from the pile; it was always away from where he started, so it threw them out over the nearest ledge whenever they stood off the middle, and a CPU foe that does not steer fell). 2) The drag onto the rubble is 24 px (`FSC1_NICK.drag`; it was the whole 80 px to the pile's middle), so the foe ends on the pile's near edge (the heap is about 110 px wide) and the launch, which now runs across the stage, starts from nearer the middle; where 24 px would end off firm ground (a foe at a wall or at a platform's edge, `fsc1_firm`) the drag goes the whole way as before, so a held foe still only ever stands on firm ground. WHY BOTH: measured with the knobs swept (the foe's 0%, bisected to 1%): the drag alone does nothing (even a drag of 0 left the heaviest KO'd from 0%: from 640 it is thrown over the near ledge), the direction alone gave 15% in the 360/640 placement (drag 80), direction with drag 0 gave 9%, with 24 gives 10% (the intended figure), with 30 gives 11% and with kb 15 and drag 30 gives 9%; kb is left alone. CHECKED AFTER: the beats at frames 30 / 46 / 64 / 82 for 14 / 18 / 28 / 40, 100.8% on the held foe (100% and the orange floor's 0.8), held 86 frames, both facings and the foe near, far, left and right, the foe and the holder on the ground and in the air (the foe set down on firm ground first), the holder KO'd at 5 / 40 / 90 and the foe KO'd at 20 / 60: every one ends clean (no lock, no freeze, both drawn); the ledge placements (the holder near either edge, the foe near either edge) run through and end on the stage; on every stage but Moon Caves at three spots each, the held foe never leaves the stage, it lands on firm ground, the drag is 24 px (79.7 where the foe stood at a wall) and the launch runs for the middle; the fizzle and the CPU's trigger as before; a four-fighter CPU free-for-all (a glow handed to him every 300 frames) ran 7 Final Smashes started and ended clean with no stray lock and no error; the in-process online pair (no relay) is identical to the commit before it (no state or lock mismatches, the guest draws exactly what the host draws, no text); syntax, boot (FFA and Teams) and CRLF clean. (The `10%` the first ledger row gave was on a 5% grid; the true figure with the cut was 6%.) **Lightning**: each landmark now stands on firm ground at the foe's own level (`fsc1_firm`), its x inside [90, WW - 90] (in effect [100, WW - 100]), at least 100 px from the foe and 130 (else 105) from the other landmark, and with firm ground 40 px to each side of its centre as well (checker, round 2: it is 100 px wide, and it hung over a pit or a platform's edge; before that rule 923 of the 2714 landmarks had firm ground missing within 40 px of the centre); one that does not fit on its side is mirrored to the other side (stepping in toward the foe 15 px at a time), then put on the floor or platform below, and left out (its bolt falls on the foe's side instead) only if nothing fits: over every stage but Moon Caves (10 stages, 1357 foe spots on the floor and on every static platform) 13 of 2714 landmark slots are left out (7 before the footprint rule: Yoyle 6, Grandplains 1, Skytower 3, Canyon 3) and none is off the stage, over a pit, hanging past firm ground, or within 100 px of the foe (on Goiky Field with the foe at the right the Oven now stands 110 px from him, not 162) (`fsc1_gkSpots(sx, base)` is a pure function of the foe's spot and the stage, so a guest draws the same); each is now seen through (`fsc1_seeThru(f, tg, x, hw, yTop, yBot)`: 0.4 alpha, easing in as any fighter other than the holder and the held foe comes within about 36 px of its box, so a jumper above the roof counts only when near it; - **2026-11-02, FSC lane 1, batch 3 (Tree): one more Final Smash redesign, a staged set piece on the foe the catch holds (the owner, round three: "REDESIGN as pitched"; then "just dont make them TOO complicated; you can vary the damage and knockback as well"; branch `g-fsc-1`):** **Tree, Cut Down to Size** (renamed: the old Timber, three dropped branches, shared its name with his own card's special; the id is `cutdown`). Canon (the Battle for Dream Island wiki, Tree page and Tree/Coverage): TPOT 8 four vines and giant, TPOT 9 six vines and larger, TPOT 10 eleven vines, tangled and reaching the clouds, TPOT 12 chopped down; in "What's Up Bell's String?" Fanny chops his roots while he winces and he falls on Marker. The catch is a circle of 420 round him, the nearest foe only (`needFoe` / `exact` / `botOk`: a human press with nobody there is spent in a puff, a CPU fires only when it can land). He is hidden from row frame 12 and the swelling tree is drawn from his feet with his own face on the trunk (a wreath of ten purple lumps for the crown, a hole 55 px in radius in it, so it never covers the foe). Five beats, six blows, 100% on the foe, 128 frames held (2.1 s): roots run along the floor under the foe and four vines wrap them, 10 (8%), they hang on the vines beside the trunk as it swells and lifts them a third of the way up; six vines, 30 (10%), two thirds up; eleven vines, 50 (14%), the crown punches up through a deck of white clouds and the foe slides into it, tangled; the chop, an axe in from off screen at his roots, 72 (7%) and 86 (9%), each shudders the trunk and whipsaws the foe, leaves rain down and he winces; the fall from 92, slow then quick, the foe at the top of the arc whipped down onto the floor where the crown lands, 124 (52%), a ring of dust, a big shake, then the tree fades and he stands where he was (148 frames in all). The crown is never higher than 420 px over his feet (the stage's top is 146 px clear), and the tree falls toward the middle onto firm ground at his level (`fsc1_firm`: the farthest firm spot 420 to 200 px out, else the other side, else the nearest firm top below), so it never falls into a pit or off the stage; cast in the air he is set down on the firm ground below first. Anyone else under the falling trunk takes one clip of 10% and anyone within 130 px of the landing a ring of 10% (a boss or an add is never caught; a teammate is never struck). CHANGED FROM THE PITCH: the camera is not moved (the crown stops at 420 px, which on Goiky Field is the top third of the view), the foe hangs beside the trunk during the first two lifts (the Final Smash drawing is over the bodies, so a trunk through them would hide them), the axe is a drawn axe only and Fanny is not drawn, and the launch is almost straight up and a little back toward him (kb -3, ky -22) because from where the crown lands an outward launch only throws a foe that does not steer off the ledge. A KO of the foe or of the holder ends it where it is (the foe let go, the holder set down on firm ground). Online (in-process pair, no relay): both screens agree on every wire field (`fsBx` the landing, `fsBy` its height, `fsCx` the crown's, `fsCy` his feet line), on the hiding and on the draw calls; the holder differs by under 0.5 px and the held foe by under 1 px. NOTE for later rows: a guest receives `fsC` as undefined when it is 0, so `hides(f)` must say `!(f.fsC > 0)` and not `f.fsC <= 0` (the first draft hid him on the host only). Three CPU free-for-alls (Smash Balls, a glow handed out now and then) ran clean with 23 Final Smashes started and caught, no stuck lock, no error; the tree was also run on all ten stages but Moon Caves. - **KO LINE HONEST (camera limit) (2026-11-03, branch `g-ko-honest`, built on ii-dlc `a2a7797`, NOT PUSHED):** the owner: "the place where you die when you fall should be honest-I keep dying when I dont hit the floor of the screen" (Goiky Field, every direction), and, when the first plan moved the KO lines to the starting view: "you should make it so that the camera LIMIT (not the init. camera) should be the ko line." The KO lines did not move (sides 120 px out from the stage's box plus `SIDE_ROOM`, top -260, bottom `WH` + 160); the default view did not change; the ledgeguard camera's LIMIT is now exactly that rectangle. Measured before, with a fighter held 3 px inside a line on Goiky Field (and Yoyleland, The Moon) at 1100 x 720: the view followed it out and showed 159 px past each side line, 148 under the bottom line (it died 115 px from the edge of the screen, 613 of 720 down) and at the top the view did not move at all, so the top line sat 102 px above the screen and it died 70 px off it; Pillars, Forest, Incinerator 143 past each side; the scrolling arenas 164 past the sides and 152 under the bottom. In a CPU brawl on Goiky Field the view went out to 0.53 and showed up to 215 px past the sides, 388 above and 257 under, and the player's KO came with the line 139 px inside the screen. NOW: (1) THE ONE PLACE: `KO_TOP`, `KO_SIDE`, `KO_DROP`, `koL`, `koR`, `koB`, `koRect`, `pastSideLine`, `pastKo` (next to `SIDE_ROOM`); `eliminate` (and the respawn ceiling), the Final Smash's flight test (`fsKoFlight`, and Marshmallow's gravity line there and in `step`), and the ledgeguard camera's `lgDoomed` read them, so they cannot drift (checked: 2,480,725 points over the 12 stages, `pastKo` against the old expression, 0 differences). The CPU's `nav` blast lines (bottom 100, `nav.M`), the CPU's -200 test and the Final Smash chair spots keep their own deliberate margins. (2) THE LIMIT, in `lgStepIn`: the zoom never goes under `lgKoZoom()`, the zoom at which the view just fits inside the KO rectangle (its tighter side: 0.66 on the edge stages at 1100 x 720, where the width binds, so a view can never show past a line on the other side either; a huge arena's is 0.19 to 0.32, but a recoverer's pull still stops at `LG.zmin` 0.50 there: `lgFloor()` = the larger), and the view's centre is clamped (`lgClampR`) so the view stays inside the rectangle: nothing past a line is ever drawn, on any side. The default view lies inside the rectangle on every stage (the line is 25 to 143 px outside the screen at the sides and bottom, 102 to 1,105 above it), so the clamp changes nothing until a fighter is near a line. (3) THE PULL: a fighter within `LG.koM` 140 px of a line (until `LG.koM` + `LG.koH` 50), or in hitstun on a flight that ends past one (`lgKoFlight`: gravity and fall speed as the sim has them, the floor stops it, at most `LG.ko` 90 frames), is THREATENED whatever else it is doing (`LGC.f[].kt`, the lines as bits 1 left, 2 right, 4 top, 8 bottom; not `_backAir` at the top, not held or in a Final Smash): it is a plan candidate at once (no `LG.enter` wait), its group reaches out to the line it threatens, every rectangle of the plan is clipped to the KO rectangle (`lgClipK`, `lgKoGroup`), and the view eases in over `LG.koF` 8 frames (24 for an ordinary recoverer) with the usual zoom-rate limit, hysteresis, far-recoverer rule, hold, markers and up arrow untouched. The line comes to the screen's very edge as the fighter nears it. PROOF (jsdom, a fighter in hitstun flung at each line, on Goiky Field, Yoyleland, Canyon and Sky Tower; the picture at the last frame before the KO): before, the line was 18 to 72 px off the screen (up to 788 px at Canyon's right, 174 at Sky Tower's top); after, the KO line is at the screen's edge (0.0 px) in all 16 scenes and the fighter within one frame of travel of it (3 to 29 px in from the edge); 0 zoom reversals on the way in (one on the way back out after the KO, the ease out); the view never reaches past any line (max 0 on the side flown at). In CPU brawls on Goiky Field (4 CPUs, 2,400 steps, 2 seeds) the player's KO is 17 px from the edge (was 139), the view's dead space is 0 (was up to 388), the zoom reversals fell 5 to 3 and 3 to 1, the plan episodes are the same; on Canyon (1,500 steps, 2 seeds) the zoom, centre and plan are identical to ii-dlc. Teams (one 3,731 x 1,890 arena): every side 0. Unchanged: the default view (centre and zoom, 16 scenes: difference 0), and the cameras of Capture the Flag, Boss Rush and Training (240 steps each: identical to ii-dlc; they never enter `lgStep`'s plan). Online: the camera is local; the client builds it from its own snapshot through the same functions, no field is added to the wire. The bottom line of Pillars, Forest, Incinerator Big Incinerator and Fortress is unreachable (a solid floor), so no pull is needed there. A fighter back from a KO is no top-line threat (the respawn ceiling), so respawning does not move the view.
- **2026-11-02, FSC lane 3 batch 3b3: Tea Kettle's Final Smash rebuilt as a staged set piece (the owner picked the pitch, "Tree, Cake, Tea Kettle: REDESIGN as pitched"; "just dont make them TOO complicated; you can vary the damage and knockback as well"; branch `g-fsc-3`):** MAMA'S GOTCHA! (id `mamasgotcha` kept; the pitch does not rename it; the old hug and five squeezes is gone) is a dinner for one served to the nearest foe, then the boil-over. Bow's chairs' model: the catch is a circle of 420 px round her, nearest foe only (`catchMax` 1), `needFoe` (a human's press with nobody the catch can take, or none that has floor for a chair and a table, is spent at once: a small puff, no intro, no pause), `exact` + `botOk` (a CPU fires only when the catch will take someone, a foe clearly inside it led 10 frames), `catchOk` (the chair and the table need a floor), a foe caught in the air is set down in the chair, and she lands at the foot of the table if she was in the air (the nearest floor below her). FIVE BEATS, 150 frames after the catch, 100% on the held foe in 7 hits: (1) the foe is pulled in about 116 px in front of her (10 frames, never through a solid block) in a firm hug, 6% (frame 10: two drawn arms wrap them, hearts), set in a chair while a round table pops up between them, a tablecloth snaps over it, a candle is lit, a napkin is tucked in, and her tray skids along the floor to the table; (2) three mouthfuls 10 frames apart, 9% each = 27% (frames 38, 48, 58: canapes fly in from the tray, the cheeks bulge, the head jerks back each time); (3) the pour, a long scalding stream of tea from her spout over their head, steam rolling off, 14% (frame 68), then she heaves up a whole tureen of her famous soup and upends it on them, 12% (frame 88), running total 59%; (4) REFUSED, no damage (frames 96-120): she turns red, the screen tints, steam climbs the screen, she and the table rattle harder; (5) the boil over, 41%, the launch (frame 120, kb 15 up 17, KO-level through `fsKbMult`): the lid blows off like a cork, she stomps the floor (a shock ring), a geyser of steam under the table throws the foe up and out, hard. Every blow but the last stuns briefly, so the foe stays seated; the last is no link of a chain. The props are scenery and the pitch says nobody else is hurt, so there is no ring: a second foe beside the table takes nothing, a boss, an add or a teammate is never caught. No letters on screen, nothing named on screen; everything that appears grows in over 12 frames. CANON (the Inanimate Insanity wiki, from the pitch's read): her page (Oversteam from spout and lid; the official bio's warning that crossing her gets you a mouthful, sometimes literally of food; she pours tea for the other team); "The Shame of the Name" (she insists on her hors d'oeuvres, turns red and steams when they are refused, throws the food down, stomps The Floor to make it get lost); "Fan the Flames" (she is ready to make her famous soup); her kit (the tray special, Blow Her Lid, the Get Lost stomp). The table, tureen and geyser are the game's own. CHANGED FROM THE PITCH: the pitch's seven beats (hug and seat, hors d'oeuvres, pour, soup, refused, boil over) are five (the pour and the soup share one beat, as two hits) for the owner's 'not TOO complicated'; the foe is set 116 px (the pitch's 110, with 96 and 76 as the fallbacks when there is no room); the chair stands behind the foe and the table between them, drawn over the fighters (so the foe stays visible). Numbers on the wire are the existing `fsAx .. fsCx` (her place at the table, the foe's place in the chair, their size); a guest draws from `fsT`, `fsLk` and those. No framework line and no shared helper was touched: the row, its constants (`FSC3_TEA`), `fsc3_teaRat`, `fsc3_teaPlan`, `fsc3_teaDraw` and the card text (the comment above the row) only, built on the lane's helper block. KO from 0% on Goiky Field (the foe ends the hits on 100%, then the launch): Puffball (cap 196) KO from 0, 15 and 50, not from 30; Pen (cap 250) not from 0, KO from 15, 30 and 50; Tree (cap 288) not from 0 or 15, KO from 30 and 50. Checks (2026-11-02, the scratchpad `fsc-3b3`, jsdom, in-process only): the sequence from ground (left and right facing), from the air (she drops onto the platform and seats them there), foe in the air, foe on top of her, foe at the reach edge, foe on the left: every hit frame [10, 38, 48, 58, 68, 88, 120] and damage [6, 9, 9, 9, 14, 12, 41] as designed, total 100.00%, lock gone, draw clean through a validating canvas (0 faults); ledge, platform and pit cases (Goiky ledges both sides, a platform edge, Stone Pillars over the gap, a foe below the stage): the chair always stands on a floor, she ends standing, she kept every stock; the foe KO'd by the first blow, KO'd between blows, the holder KO'd early and late, a second foe, a teammate, a boss (no hp lost), a miss (spent in 6 steps, no intro), a foe invulnerable at the catch (not taken): all end clean with no stuck lock; the CPU cue (fires into a foe 0.6 reach away, never one out of reach); the online pair (host and client agree on id, fsT within 1 frame, lock, damage; the client draws all 150 frames with no fault); three CPU free-for-alls (Goiky with Pen, Bow and Tree; Stone Pillars with the same; Goiky with a second Tea Kettle, Puffball, Pencil and Tree): 8 casts each, all caught, no error, no NaN, no stuck lock, intros at most 30 frames; syntax, boot (0 bad positions, no error), CRLF, contact sheet `reports/moves/fsc-3b3-sheet.png`. Test thresholds adapted and not failures: the generic p3 'second foe takes it once' now reads 'takes nothing' (no ring by design), and the online 'draws more than 150 frames' reads 'more than the row's length less 5' (150 of 150 frames drawn, 0 faults). ROUND 2 (2026-11-02, same batch, the sheet's review: 'the REFUSED beat does not read as she turns red, the screen tints: at frame 110 the tint is a grey-lilac wash over the sky, only a small pink halo marks her and her body stays pale'): only the REFUSED block of `fsc3_teaDraw`, a new helper `fsc3_tintBody` in the lane's block and the card text changed; no number moved (7 hits [6, 9, 9, 9, 14, 12, 41] = 100%, frames [10, 38, 48, 58, 68, 88, 120], 150 in all). CHECKS: the seven placements (k1, 63 checks: ground both facings, air, foe in the air, foe on top, foe near the reach edge) hit frames and damage as designed, total 100.00%, draw clean over 150 / 195 frames through the validating canvas (0 faults); the tint through the validating canvas with the picture loaded (the test profile's own skin, then the plain render, then a skin whose picture has not decoded) and with no picture at all: laid on every drawn frame of the refused beat (none before 96, none after the blow's 4-frame fade; the stand-in with no picture), 0 faults, 0 throws; the platform, ledge and pit placements (31 checks); interruptions (49 checks); the CPU cue (5); the fizzle (13); the in-process online pair (5, the client draws all 150 frames with no fault); KO from 0% on Goiky Field (the foe a CPU from the let-go): Puffball from 0, 10, 30+ (not 20), Pen from 10 (not 0), Tree from 20 (not 0 or 10); the three CPU free-for-alls listed in the Coiny row (8 casts each, all caught).
