No behaviour changed.- **FIN lane c1, 2026-11-02, batch c11: Nickel (II), Test Tube, Saw, Fan and Soap get their own finishers (the owner, "finish the finishers"; design `smash-bal/reports/moves/finisher-ideas-v2.md`; branch `g-fin-c1`, built on ii-dlc `e37de6b`; NOT PUSHED):** each had the shared swing because its idea waited for a piece of the engine; each now has its own, with the nearest honest version of that piece. All five follow the finishers' rules (FIN_STARTUP 12 frames of the fighter winding up, nothing drawn where it will land, what a move puts out grows in over FIN_GROW 12 frames, one at a time with the cooldown from when it is gone, a boss takes a plain hit and is never roasted, shrunk, ridden or cut, foes only, no words). **Nickel (II), Roasted Live on Air:** a muttered roast (12 frames, a bubble of dots), then a shin kick (52 px past his front, 5 frames live): 7%, a small shove; the nearest foe it hits is roasted for 5 s and each attack of theirs that touches nothing costs them 3% with a laugh track (laughing faces over them, a canned laugh). THE PIECE (damage when an attack misses): an attack is a run of live hit areas of theirs -- the engine's own hit art -- plus the shots they fire in it; it is over when none has been live for 8 frames and no shot of it still flies (150 frames at most) and it touched nothing if they dealt no damage from its first frame to its last (a grab they hold counts); moves that put out no hit area are not attacks. THE HONEST VERSION (stated 2026-11-02): only damage dealt to a fighter counts as touching something (`_dmgDealt` is fed by applyHit and holdDamage alone), so a foe's attack that lands only on a boss, a summon or an item counts as a miss and costs the roasted foe 3% (measured: a jab at nothing cost them 3%, a jab on a fighter 0, a jab that took 8 off a boss cost them 3%). **Test Tube, Shrinking Formula:** shakes a flask (12 frames) and lobs it in a slow arc (24 to 48 frames, 380 px range, 230 px out with no foe; hold back to lob it behind her); it bursts on the first foe, the floor or a ledge, or a wall: a 50 px splash, 6%, and every foe in it shrinks to 0.7 over 12 frames for 4 s (setFighterSize, feet where they were): their hits land 0.75 as hard, the hits they take launch 1.3 as far (applyHit, `_shrunkT`); their body and moves' reach are smaller too (a jab's reach measured 80 -> 72, 64 -> 60 and 76 -> 72 px centre to centre: about a tenth, the size piece's own scaling). A foe with a size of its own is splashed but not resized; the size is put back at once when the shrink's row is gone (a KO of either), except under that foe's own Grow, whose size stays through it (a KO of the thrower leaves her big until her Grow ends; see the follow-up below). **Test Tube, follow-up 2026-11-02 (a foe's own size change):** a Puffball shrunk by the flask who then cast Grow was resized by both (measured: 0.7 for the 12 frames of her breath, 1.04 to 1.5 as the Grow swelled her, then 0.7 again for the rest of the big time while the formula held, size 1 for the rest of it once the formula ended -- its end put her back to 1 with the Grow still on -- and a pop to 1.46 when the Grow shrank her back). Now `fxShrinkStep` leaves a foe's size alone while their own Grow lasts (it skips setFighterSize when `_growSz` is set or a 'grow' row of theirs is live) and `finC1_unshrink` never forces size 1 over a Grow's (`_growSz`); the weaker hits and the 1.3 launch keep their whole time (a big and shrunk Puffball lands a 10 as 9.75 = 10 x 1.3 x 0.75; only big, 13; only shrunk, 7.5). After: 1.5 from the end of the swelling to the start of the shrinking back, then 1; a KO of the thrower leaves her big until her Grow ends, her own KO puts her back to 1; a plain shrink is number for number what it was. Two cosmetic leftovers, left as they are (Puffball code, unchanged): the Grow's swell still starts from 1.04, so a shrunk Puffball goes 0.7 to 1.04 in one frame at the swell start; and a Grow cast 240 to 251 frames after the splash freezes the formula's size mid-ramp for the 12-frame breath, then jumps to 1.0 (cosmetic, one frame). One rare edge: a second splash that restarts the formula's clock cannot be reached in play (the finisher waits for the formula, and a flask on an already-big Puffball only splashes); forced by script it snaps 1 to 0.7 for one frame. Checks (scratchpad `fin-c1/g1_grow.mjs`, `g2_card.mjs`, `g4_net.mjs`, `g5_cpu.mjs`, `d1_hash.mjs`): the same six scenes before and after (the grow cast mid-shrink, late in it, grow live first, the thrower KO'd, Puffball KO'd, no Grow) and the second-splash edge after; the flask's card in numbers through applyHit (6% splash, 0.7 size, 0.75 damage, 1.3 launch); the lockstep hash of two matches without Test Tube identical before and after (and two with her); the in-process online pair equal on every frame (rows and sizes, with and without the second splash); three Hard CPU free-for-alls with forced overlaps: her size was the Grow's on every frame (the build before: 131 to 497 frames off per match). **Saw, Cut a Hole in the Ledge:** sparks at her blade (12 frames), then on a floating ledge (a thin one-way ledge of the map; not a solid block, the floor, a timed piece or Capture the Flag / Moon Caves) a gap 140 px wide opens ahead of her, starting 40 px out (the other way if there is no room) -- 40 px ahead of her, not under her, because a hole under her feet would drop her (measured 2026-11-02: as built her gap runs 40 to 180 px in front of her and she stays on the ledge; with the same gap centred on her she fell 154 px through it), widening from its middle over 12 frames, open 3 s, closing over 12 (`finC1_holeSync` really splits the ledge in worldPlats and puts it back whole; a guest gets the cut map with the host's geometry sync, `worldGeo`); a foe who falls through takes 6%, once each; the gap is terrain, so a teammate in it falls too (no damage). On solid ground a groove opens along the floor ahead (170 px, 12 frames, 3 s): a foe who steps on it trips, 8%, knocked down. **Fan, Number One Fan Won't Let Go:** a crouch (12), then he is flown along an arc at the nearest foe within 200 px (12 to 20 frames; a hop 110 px ahead with nobody near) and clings to the first foe his body touches for 2 s, untouchable himself: they are slowed, cannot smash and take 2% every 15 frames (8 squeals, 16% at most); three real jumps of theirs shake him off (a CPU foe taps jump by level), as does the end of the 2 s (a hop back, 14 frames); a hard launch of the foe, a fall off the stage or a blast line lets him go toward the middle, so a foe's KO never takes his stock; a leap that touches nobody lands him flat (22 frames). **Soap, Germs OUT! Sterile Blast:** a hiss (12 frames, mist round her), then a ring of disinfectant grows over 12 frames to 220 px, hitting each foe as its front passes, once: 9%, thrown radially out (9 to 14 px a frame of launch, harder the closer, with a lift), never through a wall; at the burst she and her teammates inside shed burn, poison, bleed and slow (and a soaking, which is a slow). **From the doc:** the 0.3 and 0.4 s tells are FIN_STARTUP (12 frames); the doc's Test Tube and Saw have no damage and got 6% (the splash; the fall through the gap), as Lifering's and Goo's did; Fan's squeal rate (15 frames) is mine; the doc's "sparks warn first" is the sparks at her blade only (no line). The two questions: one at a time, cooldown from when it ends (all five). **Hooks outside the five's own rows (one line each):** the CPU chain (`finC1Want` after `finEventWant`), applyHit (the shrink's damage and launch), the input mask (Fan's no smash and a CPU foe's jump), the step (`finC1_holeSync`), and the five `finisher:` lines of the kit text (the move card and the all-moves list read them); the rest is in the marked block `// ---- FIN lane c1: begin/end` and the batch block above batch 8's. Checks (scratchpad `fin-c1/`, jsdom, no browser): syntax, boot (FFA and Teams clean), CRLF intact; each from the real keys (V+C) with its frames and numbers printed; foes only (a teammate beside the target untouched); a boss takes the plain hit; a miss ends cleanly; 162 interruptions (the user hit or KO'd, or the foe KO'd, at nine moments of each of the six forms) left no row, lock, hold, size, slow or cut ledge and cost exactly the one stock; a foe KO'd by each finisher's own damage lost one stock; five Hard CPU free-for-alls (Fan 33, Soap 36, Test Tube 10, Nickel (II) 10, Saw 9 grooves, and 3 holes in 4000 frames on a ledge) ran clean (no bad positions, sizes or leftover cuts); the in-process online pair shows every row the same on both screens on every frame (and the cut ledge, through the geometry sync); the contact sheet is `smash-bal/reports/moves/fin-c11-sheet.png` (drawn by the game's own draw() on a software canvas).
- **FIN lane c1, 2026-11-02, batch c12: Tissues, Yin-Yang, Starfruit, Blueberry and Cherries get their own finishers (the owner, "finish the finishers"; design `smash-bal/reports/moves/finisher-ideas-v2.md`; branch `g-fin-c1`, on batch c11 `4a3a39b` on ii-dlc `e37de6b`; NOT PUSHED):** each had the shared swing; each now has its own, from the doc's idea, grounded in the II wiki where it rests on the fighter (Tissues' condiShAWn that does not infect everyone, Yin's "intent is to balance out Yang", Starfruit the lead singer of Spoiled Lemon, Blueberry's Super Strength, the Cherries "connected at the stem"). All five follow the finishers' rules (FIN_STARTUP 12 frames of the fighter winding up, nothing drawn where it will land and no shadow, line or word, what a move puts out grows in over FIN_GROW 12 frames, one at a time with the cooldown from when it is gone, a boss takes a plain hit and is never grabbed, balanced, boulder'd or clotheslined, foes only, a wall stops what passes it). **Tissues, Achoo, Borrowed Hanky:** a hanky comes out (12 frames), then a grab (24 px disc, 14 px past his front, clear sight, never through a wall) on the nearest foe in front; he honks his nose into them for 18 frames (planted, they are held where they are, pulled in at most to his front), then 9% and a small shove; slimed, they are slowed 120 frames (SM_FX.slow) and ALWAYS catch his next CondiShAWn (the special's own chance, about one in two, is skipped for them: `finC12_hankyUse` in `case "condishawn"`), for as long as the goo shows and at most 6 s; the finisher waits through the first 2 s of the slime. the nearest foe it touches (never through a wall) is the one: both fighters' damage meets in the middle, each shifting by half the gap and at most 15% (a foe with less takes it and he gets it back, a foe with more is healed and he takes it, equal: only the shove of 3); a boss takes a plain 10 and no shift. **Starfruit, Mic Drop on a Screaming Amp:** the mic is raised (12 frames) and dropped onto a stage amp (46 x 58 px) that grows up out of the floor 64 px ahead over 12 frames (the arrow turns it behind him; it fits itself on one floor or does not come, and the finisher is then not spent); whole, it stands 240 frames and 20 frames in, then every 60 (four times), screams a pushing soundwave along the ground BOTH ways (9 px a frame, up to 140 px out, 46 px tall so a jump clears it, a wall stops it): 6% to each foe it meets, once a wave, shoved 7; a boss in a wave takes a plain 6; three foe hits on the amp (a swing counts once, a shot once) knock it over (his own never). **Blueberry, Big Rock, Little Effort:** a boulder grows in his hands over his head (12 frames) and is lobbed in an arc (22 to 44 frames, at the nearest foe ahead within 300 px in clear sight else 190 px out; the arrow turns it behind him): a falling thing that hits while it flies, 12% (12.5 with his 1.04) and a knockdown to every foe it touches once, a boss a plain 12, a wall drops it straight down; where it lands it stands 360 frames, 64 x 52 px, its top (the part with floor under it) a one-way platform anyone can stand on (none on Moon Caves); his next hit that reaches it (any live hit area of his, read from HIT_ART, six frames after it lands; a foe's never) rolls it away from him, 8 px a frame, up to 170 px, 8% and a shove to each foe it meets once, stopped by a wall or the edge of its floor, then it crumbles; expired it crumbles too. **Cherries, Stem-Stretch Clothesline:** a crouch (12 frames), then one cherry hops off along the floor, 250 px or as far as the floor goes (14 px short of a wall or a drop; at least 90 px or the other way, else not thrown and not spent), the stem paying out behind it over 24 frames (harmless); then for 180 frames the stem is TIGHT from them to the far cherry at chest height (a 7 px line, 26 px above the floor): a foe it touches, from either end or in it when it snaps tight, is clotheslined once each, 7% and knocked down (30 frames), thrown out from the middle; a foe above it (a jump along the whole span) is over it, one who falls through it is not; a boss a plain 7; the near end follows them and the stem lets go (the far cherry hops home, 14 frames) if they get within 60 px of the far cherry or draw it past 340 px. **From the doc, and what is mine:** the 0.3 to 0.5 s tells are FIN_STARTUP; "slow glowing palm" is the 14-frame push; Blueberry's "shadow first" is not drawn (NO TELLS), nor is any mark where a thing will land; the boulder's roll is the doc's "rolls it away" and its 8% and 170 px are mine, as is its platform being a one-way top (Book's castle's way) not a solid block; Starfruit's waves running both ways, the first at +20 frames and four in all are mine (the doc: every second for 4 s); the amp is knocked over by foe hits only; Cherries' near end following them, the 60 and 340 px limits and "once each" are mine; Tissues' mark lasting as long as the goo shows (6 s at most) is mine (the doc: "his next CondiShAWn"); Yin-Yang's equal-damage case and the healing of a foe with more are the doc's "meets in the middle" read both ways. Nothing needed an engine piece too large, so no nearest-honest-version was needed. - **2026-11-02, Bow slams the chairs herself (the owner, seeing the chairs swing in on their own: "bow herself should actually slam the chairs onto them."; branch `g-bow-slam`):** Bow's Final Smash (SNOW BOW, `snowbow`; the card text, the reach, the 12.5% a chair, 100% in all and the launch values are untouched) is now done by Bow. THE SEQUENCE (row frames): 0-13 she gathers (the startup) and comes to straight above the foe, a chair of hers glowing and growing in over her head; 14-17 she slams it down onto them: the cage, no damage, a squash and dust at 17 (the cage is a little smaller, 3.7 in place of 4.3, so her own place above them is clear of it); 18-25 THE LIFT: the cage takes the foe up to 220 px above the floor or platform they stood over (a foe already part-way up is lifted the rest; one with room below is not moved), in 8 frames, slowing as it gets there, and she rides up with it; then a quick dash round to the lower left (5 frames, an afterimage of her shape behind her, no lines), and EIGHT blows 14 frames apart, the first on frame 39 and the eighth on 137: after each she recoils (2 frames), dashes to the next place round the foe (3), winds the chair up over her head (6: her arms go up with it, the body is the tell) and swings it down through them (3), the blow landing on the last frame of the swing, with the flash and spokes of the old chairs (a bigger one, and a ring, on the eighth). She stands 128 px from the foe's middle (`fsChairD`: 114 x their size + 14, so the cage's top is always clear of her), her feet toward them: above them she is upright, below them upside down, at the sides turned to match, so round the foe she turns -135, -90, -45, 0, 45, 90, 135, 180 degrees (printed per blow, all within a degree). The eighth is from straight below, slamming up: the launch, let go three frames on as before. Then she dashes home (8 frames, turning upright on the way) to exactly where she began, on its surface or in its air as she started, facing as she did: 148 frames in all (was 140). HOW SHE IS DRAWN: her whole picture (the loop's own `drawFighter`) is turned round her middle by the row (`hides` keeps the loop from drawing her meanwhile; the row draws her aura, her body, her chair, the cage, the afterimage and the cracks, `fsChairDrawMove`); `drawFighter` turns by `FS_TURN` (a global the row sets round the call) and keeps her 'you' triangle, her name tag and her status icons upright over her. Her arm is the rig's 'chop' (straight up, then straight down at the foe in her own frame: the chair's own path), driven from the beat so that it points the way the chair goes (`fsChairArmAnim`): it rises over the six frames of the wind-up, is straight up as the swing starts and lands as the blow does (the swing timer is set from the row frame every frame, a frame high, because the loop counts it down once more before she is drawn). WHERE SHE STANDS is a pure function of the frame, the foe's spot, where she began and her plan (`fsChairPose`): the host puts her there each frame (a hold that cancels the step's own gravity and one-way catches, `fsChairHold`); a guest works out her turn, chair and afterimage from the same numbers on the wire. Nothing was added to the snapshot: the foe's spot and size ride in `fsBx`, `fsBy`, `fsCx` as before (`fsBx`/`fsBy` are where the lift left them) and her plan rides in the otherwise unused `fsCy` (`fsChairCode`: whether her way to them and her way home are blocked, the place she begins at, and her angle at each of the eight blows as eight steps, 37 bits in all; 0 or nothing: the still version). NO ROOM (her places are never in a solid block, past a KO line (100 px short of it) or in the floor; `fsChairPlan`, at the catch): the lift stops at a solid block over the foe or at the top of the world (a foe on a platform near the top is lifted 136, not 220, and she never goes above y -140); an angle she cannot stand at is skipped and she slams from the nearest one she can reach, going round the way that is clear (a low solid ceiling over the floor with her in the tunnel: lift 8, and she slams from the left, left, upper left, above, upper right, right, right, right: the eighth from the side, the same launch); with no room above them she begins at the free place nearest to above and the cage does not lift them. A BLOCKED WAY IS NEVER A REASON TO STAND STILL (the check's first catch: a near-miss of a pillar's edge, or a block between her and the foe, used to fall back to the still version: 3-4% of random placements overall, 10-12% on Pillars and Yoyleland, Canyon and Fortress too): if the straight way from where she stands to her first place is blocked (she is on top of a block and they are under it) she BLINKS there on row frame 6, a puff where she was and where she is (`blinkIn`), and if the way home is blocked she blinks home (`blink`); a way counts as blocked only when it crosses a solid or is tighter to one than where she started, so beside a pillar a way along it is clear and she flies. The still version (`fsChairDrawStill`) is left for Moon Caves (frames of its own: as for Pencil's knock back, `fsPencilRoom`, she is not moved by position there, and nothing in `mc*` was touched) and for a foe with not one free place round them (none in about 2,700 random placements). On Moon Caves in the scene tried (Bow 220 px over the surface, the foe on it, at the top of the disc) it ran exactly as ii-dlc does, frame for frame (her place, the foe's, the let-go: 12884, 437 at speed 41 on both builds), the blows on frames 39 + 14 i against 28 + 14 i. INTERRUPTS end it on the frame and put her back where she began at once (upright, facing as she did, dropping cleared): the foe KO'd by a chair (the damage cap), KO'd by something else, KO'd during the lift, their last stock, the launch that ends a match, a third fighter's Final Smash going off mid-sequence (the foe stays held), the held foe a Smash Ball holder; Bow KO'd by a third party ends it with nothing drawn and the foe let go, in the air, to fall; a second foe standing in one of her places, a teammate below her and a boss are never held or hit; no lock outlives it; one stock, never two. PROOF (jsdom, the real loop; scripts of the session, no suite): the sequence start to finish on the ground, on a platform and in the air, both facings, against Puffball (light), Pen (mid) and Tree (heavy): 18 runs, the eight blows exactly 12.5% each, on frames 39, 53, 67, 81, 95, 109, 123, 137, 100% in all, her angle and distance at each blow as above (distance 128 every time), her body turned to match and the chair at the foe on each blow (sampled from the row's own draw calls through a recording canvas that tracks the transform), the lift 220 (0 where there was room), she ends within 1.5 px of where she began, on the ground or not as she began, facing as she did; the in-process online pair (no relay): all 147 row frames drawn the same on both screens (her place within 0.7 px, her turn 0.00 degrees, the chairs within 0.7 px, the same afterimage frames, the same wire fields, the guest sees the foe lifted 220); the CPU still uses it (it fires with a foe clearly below and the catch holds them; the decision table is unchanged: nothing fires at the same level, above, at the edge of the reach, with the foe running out or jumping up; a press that finds nobody is swallowed and the glow kept) and three CPU free-for-alls with Bow (Bow/Pen/Rocky, Bow/Firey/Tree/Puffball, Bow/Leafy/Needle) ran clean to the end of the match with no error and nothing stuck (16 of 17 casts caught their foe; one found nobody when the catch landed, a foe who had stepped out of it in its 10 frames); Teams, the other stages, the draw through the real `draw()` with the real `drawFighter`. AGAINST ii-dlc, the same scenes: the DAMAGE is the same (8 x 12.5); the LAUNCH is the same to the digit (18 scenes, three foes at 0, 40 and 80%, with room and on the floor: the foe's speed on the first free frame, e.g. Pen at 0%: -8.91, -42.21, 23 frames of stun, on both builds; only the height they are launched from differs for a foe the cage lifted: 220 px higher); the KO THRESHOLDS (the lowest damage before it at which the eighth blow takes the stock, on Goiky Field, Pillars, Sky Tower and Yoyleland, Puffball, Pen and Tree, foe on the floor) are the same in every case but one: a standing Tree on Goiky Field and Pillars now dies from 0% (before, from 10%): every standing foe is launched from the cage's height, and even the least lift that gives her room (126 px) does it, so this cannot be kept without launching them from the floor; the light and mid-weight foes already died from 0%, a foe with room and the big Sky Tower and Yoyleland scenes are exactly as before. A change the owner may want to know: the whole Final Smash is 8 frames longer (148, not 140; the first blow on 39, not 28, for the lift and the dash). AFTER THE CHECK (the same day; the checker's three should-fixes, all done): (1) THE STILL VERSION IS NO LONGER THE FALLBACK for a blocked way (above): proof, a plan-only fuzz of 2,667 random placements (the foe below her in reach) over ten stages, two seeds: 0 still versions (the build before, on the same 955 seeded placements over the 11 stages: 44 still versions, 4.6%: Pillars 13.2%, Yoyleland 12.3%, the Moon stage 12.2%, Fortress 10.5%, Canyon 6.6%, none on the other six; on this build 0); she blinks in on 2.4-2.8% (the real block-between cases; 4% on Pillars, up to 8% on Fortress) and blinks home on 3.5-4.0%; every plan survives its own wire code; the real sequence on 56 of them (Yoyleland, Pillars, Canyon and Fortress, 14 each: six that blink in, three that blink home, three that start off 'above', two plain, with and without a lift): eight blows of exactly 12.5% on frames 39 + 14 i, never inside a solid block (every place of the row), ends exactly where the press found her facing as she did, no lock left, no error; and the hand cases (Pillars, Bow beside each pillar's edge with 6 px to spare, the foe on the floor below or 70 px across: all eight are moving plans and none blinks; on top of a pillar with the foe under it: blink in and home, 8 x 12.5%, home to the pixel). (2) HER 'YOU' TRIANGLE, NAME TAG AND STATUS ICONS stay upright over her whichever way her picture is turned, and the name tag now shows whenever the tag rules say so (it was switched off for all of her frames): `drawFighter` turns by `FS_TURN` and un-turns those three; proof, the real `drawFighter` on a recording canvas at turns 0, 132 and 180 degrees: the body at the turn, the triangle, the tag and the icons at 0. (3) THE TELL, rebalanced inside the 14 frames: recoil 2, dash 3, chair up 6, swing 3 (was 3, 3, 4, 4: the wind-up was 4 frames, 67 ms, and is now 6, 100 ms, the chair and her arm up over her head for the whole of it), and her arm checked against the chair's path: the arm is now the rig's 'chop', up to down in her own frame, the same way as the chair (not the generic 'slam', which ended 30 degrees below horizontal and forward); the arm's swing timer is per beat and follows the chair's own direction (`fsChairArmAnim`: the first cut raised the arm in a straight line while the chair eased up and swung down quickening, so mid-swing the arm led or lagged the chair by up to 90 degrees; now the timer is worked out from the chair's angle each beat). Read off the real rig (`limbMoves` on her `LIMB_RIG` arm) with her arm and her chair in her own frame (0 toward the foe, 180 straight away from it), beats 6 to 14 of a blow (the wind-up's six, then the swing's three): the arm 107, 133, 154, 168, 177, 180, 160, 100, 0 against the chair 99, 123, 142, 155, 163, 166, 148, 92, 0 from her middle (never more than 14 degrees apart, 17 from her shoulder, counting the rig's arm swing alone; as really drawn, with the body's own lean and flounce added, up to about 59 degrees on beats 9 to 12, on a 9 px arm stub; the 'slam' of the build before was 53 degrees off the chair at the blow and had the arm straight up a beat before the chair); the arm rests on beats 1 to 5 while the chair comes back to her side. The arm is one short stub (9 px, the left of the picture, hanging from hip height) and the chair is 54 to 111 px from her middle, so the chair is pointed at, not held (see below). It is the same on the host and the guest (the in-process online pair: swing kind and length identical and the arm angle identical on 121 of 121 compared frames, ground and air, the wire plan code identical on every frame). SEEN, in plain node, with her real picture: the real `drawFighter`, `drawSpriteBody` and `drawRigged` (her sprite `assets/sprites/bow.png`, her `LIMB_RIG` arm cut out and swung by the real `limbMoves`) and the row's own draw, onto a software canvas (the page's own `ctx` forwarded to it; only the image decode is faked): both facings and all eight places at the top of the wind-up and at the blow, and the 14 beats of one blow, before and after (the first sheet's stand-in sprite had no arm, which the old contact sheet `reports/moves/bow-slam-sheet.png` still shows). The sheets and scripts are the session's, not in the repo. NOT SEEN: in a browser. Kept as disclosed by the builder: a standing Tree on Goiky Field and Pillars dies from 0% (the owner may want to know; the launch values are identical to ii-dlc, checked again on this build: the same 18 scenes as the build before it, vx, vy, stun and damage the same as ii-dlc to the digit), and the chair is not literally in her hands (about 58 px from her middle at rest and swung out to about 111 at the blow, 128 px from the foe: it reads as her telekinesis; shrinking it means shrinking the 128, which the brief gave as 120 'tune'). Moon Caves is hidden and not released by this.
- PRIZE FINISHERS (2026-11-03, branch `g-fin-prize`, built on ii-dlc `e5c52b2`, NOT PUSHED): the first two of Steve Cobs's three prize fighters get their own finishers (the owner, 2026-11-02, picked pitch 3 of the first's file and pitch 2 of the second's, `smash-bal/reports/moves/finisher-ideas-prize-A.md` and `-B.md`, "as written"; the third keeps his Big File: "a hit is included"). Each is a row in `PRIZE_KIT.ATKSPECIALS` (`spill`, `voices`) with its card line in `PRIZE_KIT.MOVE_TEXT`, and the code is one marked block (`// ---- Steve Cobs's prize: begin/end`, `PRIZE FINISHERS`, after lane c2's batch c22: `PZF`, `finCloset`, `finRace`, `pzDoorStep`, `pzRaceStep`, the drawing, `pzWant`). THE KIT STILL HIDES EVERYTHING: `installPrizeKit()` calls `pzFinInstall()` (FINX_STEP, FINX_DRAW_UNDER/OVER, FINX_LEFT, FX5_HURT, FX5_LETGO, FINX_AI_KITS, FIN_AI_EAGER) and `uninstallPrizeKit()` calls `pzFinUninstall()`; proved: 32 live tables are identical to main's with the gate shut, and again after an install and an uninstall, and a seeded 4-CPU match is identical to main's. Nothing is added to the snapshot (every field the drawing reads is on FINX_NET already) and no framework function was edited. THE FIRST, DON'T GO IN THERE! (`closet`): he stamps and holds up a palm (12 frames) while a closet door with a skull sign grows up out of the floor 76 px ahead (34 wide, 76 tall, not solid; whole at frame 12) and stands 4 s from then. The first foe whose body touches it is slid to it over 6 frames and held there 48 (the door open over them, a tangle of arms drawn over them, no faces), thumped at +12, +24, +36 for 3% each, and at +48 the door bursts and a last 3% flings them out away from him (launch 9, up 11): 12%, four hits. A second press (V + C again, which the 74-frame cooldown lets through 74 frames after the first) springs it early on the foe nearest the door within 110 px in clear sight; with nobody there it does nothing and is not spent. A hit on him while they are inside frees them at once; a hit in the stamp is no door; a KO of either end, a jail or a Final Smash's catch ends it; two hits from a foe's swings or shots break it; a boss takes a plain 6% on touching it and the door opens; a teammate walks through. It needs 34 px of level floor at 76 px and no wall or closed door in the way: from the air, over a gap or against a wall nothing comes and the press is not spent. A solid arriving over a standing or a holding door frees the foe and sinks it (checked every 4 frames). His cooldown starts when it is gone (an unused door costs about 5.7 s of finisher). THE SECOND, OFF TO THE RACES (`race`): she rears back (the throw pose, on every screen) and her lid flaps (12 frames; the flap is host screen only, like Full of Voices' lid today: its timer is not on the wire), then a red-and-white striped wall (14 thick, 60 tall) rises out of the floor 42 px ahead over 12 frames (committed 24 in all; harmless while it grows), and races along the floor the way she faces: 3 px a frame plus 0.2 each frame for 45 frames (333 px, to 11.8 a frame; it ends 375 px out), stopping short at a solid wall or where her floor ends (it never stands off the floor or inside a solid), then sinks away over 6 frames. Each foe it touches takes 12% once and is launched (7 forward, 11 up); a boss a plain 12%. Run clear early (a foe running away at full speed from the first moving frame is caught from 45 to 90 px ahead of her and escapes from 130) or jump it (clears when jumped with the wall 90 or more px from the foe). A hit on her in the 24 frames is no wall; from the air or with no floor under the start nothing comes. Her cooldown starts when the wall is gone. THE CPUS: the door's owner plants it at a grounded foe coming at him 120-230 px off on his floor with room for it (`FIN_AI_EAGER.spill` 8: the read holds about 40 frames of a 40 s match; about one door a match) and springs it when a foe is within 60 px of it; the wall's owner lets it go at a grounded foe 50-300 px ahead on her level who is not running away, with floor and no wall between (about one a match). CHANGED FROM THE PITCHES: (1) the door's second press is the ordinary finisher button, which the cooldown lets through while the door stands (the pitch named FX5_PRESS, which only reaches an owner who is locked; his is free to fight); no second door can come while one stands. (2) The press catches the foe nearest the door within 110 px (the pitch gated the press at 110 but pulled in only the foe within 48, which is a touch, so the press would add nothing); a touch is a body on the door. (3) Shots that reach the door count as hits on it and stop there, as on every prop (FX5_HURT); the pitch had them pass through. (4) The wall sinks over 6 frames at its end (gone about 75 frames from the press, the pitch 69). (5) The wall's CPU read is Firey's widened to 300 px, with not-running-away, floor and no-wall checks, the pitch's "reuse Firey's". No piece of the engine was added. KNOWN LIMIT, OWNER'S CALL: a guest who has not beaten Cobs sees neither the door nor the wall. The two draw hooks are installed with the kit (hidden = main's code), and that guest already swaps the host's prize seat for Firey and has no sprites for the fighter either; the rows still reach it and the host's hits and holds run on both screens, so the door pulls them in and the wall knocks them with nothing drawn. Options if wanted: install the draw hooks on a guest when a closet or race row arrives (the hooks are inert without rows), or leave it. PROVED (jsdom, in-process, no relay; drawing on the strict canvas, 1990 draws, no fault and no open save): both facings; every frame timing and each hit through applyHit; the dodge (door: jump with the foe 100-170 px off clears, 60-80 and 200+ is caught; step clear; wall: as above); the miss; the air and the edge and wall placements (not spent); the boss; a KO of the owner, of the foe (one stock), of the foe on the last thump at three damage-cap margins; a hit on the owner in the stamp and in the hold; a second foe; a teammate; one fall one stock; nobody inside a solid or off the floor on any frame; the pair (host and guest owners, rows equal on every frame, guest draws every frame); three CPU free-for-alls with both fighters in the room (no stuck hold or lock, no bad position).

- **2026-11-02, updated 2026-11-03, LEDGEGUARD camera (the owner: "it is hard to ledgeguard, because cpus always go so high, i would never be able to hit them."; asked what would make it better, for seeing offstage they chose "Camera zooms out offstage", "Wider default view" and "Offscreen marker for a recovering fighter"; branch `g-lg-cam`, NOT PUSHED):** local view only. The sim's camera (`camX`, `camY`, `CAM_ZOOM`, `updateCamera`, `clientCamera`) is exactly as it was, so what reads it (the single-screen respawn, a boss's falling shots, the guest's fallback follow) does not move; the picture has a camera of its own (`LGC`; the block `// LEDGEGUARD CAMERA` before `NET_VIEW`; `lgStep` is called from `updateCamera` on a host and `clientCamera` on a guest, from the fighters and the floor each already has; `drawView` lends it to `draw()` for one picture through `lgBegin` / `lgEnd`, and `viewZoom()` answers with its zoom while that picture is drawn). Nothing is added to a snapshot. It applies to normal Free-for-all, Teams, event and World Cup stages only (`lgMode`): a boss, Capture the Flag, Moon Caves, Training, the tutorial, a custom level, One, Steve Cobs's fight, RUNNING!, the plays camera, a spectated flag match and a watched cup fixture on a scrolling map keep their own cameras. (1) THE WIDER DEFAULT (`LG.wide` 1.09, `LG.below` 1.13): every view is 9% wider and taller (zoom divided by 1.09); on a single screen the extra height goes below first, so the drop under the floor line in view is 13% deeper (Goiky Field, Yoyleland and The Moon 234 px to 265 and 160 px of air either side to 224; Pillars, Forest and Incinerator 130 px to 146 and 0 to 50 either side); a scrolling arena widens about the sim's own follow centre (`camTarget` at `CAM_ZOOM`, smoothed as it is): 9% more beside and above, 14% more under the floor with you on it (324 px to 369, 14%; the Teams arena 265 px to 310, 17%). The floor and the drop are drawn as far as the picture reaches (`lgReachL`, `lgReachR`). (2) THE OFFSTAGE PULL: a fighter is recovering (`lgRaw`) when it is alive, not past saving (`lgDoomed`: in hitstun on a flight that ends past a KO line) and over the drop (`overDrop`, the test the CPU's `edgeGoal` uses), past a side of the world, or falling out of the bottom (`brainRecover`'s own `fallingOut`), for `LG.enter` 6 frames in a row; it is let go after `LG.leave` 36 frames of not being (the same 36 on the ground), at once on a KO (a stock lost, or a jump of over 320 px plus 40 for each step the call stands for: the larger of the call's own steps, which a guest's camera clamps to 6, and the host steps since the last call (`hazardT`; a guest's snapshot lands 3 on, a lag spike's worth at once; at most 30), so the allowance is 360 px on the host, 560 at 6 steps, 920 at 15 and 1,520 at 30. In the in-process online pair a 15-step lag spike (a recoverer 375 px lower) keeps its state on the guest, and so does a 900 px jump over 15 host steps, which dropped it with the steps alone). The view is the union of your fighter, each recovering fighter (70 px of margin, 10 frames of lead) and its nearest ledge with 200 px of air above it, shifted the least that holds them inside the screen less 48 px either side, 84 above (the cards) and 36 below, and pulled back only as far as that still takes (`LG.pull` 1.00; a first try at 0.90 zoomed out for every trip and breathed 30 times a minute in a CPU brawl), never past `LG.zmin` 0.50 (the nearest fighter first; one that no longer fits is left to its marker). Who is in that plan has a margin of its own so the zoom does not pump: a fighter joins only if it fits at 0.54 (`LG.zmin` + `LG.zhys` 0.04) and stays until it needs 0.46, or has not fitted 0.50 for `LG.missF` 30 frames; where you and a recoverer do not fit even then, the view is the middle of them, but never so far that you are out of the picture. FAR RECOVERERS (2026-11-03, the owner chose "Only a marker if far"): a recovering fighter more than 900 px (`LG.far`: the straight line from the centre of the fighter the view follows to its centre, in world px; a fixed distance on purpose, because the zoom is itself decided by who is in the plan and a rule tied to it would chase its own tail) is never in the plan: no zoom, no pan for it, only its marker. A recoverer is far from 960 px (`LG.far` + `LG.farH` 60) and near again under 840 px, a fresh one is far if over 900, and a near one joins the plan exactly as before. Measured in Canyon, a CPU pinned over a pit and you swaying 30 px each way for 300 steps: at 1,400 to 1,650 px away the view used to zoom to 0.55 to 0.62 and move 324 to 425 px toward it (6 zoom reversals; at 1,800 px it no longer fitted, so nothing); now the zoom, the view's centre and the pull are identical to a run with nobody offstage (difference 0, plan members 0, no visible pan) and its marker is drawn from the 6th frame; a recoverer 600 to 870 px away pulls with exactly the numbers it had. Swaying 60 px each way across the 900 px line flips nothing (7 crossings of the line, 0 flips); a sway wider than the 120 px margin flips once for each real crossing (5 and 5, 3 and 3). A fighter that goes from near to far in mid recovery is let go, and the view eases back over 40 frames from where it was (it can still zoom a few frames further toward what it last held, 0.598 to 0.522 in the worst scene, and then comes back). It eases in over 24 frames and out over 40, the zoom never changes by more than 0.02 of its log a frame, and each edge of what the recovering fighters needed is held 36 frames and then let go at 5% a frame, so a fighter bobbing in and out under the ledge moves the view once. (3) THE MARKER (`lgDrawMarkers`): a recovering fighter outside the view gets a chevron at the screen's border pointing at it, in its colour (the team colour in teams, a white rim round yours), with a stem that grows with the distance, grown in over 12 frames, at most 3 at once across chevrons and up arrows together (chevrons first, then arrows, the nearest first within each, which keeps the lead through a near tie), markers that would sit on one another slid along the border at least 26 px (`LG.gap`) apart, toward the middle of it, the nearest staying where it is, and none on the minimap, the Teams standings strip or under the cards (`lgHud` reads the cards' strip and the standings box from the page every 30 pictures; 84 px from the top, more when the cards wrap to a second row), gone 6 frames after the fighter is back over the stage and at once on a KO; no words, drawn over the world and under the HUD; it takes the place of the old ring (`drawOffscreenArrows`) for that fighter on a single-screen edge stage, which still marks everyone else off the screen. The respawn point is still `sideRoomTop() - 40` (a sim constant), so a fighter coming back now shows 9 px from the top of the screen on the edge stages (it was 31 px off it). (4) THE UP ARROW (2026-11-03, the owner chose "Yes, an up arrow at the top edge"): a live fighter (`lgAlive`: in the match, not held or carried, not past saving, not a fighter just back from a KO under its respawn ceiling) whose whole body is above the view and whose x is inside it gets a small up arrow at the top edge (the cards' strip's lower edge, 84 px, more when the cards wrap) at its x, in its colour (the team colour in teams, a white rim round yours), no words, grown in over 12 frames (the same `LG.growF`), and gone the moment its centre is back in the view or it is out; no zoom or pan for it. It is drawn by the chevrons' code and shares their cap of 3 (chevrons first, then arrows nearest first), their 26 px spacing and their clearance of the minimap and the standings strip, but it only slides along the top edge, never round a corner, and its stem shows how far above the top the fighter is. A chevron's fighter that is also above the view keeps its chevron (one age for both kinds, so a chevron that becomes an arrow does not grow in again). Measured on Goiky Field: the plain view's top edge is 748 px above the floor and the top KO line 850, so a live fighter is above the view for 770 to 850 px above the floor: at 780, 800, 820, 840 and 850 px an arrow at the fighter's screen x (336.8 for 336.8) in its colour, pointing straight up; at 700, 740 and 760 px it is still in the view (none); at 860 px and over it is KO'd (none; with stocks left it is held by the respawn ceiling and gets none); in hitstun on a flight that ends past the KO line (`lgDoomed`) none, in hitstun on one that does not an arrow. Grown in 0.02 to 1.00 over 12 pictures; gone when its centre is back in the view (screen y 1.1); yours has the white rim; three arrows 6 px apart in the world are 30 px apart on the screen; four candidates give 3 (the farthest from the middle left out); with two chevrons (pit recoverers far from you) and two high fighters in Canyon the three are the two chevrons and the nearest arrow. In Canyon Teams an arrow above the top right slid left along the top to x 894, clear of the minimap (x from 912), in the team colour; with a two-row cards strip it sits at 136 px. A view that throws once switches itself off for that match (`LGC.off`, one `console.error`) and is tried again with the next one (`setupWorld`).

- **2026-11-03, PENCIL VAN DRIVES (the owner: "the supervan does not drive"; asked what they meant: "make the van drive across the stage"; branch `g-pencil-drive`):** `FINAL_SMASHES['Pencil']` (id `twofour`, name kept) no longer blasts from a standing van. The entrance is as it was (the van grows in over 12 frames, she hops in over 18, the exhaust puffs and the van shakes up to row frame 48, the catch lands on 48), and then the van DRIVES: it sets off along the floor the way she faces, speeding up over 36 frames (distance goes with the square of the time: 1 px a frame at the start, 23 at the end), up to 420 px (416 on a flat floor: steps of 8), wheels turning (spokes over the two hubs, turned by the road covered), exhaust streaming behind it (a puff every 2 frames, left where it was made), dust puffs at the back wheel, a small bounce. Every foe it overruns takes one heavy hit, once (raw 8 = 16% on screen after `FS_DMG_MULT`, launched 13 forward and 14 up); the blast goes off at the END of the road, centred on the van, on row frame 84 (was 62), and throws her out of the van as before (the arc now starts from her seat at the end of the road). The van blows apart over 12 frames (was 10). **Damage split** (the budget is the old one: a foe in the old inner ring took 40%, one in the outer ring 28%): run hit 16% + inner ring 24% (raw 12, was 20) = 40% for a foe the van ran down and the blast then reached; the outer ring is 24% (raw 12, was 14: 28%), so run + outer is also 40%; a foe only the blast reached takes 24% (was 40% inner, 28% outer); a boss takes 8 + 12 = 20 raw, the same 20 as before. With no road (below) there is no run hit and the old blast is whole (raw 20 and 14). The orange floor is untouched (`fsfNote`, every hit passes `applyHit`). **KO from 0%** (a CPU foe steering back, Goiky Field, first starting % at which it loses a stock, steps of 10; before -> after): foe 150 px ahead, Puffball (46) 10 -> 0, Firey (74) 0 -> 40, Tree (138) 40 -> 50; foe 350 px ahead, Puffball 10 -> 0, Firey 30 -> 10, Tree 60 -> 40; the same both facings. **The road** (`fsPencilDrive`, fixed when the row starts): the front wheel (60 px ahead of the van's middle) must stay over the floor or the platform she stood on (never a ledge or a gap), the nose stays 70 px inside the stage walls and short of any solid block as tall as the van; under 24 px, in the air, and in Moon Caves the road is 0: the van stays put and the old blast plays (the blast kept whole, as above). In Moon Caves the van does not drive: its floor is the frame's own curve, which a guest cannot work out from three numbers. **The catch** (`catchOk`, a row option that was there): the 300 px circle is narrowed to the foes in the van's way (ahead of her or just under the van, no further than the road plus a van's length, low enough to be met), so nobody is held for nothing; whoever it took that neither the van nor the blast reached is let go, unhurt, on row frame 99 (`w + 15`). A CPU fires it only when that catch will take someone (`exact: true`; the old row also fired after 150 frames on any foe within 420). Pencil's Final Smash never had a fizzle (no `needFoe`); a human's press with nobody near still fires, as before. **The wire:** one new fighter field, `fsPd` (the road in px, 0 when the van stays; whole numbers; cleared by the row's end, her KO, the match start and the dead path), in `NET_FIGHTER_FLAGS`; the van's place, the dust and the exhaust are pure functions of `fsT`, her wire place and facing, `fsAx`/`fsAy` and `fsPd` (`fsPencilVanX`); nothing new in `fsBx`/`fsBy`/`fsCx`/`fsCy` (her seat, as before). **Checks** (jsdom, throwaway, scratchpad `pdrive/`): the sequence both facings from the real fire (the road 416 either way; the van's x on frames 48..84 = 0, 8, 26, 54, 93, 142, 201, 270, 350, 416 at 52..83 by 4; foes locked on 48, hit by the run on 61 and 74 as the nose reached them, by the blast on 84, 16% + 24% each); a cast from the air (no road, the old blast whole, she falls to the floor after it as she always did); a ledge 290 px ahead with a foe at its edge (road 224, front wheel on the floor); the floor ending behind her, and 40 px ahead (no road: the old blast); a solid block 300 px ahead (road 216, nose 291; the foe before it hit by the run, the one past it by the blast only); a teammate in the way (untouched, not held); a boss (8 then 12, total 20 as before; one at 620 px: the blast only, 12); interrupts: her KO on row frames 30, 60 and 90 (exactly one stock, no lock, no row, `fsPd` 0), a held foe's KO, a free foe's (clean); the in-process online pair (host and guest drew the van with identical arguments on 94 of 94 shared row frames; the road 416 on both); Moon Caves (the row runs, no road, the old blast's 20 raw = 40% hits a foe beside her; it ends as it ever did there, she comes down where the old row left her); a CPU Pencil fires with the foe ahead and turns before firing when it is behind; three CPU free-for-alls (`merged_ffa3.mjs`) and three more with Pencil in them (18 finishers run, errors none, nothing stuck); - **2026-11-03, BOW TELEPORT KEEPS SPEED (the owner: "is this a bug: when I use teleport, it cancels my momentum, allowing me to carry stocks for very long."; he chose exactly two changes; branch `g-bow-tp`, NOT PUSHED):** It was not a bug but it was a loophole: the Teleport zeroed her speed at both ends (`startTeleport`, `teleportStep`, `teleportArrive`), held her still for `TP.hang` (6 frames) where she appeared, made her untouchable while she faded, and gave itself back when she was HIT (`_tpUsed` reset on `hitstun`), so a launched Bow could teleport, stop dead, be hit again and teleport again. CHANGE 1, SHE KEEPS HER SPEED: if she was hit this airtime (`_tpLaunch`, set while `hitstun > 0` and cleared on a floor, on a KO and on a new stock) and moves `TP.keep` = 2.5 px a frame or more on the press, that speed (vx, vy, read on the press frame after that frame's air control) is remembered on the `_tp` object and given back the frame she appears, and the 6-frame hang is skipped for it (the simplest way to really keep it: the hang would otherwise hold her still with no gravity). The bar is low on purpose: hits here are slow (a side launch is 4-8 px a frame and nothing slows it in the air). A jump, a drift or a use on the stage (no hit this airtime) is exactly as before: zeroed, then the hang. Distance (240), the fade (8 frames), invulnerability while fading, CTF/carrier/copy scaling, the pop damage and the CPU's reads are untouched. CHANGE 2, A HIT NO LONGER GIVES IT BACK: the `_tpUsed` reset is now on landing only (and still on a KO and a new stock; the cancel in `teleportStep`, where a freeze or sneeze ended the fade before she went anywhere, still refunds it). Nothing was added to the wire: the host does the move, a guest sees her position (`x`, `y`, `vx`, `vy`, `_tpUsed`, `_tp`, `_tpHang` are already in the snapshot). Text: Bow's move card (Teleport) and kit line say she keeps her speed after a hit and gets the move back only on landing; one line on the What's new card. PROOF (jsdom, Goiky Field: floor ends at x 922, world 1024, right KO line 1304 = 382 past the ledge; Bow hit off the right ledge, `applyHit` with her at 100% and 150%, free after 26 frames of hitstun at x 1008-1072 moving 4.4-7 px a frame; she presses Teleport the first free frame; scripts in the session scratchpad `bowtp/`, before = ii-dlc): TOWARD THE STAGE (left): before, she appears 240 px back at speed 0 and lives, on the floor, every time (A: 100%, vx 7: appears at x 832, speed 0, lives; B: 150%, vx 7: 838, lives; D: 100%, vx 4.4: 768, lives); after, she appears at the same spot (839, 844, 772) with her speed (6.1, 6.1, 3.4 px a frame away from the stage after that frame's left key) and, if the player lets go, drifts off the ledge and is KO'd on frame 76, 75 (A, B) or lives (D, the slow one: 3.4 px a frame is slowed to a stop and lands); if the player steers back (holds left while she is moving right) she lives in all three, so the move is still a recovery, no longer a free stop. UP / NO AIM (she is past the world's edge, so the trip is brought just inside, x about 1000): before she appears at speed 0 and is KO'd falling on frame 93-94; after she keeps (7, 2.1) and is carried to the side KO line, 381 px past the ledge, KO'd on frame 81-85. (Up + C is Chair Slam, so 'up' is the neutral aim.) GENTLE USES unchanged: drift of 2 px a frame, no hit (appears 240 px away at speed 0, lives, same as before), standing on the stage (no change), a jump's rise with no hit (no change). A HIT AFTER TELEPORTING: hit in the air after appearing, 30 frames later (cooldown cleared): before, the move was back (a second press started a fade); after it is still spent (the press only puffs) and returns when she lands (36 frames later); a freeze that cancels the fade still refunds it; a KO clears `_tpUsed` and `_tpLaunch`. CPU: Bow in three Expert CPU free-for-alls (Bow/Toothpaste/Firey/Bomby, Bow/Pen/Lifering/Tea Kettle, Bow/Leafy/Rocky/Needle, 2,400 frames each): errors none, bad positions 0; she teleported 4, 2 and 1 times (before 1, 2, 1) and 5 of the 7 kept a launch; she still lives in the same range of stocks. ONLINE (in-process host + guest, no relay): Bow hit out, teleports, same path on both screens: the guest equals the host 3 frames earlier to within 0.5 px over all 134 frames, the teleport included, final position the same. Syntax, boot (ffa and teams, bad positions 0, errors none), CRLF intact. BALANCE NOTE: this nerfs a defensive recovery the owner called too strong; the change is mild where the launch is slow (a light hit she can steer out of) and sharp where it is big (a launch toward the blast zone now still kills her unless she steers back at once, and cannot be chained by hits). Her Final Smash, Chair Slam, Snowballs and the CPU's logic were not touched; the CPU still prefers the Teleport as before and its use after a hit now also costs it the move until it lands. Not tuned further: `TP.keep` (2.5) is the one number if the owner wants more or less.
- **2026-11-04, SONIC BLAST P3 (the owner: "phase 3 sonic blast is undodgeable"):** Measured on Puffball Speaker Box: the wind-up from the first tell to the first ring was 35 frames in every phase. When the platform is the gap (every second blast; the lanes at the floor and one above it are filled, the two lanes at platform height are empty), a fighter in the middle of the floor needs about 40 frames to walk and jump onto a top platform (a held jump takes 33 frames and peaks only a few pixels under the next ring lane), and phase 3's rings move at 13 and 8.4 px a frame, so a late start was hit: a test fighter reacting after 8 frames made it from every start, one reacting after 18 frames was hit in 2 of 6. Fix: phase 3's SONIC BLAST! now winds up for 58 frames (PSB.soundwave3 = 72 at the 0.8 pace), 23 more; phases 1 and 2 keep 35. Second change, phase 3 only: when the platform is the gap, the lane above the gap is empty too (lanes 2, 3 and 4 of 7), because a held jump onto a top platform peaks only about 3 pixels under that lane's ring and a test fighter was clipped there. The speeds, the four waves and every other lane are untouched, so phase 3 is still the hardest (7 lanes, 13 px rings, four waves).
- **2026-11-04, JURY 1 FIXES (the owner: "the game needs to feel better"; ten reviewer personalities played and read the game, their findings merged, ranked and the small safe ones fixed; nothing here changes damage, knockback or stats):** (1) NAME TAGS: with up to 6 fighters everyone's name rides over them (it was 4, so the default 5-fighter match showed only your own). (2) THE KO SAYS WHO: your KO banner reads "KO'd by Leafy − a stock" (or "You're out! KO'd by Leafy") when someone hit you in the last 10 seconds; with nobody it is the old text (online guests still get the old text: the guest does not know who). (3) A KO IS LOUDER: the KO star's rings are about twice the size and last 14 frames (were 8); the screen shake on a KO is at the cap (12) for your own KO or one you scored and half that for others (it was 3.6, less than a plain 10% hit). (4) INPUT BUFFER (people only, not CPUs): a jump pressed in the air with no jump left is played if you land within 6 frames, and X pressed while the last move's lag or cooldown was still running is played the moment it ends if that is within 8 frames (before, both were thrown away; smash already worked this way). (5) GUEST PREDICTION: an action key already held when the host said "free" no longer stops the guest's own fighter dead (it was drawn 57 px behind where it should be, now it is predicted); only a new press stops the prediction, as before. (6) GUEST SLIDE-BACK runs on real time, not once per drawn frame (a hit's correction was a snap at 144 Hz and a drag at 30). (7) DAMAGE NUMBER BUMPS: the % on a fighter's card pops up (1.5x for a fifth of a second) each time it goes up (off with reduced-motion). (8) SCREEN SHAKE: one random offset per 60th of a second instead of a new one every drawn frame (it read as static on fast screens). (9) HIT SOUND: a little pitch variety (about 7% up or down) so a string of jabs is not one note. (10) THE CROWD on the floor line is fainter (0.28, was 0.5) so it is not read as items. NOT FIXED, FOR THE OWNER (balance or a decision): the Final Smash catch (10 frames after the freeze, circles of 230 to 400 px, no dodge: raise FS_CATCH to about 22 and cap reach at about 300); a launch's speed is cut to the walking cap in one frame when the stun ends (ease it down instead: kills come earlier); no steering during stun; grace shorter than the stun for big hits (3 to 4 hittable frames); jabs that KO from 90 to 130% (Firey, Cake, Magnet, Match, Pencil); flat 20-frame stun for every hit; hit freeze stops growing at 18%; the standing jab waits 7 frames for a grab (CI_GRAB_DELAY, your own request); ground turn-around and air drag; guests' boss-shot windows lose about 8 frames to the network (add the guest's lag to boss warnings); NET_PRED.max 30 against 12 to 15; two CPUs standing nose to nose for 10 s on Easy and Normal; CPUs stepping off the stage with a carrying move. NOT DONE: the Pencil Final Smash camera (four reviewers, but only seen in all-CPU captures where the camera follows the first fighter, so the cause is not proven), the ledge-catch ease, the Up+X hop, the first-run tutorial page, results-screen layout. Checked: syntax, boot (FFA and Teams, no errors), three Expert CPU free-for-alls, and short scenes (before and after numbers in the reply).

- **2026-11-04, NO FINISHER IN THE FIRST FRAMES AFTER HITSTUN (the owner, about Toothpaste's Squeeze Rocket: "he can charge it during hitstun and release within a frame, with easily done inhuman timings"; he chose 'No finisher in the first frames after hitstun', for everyone):** a finisher (V + C) can no longer start until FIN_HS_DELAY = 8 frames after the fighter's hitstun has ended (a per-fighter counter `_hsAgo`, reset every frame of hitstun; the CPU's finisher input goes through the same gate). Measured with scripted presses after a 6-frame hitstun on Toothpaste and on Bow: a press on the first frame or 3 frames after hitstun is blocked, a press about 7 frames after starts the finisher (the same for both: no per-fighter exception). Nothing else changed: the finisher's own numbers, the Squeeze Rocket's continuous push and its turn are as before (the owner did not choose the single-motion dash). NOT PUSHED (held for Update 2).

- **2026-11-04, JURY 1 DECISIONS (the owner's four answers after game-feel jury 1):** (1) FINAL SMASH CATCH, 'longer, smaller window': a Final Smash's catch now lands 22 frames after the freeze ends (FS_CATCH 10 -> 22, about 0.37 s; it was a sixth of a second, shorter than a human's reaction) and the catch area is capped at 300 px (FS_CATCH_CAP, fsCapSh): a circle's radius is cut to 300, a box is cut to 300 px either side of the holder (its height is kept). Only the catch is cut: each row's blast, props and hits keep their own sizes, and a foe who is caught is held as before. The cap is applied where the area is made (fsCatchOf), where a row lays it late (fsCatch) and where it is drawn (hitArt), so the CPU's 'will it land?' check, the needFoe fizzle and the drawn area all use the capped area. Reach before/after: 76 of the 109 rows had a catch wider than 300 and are now cut to 300 (circles 310-500 -> 300; boxes up to 1444 wide -> at most 600 wide, centred, or 300 ahead); the other 33 rows, at or under 300, are unchanged (Bubble 230, Pencil 300 and so on). Catch frame before/after: a row that catches at its start: freeze + 10 -> freeze + 22; Pencil, which lays her catch when her van sets off (catchAt 48): 58 -> 70 frames after the freeze; the rows with an entrance (batches A and B, the redesigns) lay theirs late inside their own entrance, so they move by the same 12 frames. Every row starts after its catch lands, so no row's blast comes before its catch (the entrances are timed from the row's start, which moved with the catch). Measured with the 109-row smoke script and the KO-at-0% script (foe 150 px away, so inside both areas): no row errors, every row deals and KOs exactly as before (the KO-at-0% table is identical line for line), and each row plays 12 frames later (every row that starts, 108 of 109); the same 6 rows flagged before the change are flagged after it, identically (Bow never starts in the script, it needs a foe below her; Saw and Goo no damage; Fries, Pillow and Marshmallow freeze/slow) -- not caused by this. (2) ACCESSIBILITY: (a) the key remaps are saved on this device ('bfsi:keymap' in localStorage, every read and write in try/catch, a stored map that binds one key twice is ignored) and Reset defaults clears them; Escape and R are refused for every action (Pause may be put back on Escape), and a key another action already uses is swapped with that action unless the swap would give it a reserved key, with a short note under the list saying what happened (keyBindCheck). (b) Colour-blind-safe teams: in Teams and Capture the Flag every fighter has a shape marker under its team ring, always on, no words: team 1 a circle, team 2 a triangle, team 3 a square, team 4 a diamond, in the team colour with a dark and a white outline (drawTeamMark). (3) QUIT CONFIRM, 'only when it matters': Quit to title in the pause menu asks 'Keep playing / Quit' (in the pause card, in its own style) only for a Boss Rush run, the Daily and an online match; a practice match, a test, a tutorial or a custom match quits at once. Pausing or resuming closes the question. (4) JAB KILLS (balance): the plain jabs of Firey, Cake, Magnet, Match and Pencil got a launch trim (JAB_TRIM, the plain swing only: the empowered jab keeps its launch, damage and reach are untouched): Firey x0.7, Cake x0.7, Match x0.7, Magnet x0.5, Pencil x0.45. A jab's launch moves the KO percent only a little (the launch grows mostly with the foe's percent), so the trims are bigger than they sound. Measured with a mid-weight foe (Pen) by a scripted scene, lowest KO percent (within 3%), centre stage before -> after / the edge before -> after (a CPU foe who recovers): Firey 97 -> 107 / 137 -> 174, Cake 102 -> 116 / 153 -> 193, Magnet 69 -> 107 / 94 -> 158, Match 92 -> 107 / 130 -> 167, Pencil 62 -> 104 / 78 -> 155. So none KOs from centre stage under 100% on a mid-weight foe any more, and all five still KO a very high percent foe at the edge. Not done: the shape markers are on the fighters only (the off-screen arrows and the score cards still show colour alone). Checked: syntax, boot (FFA and Teams, no errors), three Expert CPU free-for-alls, CRLF.

- **2026-11-04, JURY 2B (three owner decisions after the second jury; nothing changes damage, knockback or stats):** (1) The faint round crowd faces along the stage floor no longer draw in Boss Rush, in Teams, in Capture the Flag, or while any Final Smash scene is playing; ordinary free-for-all stages keep them. (2) Player name tags are moved back inside the screen when they would be cut off at the left, right, top or bottom edge, and the tags still lift off each other when they overlap (the icons above a tag move with it). - **2026-11-04, JURY 2C (the open items left by the second jury; art and camera only, nothing changes damage, knockback, timing, hitboxes or stats; no picture was downloaded; none of it was looked at in a real browser, only scripted):** (1) PENCIL'S FINAL SMASH CAMERA: while any Final Smash plays, the recovery camera no longer swings the view up to chase a fighter standing off the stage or in a respawn bubble (in a scripted run the view centre went from 373 to 247 and the holder and van sank to the bottom edge; now it stays at 349 the whole way); a fighter near a KO line still gets the KO view. (2) GELATIN'S STEAKHOUSE: the checkered floor of the dining room is drawn only over the floor he stands on, so no tile floats over the water. (3) GRAND PLAINS: the mint rectangle in the sky was the real safe respawn island, drawn as a flat box; it now wears the stage's grass-top platform art with a thin mint strip under it. (4) LOCKED CARDS on the select grid have a darker tint, an inner edge and a small padlock, and a card whose portrait is still loading shows its fighter's colour as a soft dot. (5) DARK SKINS (Bandaged Bomby, Robot Pin, Scorched Baseball, Lead Snowball, Hooded Fern) are flagged dark and get a thin light halo at match time; room 1.28 for Robot Pin and Bandaged Bomby and 1.3 for Backpack Firey (the cap works out the same picture size for these three, so that part is a no-op today). (6) WINNER SCREEN: a pale glow behind the winners. (7) TEAM RING: a dark under-stroke, and a small team-coloured pip over the head when there are more than six fighters. (8) GOIKY FIELD: a 30 px green-to-water blend and a thin dark waterline at the top of the water. (9) STORE: out of October a greyed line says 'Halloween costumes return in October'. Skipped as asked: new sprites, Blueberry and Cake Final Smash props, name banners.
- **2026-11-04, FS MENU LINES (the owner picked three places in a box):** each fighter's Final Smash now shows in the menus as one extra row, 'Final Smash', with its name and one plain line of what it does: in the fighter card's Kit tab (the name, with the line under it, like the V row), in the card's All moves tab, and in the pause menu's Move list. All 109 lines were written by hand from the designs and the code, in one table (FS_BLURBS, next to the move list code) so they are easy to edit; none is over 15 words. A fighter with no row of their own shows the default Surge. A hidden prize fighter shows no Final Smash until they are in the roster, the same as the rest of their kit. Menu text only: nothing changes damage, timing or stats. Checked in a scripted page (no real render was looked at): the three menus built for several fighters, every roster fighter has a name and a line.

- **2026-11-04, FS POLISH 1 (the owner: "do the final smashes for gelatin and blueberry", "fix known problems", and the jury's note that Blueberry's and Cake's Final Smashes read like small floor-level props next to Pillow's and Gelatin's; picture layer only, nothing changes damage, knockback, timing, hitboxes or the catch (FS_CATCH 22 frames, FS_CATCH_CAP 300 px); not looked at in a real browser, only scripted):** (1) BLUEBERRY (THE ROYAL PROCESSION) and CAKE (THE CEREMONY) each get a showpiece layer, one table `FSP` and the functions `fsPolishScreen`, `fsPolishFocus`, `fsPolishBanner` beside `fsDrawScreen`: a name banner for the first 60 frames after the press (the freeze, the catch, then 8 row frames; a dark band in the upper third of the screen with the holder's name over it and the move's name large, sliding in and fading out); the rest of the screen dims round a clear spot on the holder and the foe held (Blueberry row frames 76-183, Cake 27-114); a ring runs out from the held foe on each blow; a 3-frame white flash on the big beats (Blueberry's drop 130 and blaze 180; Cake's pie 70, Grand Cake 96 and the launch 106); and a short camera push-in to 1.25 on the pair (Blueberry 100-186, Cake 22-112), only after the catch has landed. All of it is read from the wire fields (fsId, fsC, fsT, fsLk, FS.freeze), so a guest draws the same. (2) GELATIN: the dining room's tables and chair are drawn only where there is floor under them (the checkered tile was fixed in 2.1, but a table could still hang over the water at the cliff edge); the far doors are still always drawn (the foe is thrown out through them). (3) The other known items on these two rows: the nameplates cut by the props (done in jury 2, nameplate pass), the floating crowd faces (done in jury 2B). NOT fixed: the dining room's wallpaper and windows still run past the cliff (it is a cutaway set, and cutting it needs a look at a picture first); the set still covers the windmill behind it (a style choice); Blueberry's and Cake's props are still the drawn ones (new art, feature freeze). MEASURED (scripted, a light and a heavy foe 150 px ahead on Goiky Field, before and after the change): the catch lands on the same frame (51), every change of the foe's percent lands on the same frame with the same value, the peak and the KO frame are the same (Blueberry 101 / KO 306 against a light foe, Cake 100 / 240, Gelatin 100 / 294; heavy foe: Blueberry 102.3 / 292, Cake 100.8 / 227, Gelatin 100.8 / 286); full draw() runs at the middle and both ends of the floor with no error; after the end the camera focus, the freeze, the slow-mo and fsHides are back to normal and no banner is left (the banner is a function of the row clock).
- **2026-11-04, FIX EM (the owner: "fix em"; the leftover looks after the third jury; art only, nothing changes damage, knockback, timing, hitboxes, the 22-frame catch window or the 300 px reach cap):** (1) Gelatin's Final Smash set: the back wall, wallpaper, windows, sign and lamps are now cut off at the edge of the floor under it (a window or lamp that would not fit whole on the floor is left out, like the tables), so the room no longer runs out over the water or covers the windmill behind it; the far doors stay (the foe is thrown out through them). (2) Dark skins (Bandaged Bomby, Robot Pin, Scorched Baseball, Lead Snowball, Hooded Fern): the white halo is now a wide soft glow with a tight one inside it (three passes while the fighter is fully solid, one while fading), clearly visible round Scorched Baseball; light skins are untouched. (3) Team pip: with a name tag the pip sits to the left of the tag, and where no tag is shown it sits a little higher over the head; it no longer touches the tag. (4) Final Smash dim: the screen-dim overlays reach 40 px past every screen edge (a screen shake slid the picture and left a bright sliver at the left edge), and the four soft fades of the edge-dark effect reach 60 px outside the view. (5) The faint crowd faces on the floor line of normal stages stay (the owner's decision) but are smaller (11 to 9), fainter (0.28 to 0.18) and 3 px lower. Pictures before and after: reports/jury/frames4. Checks: syntax check failed 0, no control bytes, CRLF intact, boot check clean, the merged Free-for-all check clean.

- **2026-11-04, DASH ATTACK TELLS (the owner: dash attacks "are very annoying, with no tell. I can think that the cpu is just using a lunge smash, but NOPE, it is an undodgeable (or nearly) dash"; and that Life Ring's "is more fair... I have time to think - oh wait, that isnt coming out, better dodge!"; branch `g-dashtell`):** Measured first. Every special that dashes at a foe fired on the press frame: the dash began at once, 12 to 22 px a frame, so a foe 160 px away was hit 5 to 10 frames after the first sign (Bomb's Collision 7), faster than anyone can react, and nothing a foe did (jump, run away) changed it. Woody's Fraidy Dash, written as a panic dash backwards, in fact dashes FORWARD at 22 px a frame, untouchable, for 12% (the dash code overrides the backwards push), so it was one of them too. Life Ring's whistle (10 frames of wind-up, then a 16 px dash) was the one that already worked: first hit 16 frames after the first sign at 160 px and 18 at 200 px, and a foe who reacts after 12 frames can jump it from 200 px. FIX: one shared helper, `dashTell`, gives every neutral or down special that dashes the same STARTUP Life Ring has (`startWindup`, which the game already draws: the body gathers and glows in its colour), 16 frames long, with a rising whistle, a puff of dust at the feet at the start and more dust every third frame while it gathers. The dash then starts exactly as before: damage, knockback, speed, distance and cooldowns are untouched. A hit on the fighter during the wind-up cancels the dash (as with any startup), so a good player can also stop it by hitting first. Smash dashes (Coiny, Lightning, Leafy, Yin-Yang, Knife, Book, Basketball) already have a 24-frame visible charge before the dash (first sign to first hit 27 to 32 frames at 150 px; the CPU charges them the same way), so they are unchanged. The CPU needs nothing extra: its press is now the wind-up, so it never gets a free instant dash. Covered: Leafy Sprint, Snowball Rage Tackle, Bomb Collision, Balloon Quick Air Release, Pickle dive, Fan charge, Liy Flip the Switch, MeTag cuff, Woody Fraidy Dash (specials), Teddy Bear Front of the Crowd and Bot Gotcha (down specials). Life Ring's own is the benchmark and is not changed. First sign to first hit, foe 160 px away / 200 px away, before then after (frames); wind-up before then after; the foe reacting 12 frames after the press and jumping or running away:

| Dash | Wind-up | First hit at 160 px | First hit at 200 px | Dodgeable with a 12-frame reaction |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Bomb Collision | 0 then 16 | 7 then 22 | 10 then 25 | no then yes (jump or run) |
| Snowball Rage Tackle | 0 then 16 | 8 then 23 | 11 then 26 | no then yes |
| Leafy BFDIA Sprint | 0 then 16 | 6 then 21 | 8 then 23 | no then yes (run: hit at 160 only) |
| Teddy Bear Front of the Crowd | 0 then 16 | 8 then 23 | out of range | no then yes |
| Balloon Quick Air Release | 0 then 16 | 6 then 21 | 8 then 23 | no then yes (run: hit at 160 only) |
| Pickle dive | 0 then 16 | 10 then 25 | 13 then 28 | no then running away works; a jump still gets hit at 160 and 200 px (it dives) |
| Liy Flip the Switch | 0 then 16 | 5 then 20 | 7 then 22 | no then yes (run: hit at 160 only) |
| Fan charge | 0 then 16 | 7 then 22 | 10 then 25 | no then yes |
| MeTag cuff | 0 then 16 | 7 then 22 | out of range | no then yes |
| Bot Gotcha | 0 then 16 | 9 then 24 | 13 then 28 | no then yes |
| Woody Fraidy Dash | 0 then 16 | 5 then 20 | 7 then 22 | no then yes (all three escapes at 160 px still hit) |
| Life Ring (benchmark, unchanged) | 10 | 16 | 18 | jump or run: hit at 160 only, jump at 200 dodges |

Every dash now reaches the Life Ring window or more at both distances, and a foe beyond the dash's own length (about 190 to 200 px, Leafy's 370) is safe. Bomb Collision in 20 CPU 1v1s against 20 different opponents (same seeds): before, 157 dashes, 29 KOs; after, 207 wind-ups, of which 58 were cancelled by a hit and 149 became dashes, 24 KOs (16 per 100 dashes, 12 per 100 presses; 19 per 100 dashes before); the dash-and-blast hits fell from 256 to 141; Bomb won 17 of the 20 (it won all 20 before). Not seen: nothing was rendered, so the glow and dust were checked as flags and frame counts, not by eye. QUESTION for the owner: Woody's Fraidy Dash was written as a backwards panic dash but runs forwards; it is left as it behaves (forward, now with a wind-up), say if it should really run backwards.
